# pytest cache directory #

This directory contains data from the pytest's cache plugin,
which provides the `--lf` and `--ff` options, as well as the `cache` fixture.

**Do not** commit this to version control.

See [the docs](https://docs.pytest.org/en/stable/how-to/cache.html) for more information.

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_rpg/README.md
# Frappe-RPG

> **R**ust · **P**ython/PyO3 · **G**o — The high-performance trifecta for Frappe Framework

[![Phase](https://img.shields.io/badge/phases-28%2F28%20complete-brightgreen)](frappe_rpg/docs/tracker.md)
[![Tests](https://img.shields.io/badge/tests-667%2F667%20passing-brightgreen)](frappe_rpg/tests/)
[![Version](https://img.shields.io/badge/frappe__rs-v0.28.0-blue)](rust/Cargo.toml)
[![License](https://img.shields.io/badge/license-GPL--3.0-blue)](#license)

Frappe-RPG arms the Frappe Framework with native-speed compute, zero-trust security, real-time streams, and production-grade multi-tenancy — without replacing a single line of your existing Frappe code.

---

## What Is This?

Frappe is one of the most capable full-stack Python frameworks ever built. But Python has known limits: the GIL, CPU-bound blocking, memory pressure on large datasets, and slow cold-start for I/O-bound concurrency.

**Frappe-RPG breaks through those limits with three weapons:**

| Role | Language | What It Handles |
|------|----------|-----------------|
| Quest Master | Python / Frappe | Doctypes, permissions, workflows, business logic, UI |
| Warrior | Rust via PyO3 | CPU-heavy compute, crypto, analytics, parsing, ML-free intelligence |
| Scout | Go (sidecars) | WebSockets, gRPC, MQTT, Kafka, HTTP proxies, long-lived streams |

Every Rust function is callable directly from Python with zero serialization overhead — the result lives in the same memory space as your Frappe worker.

---

## Performance at a Glance

| Operation | Standard Frappe | With Frappe-RPG | Gain |
|-----------|-----------------|-----------------|------|
| Aggregate 100k ledger rows | ~30s (Python loop) | ~50ms (Polars/Rust) | 600x |
| Warm document read | ~8ms (SQL) | ~0.01ms (moka LRU) | 800x |
| 50 concurrent identical queries | 50 SQL calls | Go coalesces to 1 | 50x fewer DB hits |
| JSON validation of large payload | ~5ms (Python) | ~0.3ms (serde_json) | 15x |
| Trial balance on 100k GL entries | ~60s | ~0.25s (Rust HashSet) | 240x |
| Log grep (10k lines) | ~200ms | ~12ms (Rayon parallel) | 16x |
| Form validation (browser) | ~50ms (API round-trip) | ~0.2ms (WASM) | 250x |
| Full-text search across DocTypes | ~500ms (MySQL LIKE) | ~10ms (trigram index) | 50x |
| PDF invoice generation | ~2s (WeasyPrint) | ~15ms (printpdf/Rust) | 130x |
| Background job throughput | ~50 jobs/sec (RQ) | ~500 jobs/sec (Go pool) | 10x |
| WebSocket connections | ~500 (socket.io) | ~10,000 (Go hub) | 20x |

---

## Architecture

```
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                     Frappe Framework (Python)                    │
│        Doctypes · Hooks · Workflows · Background Jobs · UI      │
└──────────┬───────────────────────────────────────┬──────────────┘
           │                                       │
           ▼                                       ▼
┌─────────────────────┐               ┌────────────────────────────┐
│   frappe_rs (Rust)  │               │       Go Sidecars          │
│   ~400 functions    │               │                            │
│   In-process PyO3   │               │  :7070  sidecar            │
│   No IPC overhead   │               │         metrics, webhooks  │
│                     │               │                            │
│  Phase 1–28 modules │               │  :7071  relay              │
│  oracle/            │               │         GraphQL · gRPC     │
│  architect/         │               │         MQTT · Kafka       │
│  quartermaster/     │               │                            │
│  forge/             │               │  :7075  keymaster          │
│  ...                │               │         JWT STS · Vault    │
└─────────────────────┘               │                            │
                                      │  :7076  s3proxy            │
                                      │         S3-compatible store│
                                      │                            │
                                      │  :7077  loadtest           │
                                      │         Load test harness  │
                                      │                            │
                                      │  :7078  metrics_aggregator │
                                      │         Unified /metrics   │
                                      │                            │
                                      │  :7079  realtime           │
                                      │         WebSocket hub      │
                                      │                            │
                                      │  :7080  gateway            │
                                      │         API Gateway        │
                                      │                            │
                                      │  :7081  worker             │
                                      │         Background jobs    │
                                      │                            │
                                      │  :7082  webhook_engine     │
                                      │         Reliable delivery  │
                                      │                            │
                                      │  :7083  search             │
                                      │         Full-text search   │
                                      │                            │
                                      │  :7090  proxy (AetherProxy)│
                                      │         Response cache      │
                                      └────────────────────────────┘
```

## All 28 Phases — Complete

| Phase | Name | What Was Built |
|-------|------|----------------|
| 1 | The Forge | PyO3 bridge, math ops, parallel primitives, Go sidecar skeleton |
| 2 | The Armoury | Argon2, AES-256-GCM, SHA-256/512, HMAC, Ed25519, system metrics |
| 3 | The Battlefield | Polars DataFrames, MySQL CDC, Firecracker MicroVM, analytics engine |
| 4 | The Citadel | AST sandbox, double-entry auditor, JWT, HKDF field vault |
| 5 | The Observatory | Rayon log parser, Go metrics endpoint, async webhook broadcaster |
| 6 | Andromeda | Live dashboard — CPU/RAM gauges, error chart, WebSocket feed |
| 7 | The Nexus | Token-bucket rate limiter, W3C tracing, Redis Sentinel, mTLS, mmap coordination |
| 8 | The Arsenal | Python SDK, type stubs, click CLI (`frappe-rpg`), module scaffolding, benchmark DocType |
| 9 | The Accelerator | `moka` LRU doc cache, parallel multi-DocType fetch, AetherProxy (:7090), WASM functions |
| 10 | The Oracle | EWMA anomaly, CUSUM/PELT change-point, Drain log clustering, SimHash dedup, causality graph, Holt-Winters forecasting, OLS capacity runway |
| 11 | The Relay | GraphQL gateway, gRPC bridge, MQTT IoT ingestion, Kafka producer/consumer, proto3 generator |
| 12 | The Keymaster | FIDO2/WebAuthn, STS 15-min JWT + Redis revocation, HMAC request signing, Vault/KMS/GCP secrets, PKCS#11 HSM |
| 13 | The Architect | DuckDB OLAP (Polars SQL), DynamoDB KV, unified storage router, Parquet/Arrow export, schema evolution engine, S3 proxy |
| 14 | The Quartermaster | Per-tenant quota enforcer, weighted fair queue, usage metering, isolation validator, GDPR export pipeline |
| 15 | The Forge Master | Property-based testing, Go load harness (p50/p90/p99), chaos engineering, PyO3 contract tests, production readiness audit |
| 16 | The Pipeline | CI/CD automation, cross-platform wheel + Go binary build matrix |
| 17 | The Integrator | DocType lifecycle hooks, cache bridge, benchmark recorder |
| 18 | The Watcher | Prometheus metrics aggregator (:7078) scraping all sidecars, Grafana dashboards |
| 19 | The Toolsmith | `frappe-rpg doctor` CLI, API docs generator, dependency analyzer |
| 20 | The Query Engine | SQL optimizer, query result cache, slow-query detector |
| 21 | The File Engine | CSV/JSON bulk import/export pipeline |
| 22 | The Realtime Engine | Go WebSocket hub (:7079) replacing Frappe's socket.io, Redis pub/sub cross-worker delivery |
| 23 | The PDF Engine | Rust `printpdf` invoice generator — 75–150× faster than WeasyPrint |
| 24 | The Encryption Layer | Per-field AES-256-GCM with HKDF key derivation |
| 25 | The API Gateway | Go reverse proxy (:7080) — rate limiting, response cache, JWT validation, circuit breaker, gzip, singleflight |
| 26 | The Background Worker | Go goroutine pool (:7081) replacing RQ — reads `frappe:jobs:*` from Redis, ~500 jobs/sec |
| 27 | The Webhook Engine | Reliable webhook delivery (:7082) — exponential backoff, dead-letter queue, HMAC signing |
| 28 | The Search Engine | Trigram full-text search (:7083) across all DocTypes — TF-IDF, fuzzy match, highlight snippets,  **Phases 29–41 are planned.** See [tracker.md](frappe_rpg/docs/tracker.md) for the roadmap.

## Quick Start

### Prerequisites

- Frappe bench (Python 3.10+)
- Rust toolchain (`rustup install stable`)
- Go 1.21+
- `maturin` (`pip install maturin`)

### Install

```bash
cd $BENCH_PATH
bench get-app https://github.com/bluephoenix/frappe_rpg --branch main
bench install-app frappe_rpg
```

### Build the Rust Extension

```bash
cd apps/frappe_rpg
maturin develop --manifest-path rust/Cargo.toml
```

The compiled `.so` is installed directly into your Frappe venv. Import anywhere:

```python
import frappe_rs as rs

rs.__version__   # "0.28.0"
rs.__phase__     # "Phase 28 — The Search Engine"
```

### Build Go Sidecars

```bash
cd apps/frappe_rpg/go

# Core
go build -o relay/relay_bin             ./relay/
go build -o keymaster/keymaster_bin     ./keymaster/
go build -o s3proxy/s3proxy_bin         ./s3proxy/
go build -o loadtest/loadtest_bin       ./loadtest/

# Phases 18–28
go build -o metrics_aggregator/metrics_aggregator_bin  ./metrics_aggregator/
go build -o realtime/realtime_bin       ./realtime/
go build -o gateway/gateway_bin         ./gateway/
go build -o worker/worker_bin           ./worker/
go build -o webhook_engine/webhook_bin  ./webhook_engine/
go build -o search/search_bin           ./search/
go build -o proxy/aetherproxy_bin       ./proxy/
```

## Core API Surface

### frappe_rs (Rust — in-process)

```python
import frappe_rs as rs

# ── Analytics ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
rs.duckdb_query(data_json, "SELECT region, SUM(revenue) FROM t GROUP BY region")
rs.frappe_to_parquet(records_json, "/tmp/ledger.parquet")
rs.olap_query("SELECT * FROM t WHERE status='Open'", data_json)

# ── Cache ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────
rs.get_cached_doc("Sales Order", "SO-001")
rs.cache_doc("Sales Order", "SO-001", doc_json)
rs.flush_doc_cache()

# ── Statistical Intelligence ────────────────────────────────────
rs.ewma_score(series, alpha=0.3)
rs.pelt_changepoints(series, penalty=5.0)
rs.holtwinters_forecast(model_json, horizon=30)
rs.drain_parse(log_lines, depth=3, similarity_threshold=0.5)

# ── Identity ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
rs.generate_registration_challenge(user_id, rp_id)
rs.sign_request("POST", url, body, secret)
rs.hsm_sign(data, key_label)

# ── Multi-Tenancy ───────────────────────────────────────────────
rs.quota_check("tenant-a", "api_calls", amount=1)
rs.quota_consume("tenant-a", "api_calls", amount=1)
rs.meter_record("tenant-a", "api_calls", delta=1.0)
rs.gdpr_export("tenant-a", encryption_key_hex)

# ── Quality ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
rs.propcheck_suite(n_trials=1000)
rs.chaos_circuit_breaker("my-service", success=False, threshold=3, half_open_secs=30)
rs.audit_production(config_json)

# ── PDF ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
rs.generate_invoice_pdf(invoice_json)          # Phase 23

# ── Encryption ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
rs.encrypt_field(plaintext, field_key)         # Phase 24
rs.decrypt_field(ciphertext, field_key)
```

### Go Sidecars (HTTP)

```bash
# Issue a 15-min JWT
curl -X POST http://localhost:7075/keymaster/token \
  -d '{"user_id":"admin","email":"admin@example.com","roles":["System Manager"]}'

# Verify + auto-refresh
curl http://localhost:7075/keymaster/verify \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer "

# GraphQL query
curl -X POST http://localhost:7071/graphql \
  -d '{"query":"{ salesorder { name status grand_total } }"}'

# Store an object
curl -X PUT http://localhost:7076/s3/frappe-attachments/backup.tar.gz \
  --data-binary @backup.tar.gz

# Run a load test
curl -X POST http://localhost:7077/loadtest/run \
  -d '{"target_url":"https://mysite/api","concurrency":20,"duration_secs":30}'

# Full-text search
curl "http://localhost:7083/search?q=sales+invoice&doctype=Sales+Invoice&fuzzy=true"

# Subscribe to real-time events (WebSocket)
# ws://localhost:7079/ws?site=mysite.localhost

# API Gateway — proxy with cache + circuit breaker
curl http://localhost:7080/api/resource/Customer

# Enqueue a background job
curl -X POST http://localhost:7081/jobs \
  -d '{"method":"frappe.email.queue.flush","queue":"default"}'

# Deliver a webhook
curl -X POST http://localhost:7082/webhooks \
  -d '{"url":"https://example.com/hook","payload":{"event":"doc_saved"},"secret":"s3cr3t"}'

# Unified metrics (all sidecars aggregated)
curl http://localhost:7078/metrics
```

## CLI

```bash
# After installation
frappe-rpg version          # SDK + Rust extension version
frappe-rpg snapshot         # Live system health (CPU / RAM / workers)
frappe-rpg logs parse --path ./logs/frappe.log --last 1000
frappe-rpg bench fast_sum   # Micro-benchmark any frappe_rs function
frappe-rpg audit verify --file /tmp/gl.json
frappe-rpg scout status     # Go sidecar health
frappe-rpg generate module mymodule --functions fn1,fn2
frappe-rpg doctor           # Full system diagnostics (Phase 19)
frappe-rpg api-docs         # Generate API reference from stubs
```

| `PROXY_PORT` | proxy | `7090` | AetherProxy port |
| `UPSTREAM` | proxy | `http://127.0.0.1:8000` | AetherProxy upstream |

## Development

### Pre-commit Hooks

```bash
cd apps/frappe_rpg
pre-commit install
```

Configured tools: `ruff`, `eslint`, `prettier`, `pyupgrade`.

### Type Stubs

The file `rust/frappe_rs.pyi` provides full type annotations for all ~400 Rust functions. Your IDE will autocomplete and type-check all `frappe_rs` calls.

### Scaffolding New Modules

```bash
frappe-rpg generate module my_engine --functions fn_a,fn_b
```

This creates `rust/src/my_engine.rs`, wires `mod` + `wrap_pyfunction!` in `lib.rs`, adds stubs to `frappe_rs.pyi`, creates a test file, and runs `maturin develop`.

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_rpg/frappe_rpg/docs/phase_10_oracle.md
# 🔮 Phase 10: The Oracle — Intelligent Operations (Zero ML / Zero LLM)

> **Status:** ⬜ Planned
> **Focus:** Anomaly detection, log intelligence, predictive scaling, and root cause analysis — all using pure Rust classical algorithms with zero ML libraries, zero LLM API calls, and zero Python dependencies
> **Prerequisites:** Phase 5 (Observatory) complete — metrics, logs, and event stream live

---

## 🎯 Phase Objective

Use the rich observability data flowing through the Phase 5 pipeline to give Frappe **self-awareness**:

- Know when something is wrong **before users report it**
- Explain **why** it went wrong automatically
- **Predict** resource exhaustion before it happens
- **Group and deduplicate** noise so engineers see signal, not spam

Every algorithm used is a classical statistical or computer science method implemented in pure Rust. No model files. No training time. No API keys. No black boxes. Every decision the Oracle makes is fully explainable with a mathematical formula.

---

## 📋 Tasks

---

### 🦀 10.1 — EWMA Anomaly Detector

> **Goal:** Flag error rate spikes in real time using Exponentially Weighted Moving Average. The most battle-tested technique in production SRE systems.

#### Rust (`src/oracle/anomaly.rs`)

- [ ] **`ewma_score(series, alpha)`** — compute rolling mean + standard deviation with exponential weighting (recent values matter more)
- [ ] **`zscore_anomaly(value, mean, std, threshold_sigma)`** — returns `true` if value is beyond `k × σ` from baseline. Default: 3σ = 99.7% confidence
- [ ] **`iqr_outliers(sorted_series)`** — IQR-based outlier detection, immune to extreme outliers distorting the mean
- [ ] **`spike_context(series, spike_idx, window)`** — returns the N values before/after a detected spike for context display

#### How It Works
```
Error rate baseline: 12 errors/min (EWMA over 24 hours)
EWMA σ = 4.2
3σ threshold = 12 + (3 × 4.2) = 24.6 errors/min
Current: 47 errors/min → ANOMALY DETECTED
```

#### Frappe Integration
- Runs every 30 seconds as a Frappe scheduled task
- Reads error counts from Phase 5 Log Parser output
- Fires `frappe:oracle:anomaly` Redis event → Go Webhook Dispatcher alerts Slack

---

### 🦀 10.2 — Change Point Detector (CUSUM)

> **Goal:** Detect when system behaviour **fundamentally shifts** — e.g., after a deployment, schema change, or configuration update. EWMA catches spikes; CUSUM catches drift.

#### Rust (`src/oracle/changepoint.rs`)

- [ ] **`cusum_detect(series, target, slack, threshold)`** — Cumulative Sum control chart, the gold standard industrial algorithm for detecting step-changes in a process
- [ ] **`pelt_changepoints(series, penalty)`** — Pruned Exact Linear Time algorithm, finds **multiple** change points efficiently in O(n) time
- [ ] **`deployment_correlate(change_points, deploy_events)`** — automatically checks if a detected change point timestamp aligns within ±5 minutes of a `git push` or `bench migrate` event logged in Redis

#### Example Output
```json
{
  "change_point_at": "2026-03-25T09:14:00Z",
  "metric": "avg_response_time_ms",
  "before_mean": 82,
  "after_mean": 340,
  "correlated_event": "bench migrate (frappe==16.2.1)",
  "confidence": 0.97
}
```

---

### 🦀 10.3 — Drain Log Template Extractor

> **Goal:** Reduce 1,000,000 raw error log lines into 20 meaningful templates with occurrence counts. No LLM. Pure tree algorithm.

#### Rust (`src/oracle/drain.rs`)

The **Drain** algorithm (used by Loki, Splunk, and DataDog internally):

- [ ] **`drain_parse(log_lines, depth, similarity_threshold)`** — builds a prefix tree → clusters similar lines → extracts parametric templates with `` wildcards
- [ ] **`top_templates(n)`** — return the top N templates by frequency, sorted descending
- [ ] **`template_trend(template_id, window_secs)`** — for each template, show if its frequency is rising, falling, or stable

#### Example
```
Input  (1,000,000 lines) → Drain → Output (23 templates):

  #1  [428,192 hits] "MariaDB: Too many connections from host "
  #2  [ 92,441 hits] "frappe.exceptions.ValidationError:  is mandatory"
  #3  [ 14,009 hits] "redis.exceptions.ConnectionError:  refused"
  ...
```

**Delivered to Slack via Phase 5 Webhook in a single digest message — not 1M raw lines.**

---

### 🦀 10.4 — SimHash Error Fingerprinting

> **Goal:** Deduplicate "the same error with slightly different details" so engineers see 1 alert, not 500 identical ones.

#### Rust (`src/oracle/simhash.rs`)

- [ ] **`simhash(text)`** — compute a 64-bit locality-sensitive hash of a log line. Two log lines that are 90% similar will have hashes that differ by only a few bits
- [ ] **`hamming_distance(h1, h2)`** — bit difference between two hashes; distance  **Goal:** Automatically discover which errors tend to **cause or follow** other errors. Built from event co-occurrence, not ML.

#### Rust (`src/oracle/causality.rs`)

- [ ] **`temporal_correlation(event_log, window_secs)`** — for every pair of error templates, count how often template B follows template A within a time window
- [ ] **`build_causality_graph(correlations, min_confidence)`** — returns a directed graph (using Rust `petgraph`) where edge A→B means "A precedes B in 80%+ of occurrences"
- [ ] **`query_likely_causes(template_id)`** — given an active alert template, return its top 3 likely precursor events from the graph
- [ ] **`export_dot(graph)`** — export the graph as GraphViz `.dot` format for visualisation in the Phase 6 Andromeda dashboard

#### Example Output
```
Active Alert: "redis.exceptions.ConnectionError"
Likely Causes (confidence):
  1. "rq worker killed (OOM)" — 91% of the time this preceded the Redis error
  2. "MariaDB long running query > 30s" — 64%
  3. "Memory usage exceeded 90%" — 58%
```

---

### 🦀 10.6 — Holt-Winters Predictive Scaler

> **Goal:** Forecast RQ queue depth 30–60 minutes ahead and pre-scale workers **before** the spike arrives. Pure triple exponential smoothing — no ML library.

#### Rust (`src/oracle/forecaster.rs`)

- [ ] **`holtwinters_fit(series, alpha, beta, gamma, period)`** — fit level, trend, and seasonal components to historical queue depth data
- [ ] **`holtwinters_forecast(model, horizon)`** — predict next `horizon` data points
- [ ] **`auto_tune_params(series)`** — grid search over α/β/γ to find params minimising MAE — pure Rust, runs in  **Goal:** Thresholds that automatically calibrate to your site's reality, not generic defaults.

Classical sites have different normal baselines. A Frappe instance processing payroll has 10,000 errors/day as "normal". A light CRM might alert at 50.

#### Rust (`src/oracle/adaptive.rs`)

- [ ] **`percentile_threshold(series, p)`** — compute the Nth percentile of historical values as the alert threshold. E.g., `p=99` = alert only in the top 1% historically
- [ ] **`rolling_baseline_update(current_baseline, new_window, decay)`** — continuously update the baseline as the site evolves, with a decay factor so old data phases out
- [ ] **`business_hours_aware(series, timezone)`** — separate baselines for business hours vs. off-hours (different normal behaviour)
- [ ] **`cooldown_tracker(alert_key, cooldown_secs)`** — prevents alert storms: once fired, suppress the same alert for N seconds regardless of readings

---

### 🦀 10.8 — Capacity Planning Reporter

> **Goal:** Answer "When will we run out of headroom?" using trend extrapolation — no ML, just math.

#### Rust (`src/oracle/capacity.rs`)

- [ ] **`linear_extrapolate(series, horizon_days)`** — OLS (Ordinary Least Squares) linear regression on disk/memory/connection growth trends
- [ ] **`confidence_interval(slope, residuals, horizon)`** — returns [lower, upper] bound so the report says "you will hit 90% disk in 12–18 days"
- [ ] **`resource_runway(current, capacity, growth_rate)`** — simple "days until full" calculation with configurable safety margin
- [ ] **Frappe Report: `Capacity Runway`** — a Frappe Report page showing each resource (disk, connections, queue depth) with extrapolated runway days

#### Example Output
```
📊 Capacity Runway Report (March 25, 2026)

Resource              Current    Trend        Runway
────────────────────────────────────────────────────
Disk (var/lib)        68%        +0.8%/day    ≈ 40 days  ⚠️
MariaDB connections   72/100     +2/week      ≈ 14 days  🔴
RQ default queue      avg: 120   +15/day      ≈ 7 days   🔴
Redis memory          48%        stable        ≥ 90 days  ✅
```

---

### 🦀 10.9 — Dead Letter Job Classifier

> **Goal:** Automatically classify failed RQ background jobs into known categories and suggest fixes — no ML, just a rule engine.

#### Rust (`src/oracle/job_classifier.rs`)

- [ ] **`classify_failure(job_error_text)`** — matches the job's error string against a configurable rule set (compiled Rust regex) and returns a `FailureCategory`
- [ ] **`suggest_fix(category)`** — returns a structured `FixSuggestion` with probable cause and recommended action

#### Built-in Rule Set
| Pattern | Category | Suggested Fix |
|---------|----------|---------------|
| `OperationalError: too many connections` | `DB_CONNECTION` | Increase `pool_size` in `common_site_config.json` |
| `ConnectionRefusedError.*13000` | `REDIS_DOWN` | Run `bench restart redis` |
| `frappe.exceptions.TimeLimitExceeded` | `TIMEOUT` | Increase `worker_timeout` or split the job |
| `OSError: [Errno 28] No space left` | `DISK_FULL` | Clear old logs or backups |
| `PermissionError.*frappe-bench` | `FILE_PERMISSION` | Run `bench setup supervisor` |

---

### 📊 10.10 — Oracle Dashboard (Phase 6 Extension)

Extends the Andromeda Phase 6 dashboard with:

- [ ] **Anomaly Timeline**: Bar chart showing detected anomalies per hour over 7 days
- [ ] **Change Points Overlay**: Mark detected changes on the metrics charts from Phase 6
- [ ] **Top Error Templates**: Live-updating Drain template table with sparklines per template
- [ ] **Causality Graph Viewer**: GraphViz-rendered interactive D3.js diagram of error relationships
- [ ] **Capacity Runway Panel**: Traffic light grid (green/amber/red) per resource with days remaining
- [ ] **Scale Forecast Chart**: Queue depth actual vs. Holt-Winters forecast for next 2 hours

---

### ✅ 10.11 — Phase 10 Validation

- [ ] EWMA detector flags a seeded error spike within 30 seconds of injection
- [ ] CUSUM correctly identifies a step-change introduced in test metric series
- [ ] Drain reduces 100k synthesised log lines to 80% confidence
- [ ] Holt-Winters forecast MAE < 15% on held-out historical queue data
- [ ] Capacity Runway report generates valid runway for all 4 resource types
- [ ] Dead letter classifier correctly categorises 10 known failure patterns
- [ ] Zero Python ML libraries, zero ML model files, zero LLM API calls in entire phase

---

## 🧮 Algorithm Reference

| Algorithm | Domain | Complexity | Crate Used |
|-----------|--------|-----------|------------|
| EWMA + Z-Score | Anomaly detection | O(n) | **none (pure Rust)** |
| CUSUM | Change detection | O(n) | **none (pure Rust)** |
| PELT | Multi-change-point | O(n) | **none (pure Rust)** |
| Drain (prefix tree) | Log clustering | O(n log n) | **none (pure Rust)** |
| SimHash | Fuzzy deduplication | O(1) per line | **none (pure Rust)** |
| Holt-Winters | Time-series forecast | O(n) | **none (pure Rust)** |
| OLS Regression | Capacity planning | O(n) | **none (pure Rust)** |
| Pearson Correlation | Event causality | O(n²) pairs | **none (pure Rust)** |
| Petgraph DAG | Causality graph | O(V+E) | **`petgraph`** |
| IQR / Percentile | Adaptive thresholds | O(n log n) | **none (pure Rust)** |

**Total external crates required:** 1 (`petgraph` for the causality graph — pure Rust data structure, no ML)

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_rpg/frappe_rpg/docs/phase_11_relay.md
# 🔌 Phase 11: The Relay — Multi-Protocol Integration

> **Status:** ⬜ Planned
> **Focus:** Protocol bridges so Frappe can speak to anything — IoT devices, mobile apps, data lakes, and legacy systems — without Python middleware
> **Prerequisites:** Phase 7 (Nexus) — distributed infrastructure operational

---

## 🎯 Phase Objective

Frappe today speaks HTTP/REST. The modern data world speaks GraphQL, gRPC, MQTT, and Kafka. Every protocol mismatch currently requires a Python adapter that blocks a Gunicorn worker.

**The Relay** builds native Go and Rust protocol bridges that let Frappe receive and emit data in any format — without touching a Python worker for the translation layer.

---

## 📋 Tasks

### 🏹 11.1 — GraphQL Gateway (Go)

> **Goal:** Auto-generate a fully compliant GraphQL API from Frappe DocType metadata. Any modern frontend (React, Vue, Next.js, mobile) can query Frappe with zero custom API writing.

#### Go (`go/relay/graphql/`)
- [ ] **DocType schema reader**: On startup, fetch all DocType JSON from `frappe/modules/` and build GraphQL type definitions automatically
- [ ] **`Query` resolver**: Maps `{customer(name: "CUST-001") { name, customer_name, outstanding_amount }}` → `frappe.get_doc()`
- [ ] **`Mutation` resolver**: Maps GraphQL mutations → `frappe.save_doc()` with permission checks preserved
- [ ] **`Subscription` resolver**: Real-time updates via Phase 5 WebSocket hub — `subscribe { Customer { name } }` pushes changes as they happen
- [ ] **DataLoader batching**: Groups N parallel field resolvers into 1 `frappe.get_all()` call — prevents N+1 query problem
- [ ] **Endpoint**: `GET/POST /graphql` served by the Go Relay sidecar

#### Frappe Hook
```python
# hooks.py — No change needed. GraphQL reads DocType metadata automatically.
```

#### Impact
- **Before**: Frontend dev writes a new `frappe.whitelist()` API for every new field they need
- **After**: Frontend queries exactly what it needs via GraphQL — zero backend changes

---

### 🏹 11.2 — gRPC Service Projection (Go + Protobuf)

> **Goal:** Expose any Frappe DocType as a gRPC service. Enables type-safe, high-performance service-to-service communication between Frappe and other backend microservices.

#### Go (`go/relay/grpc/`)
- [ ] **`DoctypeService.proto` generator**: Rust tool that reads DocType JSON and emits a `.proto` schema file
- [ ] **gRPC server** at `:7073`: `GetDoc`, `SaveDoc`, `GetList`, `StreamEvents` RPCs
- [ ] **Bidirectional streaming**: `StreamEvents` RPC sends real-time DocType change events to subscribers over a persistent gRPC stream
- [ ] **Interceptors**: Auth check, rate limit (Phase 7), and trace span injection per call

---

### 🏹 11.3 — MQTT Broker Bridge (Go)

> **Goal:** IoT devices (factory sensors, warehouse scanners, POS hardware) publish data to an MQTT topic → Frappe DocType is created/updated automatically.

#### Go (`go/relay/mqtt/`)
- [ ] **MQTT subscriber**: Connects to any MQTT broker (Mosquitto, EMQX, AWS IoT Core)
- [ ] **Topic → DocType mapper**: Config-driven: `sensors/temperature/#` → `IoT Reading` DocType
- [ ] **Payload transformer** (Rust): Translate raw MQTT binary/JSON payload into Frappe-compatible field format
- [ ] **Batched insert**: Buffer 1,000 incoming messages and write in one Frappe bulk insert — prevents worker saturation from high-frequency devices

#### Use Case
```
Factory floor sensor → MQTT broker → Go Relay → Frappe "Machine Reading" DocType
(1,000 readings/second)                         (1 bulk insert every 500ms)
```

---

### 🏹 11.4 — Apache Kafka Producer/Consumer (Go)

> **Goal:** Route Frappe events to/from Kafka for mission-critical, guaranteed-delivery event streams.

#### Go (`go/relay/kafka/`)
- [ ] **Kafka producer**: On `doc_events`, publish structured Avro events to configured Kafka topics
- [ ] **Kafka consumer**: Subscribe to external Kafka topics and replay events as Frappe hooks
- [ ] **Dead letter topic**: Failed Frappe writes go to a `frappe.dlq` topic for replay
- [ ] **Exactly-once semantics**: Idempotent producer + consumer group offsets for guaranteed delivery

---

### ✅ 11.5 — Phase 11 Validation

- [ ] GraphQL: Query a Customer with 3 linked DocTypes in one request, no extra API code needed
- [ ] gRPC: Fetch 1,000 Sales Orders via gRPC in < 500ms
- [ ] MQTT: 1,000 messages/second ingested without overwhelming a Gunicorn worker
- [ ] Kafka: Frappe `doc_events` arrive in Kafka consumer within 200ms of save

---

*Previous Phase: [Phase 10 — The Oracle](./phase_10_oracle.md)*
*Return to: [Master Plan](./plan.md)*

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_rpg/frappe_rpg/docs/phase_12_keymaster.md
# 🗝️ Phase 12: The Keymaster — Zero-Trust Identity

> **Status:** ⬜ Planned
> **Focus:** Production-grade, hardware-backed identity and access management for Frappe — passwordless auth, ephemeral tokens, request signing, and external secrets management
> **Prerequisites:** Phase 4 (Citadel) — base crypto and token infrastructure operational

---

## 🎯 Phase Objective

Phase 4 built strong cryptographic primitives. Phase 12 applies them at the **organisational identity layer** — how humans and services authenticate, how credentials are issued and rotated, and how Frappe fits into a modern Zero-Trust security model.

---

## 📋 Tasks

### 🦀 12.1 — FIDO2 / WebAuthn (Passwordless Login)

> **Goal:** Allow Frappe users to log in with a hardware key (YubiKey), Touch ID, or Face ID — no password ever transmitted or stored.

#### Rust (`src/webauthn.rs`)
- [ ] **`generate_registration_challenge(user_id, rp_id)`** — creates a cryptographic challenge using `webauthn-rs` crate
- [ ] **`verify_registration(challenge, credential_json)`** — validates the device-signed response and extracts the public key
- [ ] **`generate_authentication_challenge(user_id)`** — per-login challenge tied to the user's stored public key
- [ ] **`verify_authentication(challenge, assertion_json)`** — verifies the device signature, returns pass/fail

#### Frappe Integration
- Custom Login Page: adds "Sign in with passkey" button
- Frappe `User` DocType gains a `webauthn_credentials` child table
- Falls back to standard Frappe login if no passkey registered

---

### 🦀 12.2 — Short-Lived Ephemeral API Tokens (STS-style)

> **Goal:** Replace long-lived Frappe API keys (which never expire) with 15-minute tokens, issued by the Go sidecar, signed with the Ed25519 key from Phase 4.

#### Go (`go/keymaster/sts.go`)
- [ ] **`POST /keymaster/token`**: Accepts Frappe session cookie → issues a signed JWT valid for 15 minutes
- [ ] **`GET /keymaster/verify`**: Validates a token without hitting MariaDB — pure Ed25519 signature check
- [ ] **Automatic refresh**: If token has  **Goal:** Service-to-service calls between Frappe apps and Go sidecars must be cryptographically signed — no request is trusted without a valid HMAC-SHA256 signature.

#### Rust (`src/request_signer.rs`)
- [ ] **`sign_request(method, url, body, secret_key)`** — generates `X-Frappe-Signature` header with timestamp + HMAC-SHA256
- [ ] **`verify_request_signature(headers, body, secret_key, max_age_secs)`** — rejects requests older than 5 minutes (replay attack prevention), validates HMAC
- [ ] **Frappe middleware hook**: `before_request` verifies signature on internal service API calls

---

### 🏹 12.4 — External Secrets Bridge (HashiCorp Vault / AWS KMS)

> **Goal:** Stop storing secrets in `site_config.json`. Fetch them at runtime from an enterprise secrets manager.

#### Go (`go/keymaster/secrets.go`)
- [ ] **HashiCorp Vault adapter**: `GET /v1/secret/frappe/` → injects secrets into Frappe config at startup
- [ ] **AWS KMS adapter**: Decrypt Frappe master key using KMS DataKey — the plaintext key never touches disk
- [ ] **GCP Secret Manager adapter**: Same pattern for GCP deployments
- [ ] **Secret rotation daemon**: Background goroutine polls for secret version changes and hot-reloads Frappe config without restart
- [ ] **Fallback**: If external vault is unreachable, falls back to encrypted local secrets file

---

### 🦀 12.5 — Hardware Security Module (HSM) Interface

> **Goal:** For highest-security deployments — sign tokens and encrypt data using a physical HSM (PKCS#11 interface).

#### Rust (`src/hsm.rs`)
- [ ] **PKCS#11 dynamic loader**: Opens the HSM's shared library via Rust `libloading`
- [ ] **`hsm_sign(data, key_label)`** — delegates signing to HSM hardware, private key never leaves the device
- [ ] **`hsm_decrypt(ciphertext, key_label)`** — HSM decrypts; plaintext returned to Rust only in memory
- [ ] **Soft-HSM fallback**: Use `SoftHSM2` for development environments that lack physical hardware

---

### ✅ 12.6 — Phase 12 Validation

- [ ] WebAuthn registration + login flow works end-to-end with a software authenticator
- [ ] Ephemeral token rejected after 15 minutes
- [ ] Request signature older than 5 minutes rejected with 401
- [ ] HashiCorp Vault adapter successfully injects secrets into Frappe config on startup
- [ ] Revoked token rejected within 100ms via Redis lookup

---

*Previous Phase: [Phase 11 — The Relay](./phase_11_relay.md)*
*Return to: [Master Plan](./plan.md)*

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_rpg/frappe_rpg/docs/phase_13_architect.md
# 🏗️ Phase 13: The Architect — Data Pipeline, Columnar Storage & Extended Storage Backends

> **Status:** ⬜ Planned
> **Focus:** Two parallel optional storage paths — **DuckDB** (self-hosted OLAP) and **DynamoDB** (cloud-native KV tier) — plus Parquet/Arrow, S3, and safe schema evolution
> **Prerequisites:** Phase 3 (Battlefield) — Polars engine operational; Phase 9 (Accelerator) — caching layer live

---

## 🎯 Phase Objective

MariaDB handles transactions well but struggles with analytics, unlimited-concurrency reads, and large file storage. Phase 13 introduces two optional extended storage backends you can enable independently or together:

### Two Paths — Use One or Both

| | **DuckDB** | **DynamoDB** |
|--|-----------|-------------|
| **Type** | Embedded OLAP analytical engine | Managed cloud key-value store |
| **Best for** | Reports, GROUP BY, exports, OLAP | Sessions, hot-path doc reads, rate counters |
| **Deployment** | Self-hosted, zero external service | AWS account required |
| **Latency** | 50–500ms (large analytical queries) | ** **Simple rule:** Use **DuckDB** when you need to ask complex questions of large datasets locally. Use **DynamoDB** when you need the same answer in under 2ms from any node at unlimited scale.

---

---

## 📋 Tasks

---

### 🦀 13.1 — DynamoDB Bridge (High-Velocity Key-Value Storage)

> **Goal:** Use AWS DynamoDB as an ultra-fast key-value tier for data that needs single-digit millisecond reads at any scale — sessions, hot-path document fragments, audit logs, and real-time metrics.

#### Why DynamoDB for Frappe?
- MariaDB `get_doc()` at high concurrency: **8–50ms**, connection-pool limited
- DynamoDB read (eventually consistent): ** **Goal:** A single Python-facing API that routes to DuckDB or DynamoDB based on `site_config.json` — switch backends with zero code changes.

#### Config-Driven Activation (`site_config.json`)
```json
// Both enabled — DuckDB for analytics, DynamoDB for hot KV
{
  "duckdb_db_path": "/var/frappe/analytics.duckdb",
  "dynamodb_table_prefix": "frappe-prod-",
  "aws_region": "ap-south-1"
}

// Self-hosted only — DuckDB, no AWS
{
  "duckdb_db_path": "/var/frappe/analytics.duckdb"
}

// Cloud-only — DynamoDB, no local storage
{
  "dynamodb_table_prefix": "frappe-prod-"
}
```

#### Single Python API
```python
from frappe_rpg.api import kv_get, kv_put, olap_query

# Routes to DynamoDB ( **Goal:** Export any Frappe dataset directly to columnar Parquet format — ready for DuckDB, BigQuery, ClickHouse, or data lakes like S3/Delta Lake.

#### Rust (`src/arrow_bridge.rs`) — using `arrow2` + `parquet2`
- [ ] **`frappe_to_parquet(sql_query, output_path)`** — stream MariaDB result rows directly into a Parquet file with native type mapping; never loads the full dataset into Python memory
- [ ] **`frappe_to_arrow_ipc(sql_query)`** — return Arrow IPC stream in-memory — directly consumable by DuckDB, Polars, or pandas without file I/O
- [ ] **Schema inference**: Automatically maps Frappe field types (`Currency`, `Date`, `Link`, `Int`) to Arrow types (`Float64`, `Date32`, `Utf8`, `Int64`)
- [ ] **Predicate pushdown**: Pass `WHERE` filters to MariaDB; only qualified rows enter the Parquet writer

#### Frappe API
```python
# Export last year's GL entries to Parquet for BigQuery ingestion
frappe_rpg.api.export_parquet(
    query="SELECT * FROM `tabGL Entry` WHERE posting_date >= '2025-01-01'",
    output="/tmp/gl_2025.parquet"
)
# → Streams 10M rows in ~8s, produces a 120MB Parquet file (vs 1.4GB CSV)
```

---

### 🦀 13.3 — DuckDB OLAP Bridge

> **Goal:** Run analytical queries over Frappe data (or exported Parquet files) using DuckDB — multithreaded columnar SQL, 100x faster than MariaDB for aggregations.

#### Rust (`src/duckdb_bridge.rs`)
- [ ] **`duckdb_query(sql, parquet_glob?)`** — run a DuckDB SQL query; optionally over a glob of Parquet files (e.g., `s3://my-bucket/frappe/gl_*.parquet`)
- [ ] **In-memory mode**: For ad-hoc dashboard queries over recently exported data
- [ ] **Persistent mode**: DuckDB file on disk for frequently-queried datasets

#### Example
```python
# Runs in 120ms. Same query in MariaDB: 45 seconds.
result = frappe_rpg.api.duckdb_query("""
    SELECT company, SUM(debit - credit) as net
    FROM '/tmp/gl_2025.parquet'
    GROUP BY company
    ORDER BY net DESC
""")
```

---

### 🏹 13.4 — S3-Compatible Object Storage Bridge (Go)

> **Goal:** Store Frappe file attachments, backups, and exports directly to S3 / MinIO / R2 / GCS — not on local disk.

#### Go (`go/relay/storage/`)
- [ ] **`PUT /storage/upload`**: Stream multipart upload directly to S3 without buffering the whole file in Gunicorn memory
- [ ] **`GET /storage/download/{key}`**: Pre-signed URL generation or direct proxy stream from S3
- [ ] **Frappe attachment hook**: Intercept `frappe.get_doc("File")` saves and route large files (> 1MB) to S3 automatically
- [ ] **Backup streamer**: Pipe `mysqldump` stdin directly to S3 multipart upload — no temp file required
- [ ] **Providers**: AWS S3, Cloudflare R2, MinIO (self-hosted), GCS — configurable via `site_config.json`

---

### 🦀 13.5 — Schema Evolution Engine

> **Goal:** Safe, validated, rollback-capable DocType migrations with Rust enforcing data integrity before any schema change is applied.

#### Rust (`src/schema_evolution.rs`)
- [ ] **`validate_migration(old_doctype_json, new_doctype_json)`** — detects breaking changes: field deletions, type changes, mandatory-ification of existing fields
- [ ] **`generate_migration_plan(old, new)`** — outputs an ordered list of safe SQL `ALTER TABLE` statements
- [ ] **`simulate_migration(plan, sample_rows)`** — runs the migration on 100 sample rows in a transaction that is always rolled back — proves it works before touching production
- [ ] **`apply_migration(plan)`** — applies with a Rust-held savepoint; on any error, fast rollback to exact pre-migration state

---

### ✅ 13.6 — Phase 13 Validation

- [ ] DynamoDB `dynamo_get()` returns in < 5ms on AWS (eventually consistent read)
- [ ] DynamoDB TTL correctly expires session items after configured duration
- [ ] Parquet export of 1M GL entries completes in < 30s and produces valid Arrow schema
- [ ] DuckDB query over exported Parquet returns same result as equivalent MariaDB query
- [ ] S3 upload of a 100MB file completes without buffering in Python memory
- [ ] Schema evolution validator correctly detects and blocks a breaking field type change

---

## 🗄️ Storage Tier Summary

| Tier | Technology | Best For | Latency |
|------|-----------|---------|---------|
| Transactional | MariaDB | DocTypes, workflows, permissions | 5–50ms |
| Hot KV Cache | DynamoDB | Sessions, hot docs, rate counters | < 2ms |
| Analytical | DuckDB + Parquet | Reports, aggregations, exports | 50–500ms |
| Object Storage | S3/MinIO via Go | Files, backups, large blobs | Network-bound |
| In-Process Cache | Rust `moka` (Phase 9) | Repeated same-doc reads, same node | < 0.1ms |
| Distributed Cache | Redis | Pub/Sub, task queues | < 1ms |

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_rpg/frappe_rpg/docs/phase_14_quartermaster.md
# 👑 Phase 14: The Quartermaster — Multi-Tenancy & Resource Governance

> **Status:** ⬜ Planned
> **Focus:** SaaS-grade Frappe — per-tenant resource isolation, fair job scheduling, usage metering, and tenant data export for compliance
> **Prerequisites:** Phase 7 (Nexus) — distributed infrastructure; Phase 4 (Citadel) — security hardened

---

## 🎯 Phase Objective

Running Frappe as a SaaS product means multiple tenants sharing the same server. Without governance:
- One heavy tenant can exhaust the MariaDB connection pool for everyone
- A burst of background jobs from one company starves another's critical workflows
- Usage-based billing is impossible without metering
- GDPR/data portability requires clean per-tenant data export

Phase 14 enforces hard boundaries between tenants using Rust at the enforcement layer.

---

## 📋 Tasks

### 🦀 14.1 — Per-Tenant Resource Quota Enforcer

> **Goal:** Hard, non-bypassable limits on per-tenant CPU time, memory, API calls, storage, and RQ job submission rate — enforced by Rust, not Python config.

#### Rust (`src/quotas.rs`)
- [ ] **`QuotaConfig` struct**: Per-tenant limits loaded from `site_config.json` (`max_api_calls_per_min`, `max_worker_memory_mb`, `max_jobs_per_min`, `max_storage_gb`)
- [ ] **`check_api_quota(site, user)`** — Token bucket per site; rejects with HTTP 429 + `Retry-After` header when limit exceeded
- [ ] **`check_job_quota(site, queue)`** — Blocks `frappe.enqueue()` calls that exceed the tenant's job submission rate
- [ ] **`check_storage_quota(site)`** — Checks tenant's total `tabFile` storage usage before allowing upload
- [ ] **`quota_report(site)`** — Returns current consumption vs. limits for the Andromeda dashboard

---

### 🦀 14.2 — Fair Queue Scheduler (Weighted Fair Queuing)

> **Goal:** Guarantee that no single tenant can starve others in the RQ worker pool, regardless of how many jobs they submit.

#### Rust (`src/fair_queue.rs`)
- [ ] **Weighted Fair Queuing algorithm**: Each tenant gets a configurable weight (e.g., Enterprise = 3x, Starter = 1x). Rust schedules dequeuing proportionally to weight regardless of queue depth
- [ ] **`next_job(worker_id)`** — selects the next job to execute across all tenant queues using WFQ
- [ ] **Anti-starvation**: Even a weight-0 tenant gets at least 1 job slot per N cycles — no tenant completely blocked
- [ ] **Go hook**: Wraps the existing RQ worker dispatch loop to call `frappe_rs.next_job()` for ordering

---

### 🦀 14.3 — Usage Metering Engine

> **Goal:** Accurate, tamper-evident metering of billable resource consumption per tenant, per minute.

#### Rust (`src/metering.rs`)
- [ ] **`record_api_call(site, endpoint, duration_ms)`** — atomic increment to in-process ring buffer, flushed to DynamoDB (Phase 13) every 60 seconds
- [ ] **`record_job(site, queue, duration_ms, memory_mb)`** — background job completion metering
- [ ] **`record_storage_change(site, delta_bytes)`** — on each file upload/delete
- [ ] **`get_tenant_invoice_data(site, period_start, period_end)`** — returns JSON summary for billing integration

#### Frappe DocType
- `Tenant Usage Log` — hourly aggregates stored in Frappe for billing/reporting

---

### 🦀 14.4 — Tenant Data Isolation Validator

> **Goal:** Rust compile-time and runtime checks that no cross-tenant data leaks occur.

#### Rust (`src/tenant_guard.rs`)
- [ ] **`assert_tenant_scope(site, doctype, name)`** — before any `frappe.get_doc()` call in multi-tenant API paths, verify the requested document belongs to the calling site
- [ ] **`cross_tenant_audit_log`** — any attempt to access another tenant's data (even failed) is logged to the immutable Phase 5 audit ring buffer
- [ ] **Query prefix validator**: Rust validates that all multi-tenant SQL queries have `site =` in the WHERE clause before execution

---

### 🏹 14.5 — Tenant Data Export Pipeline (GDPR / Data Portability)

> **Goal:** Any tenant can request a complete, encrypted, signed export of all their data — delivered as a single archive with full integrity guarantees.

#### Go + Rust (`go/quartermaster/export.go`, `src/export_signer.rs`)
- [ ] **Full export packager**: Go streams all tenant DocType data from MariaDB + file attachments from S3 into a compressed archive
- [ ] **Encryption**: Wraps the archive with the tenant's public key (Phase 4 Vault)
- [ ] **Signing**: Rust signs the archive with the platform's Ed25519 key — tenant can verify authenticity
- [ ] **Progress tracking**: Frappe `Tenant Export Request` DocType tracks status, progress %, and download link
- [ ] **Import validator**: Rust tool to verify inbound archives from other Frappe instances before import

---

### ✅ 14.6 — Phase 14 Validation

- [ ] API quota enforcer rejects requests beyond the limit with correct `Retry-After` header
- [ ] WFQ scheduler distributes jobs proportionally across 3 tenants with different weights under load
- [ ] Usage metering records match independently counted API calls within 1%
- [ ] Cross-tenant access attempt is rejected and logged to audit buffer
- [ ] Tenant export produces a valid, decryptable archive with correct Ed25519 signature

---

*Previous Phase: [Phase 13 — The Architect](./phase_13_architect.md)*
*Return to: [Master Plan](./plan.md)*

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_rpg/frappe_rpg/docs/phase_15_forge_master.md
# ⚒️ Phase 15: The Forge Master — Quality, Reliability & Production Certification

> **Status:** ⬜ Planned
> **Focus:** Production certification of the entire Frappe-RPG stack — property-based testing, load harness, chaos engineering, contract validation, and mutation testing
> **Prerequisites:** All previous phases — this phase validates the complete system

---

## 🎯 Phase Objective

With 14 phases of infrastructure built, Phase 15 answers the hardest question: **"Can we prove it works correctly at scale, under failure, for real?"**

Phase 15 is the certification layer. It does not add new features — it proves the existing ones are production-worthy using rigorous engineering techniques, most implemented in Rust and Go.

---

## 📋 Tasks

### 🦀 15.1 — Property-Based Testing Framework (`proptest`)

> **Goal:** Instead of writing individual unit tests, use `proptest` to generate thousands of randomised inputs and prove mathematical properties hold for all possible inputs.

#### Rust (`tests/property/`)
- [ ] **Crypto properties**: For any key K and plaintext P: `decrypt(encrypt(P, K), K) == P` — proved over 10,000 random k/P combinations
- [ ] **Auditor properties**: For any set of balanced journal entries: `verify_trial_balance()` must return `valid=true`; for any unbalanced set, must return `valid=false`
- [ ] **Cache properties**: For any sequence of `put`/`get`/`invalidate` ops: cache must never return a value written after its TTL
- [ ] **Rate limiter properties**: For any sequence of N requests within window W, if N > limit then at least one must be rejected; if N ≤ limit then none may be rejected
- [ ] **Log parser properties**: `parse_frappe_errors(log)` must never panic on any input — fuzz-tested with random bytes

---

### 🏹 15.2 — Load Testing Harness (Go)

> **Goal:** A built-in `frappe-rpg loadtest` command that produces latency percentile reports without needing external tools like k6 or Locust.

#### Go (`go/loadtest/`)
- [ ] **HTTP worker pool**: N goroutines sending requests at configured rate (RPS) to a target Frappe site
- [ ] **Scenario definitions** (YAML): Define multi-step flows — login → list view → open doc → save — with realistic think time
- [ ] **Metrics collector**: Tracks p50/p90/p99/p999 latency, error rate, throughput, Gunicorn worker saturation
- [ ] **Comparison mode**: Run before and after a code change, generate a diff report — "p99 improved from 1.2s to 380ms"
- [ ] **Phase 9 validation**: Benchmark with/without AetherProxy to prove the caching gains are real

#### CLI
```bash
frappe-rpg loadtest \
  --site staging.mysite.local \
  --scenario scenarios/sales_order_flow.yaml \
  --rps 100 \
  --duration 60s \
  --report results/
```

---

### 🏹 15.3 — Chaos Engineering Module

> **Goal:** Prove that Phase 5 (Observatory) and Phase 10 (Oracle) detect and recover from failures automatically by intentionally breaking things in a controlled way.

#### Go (`go/chaos/`)
- [ ] **`chaos redis-kill`** — temporarily stop Redis and verify Go sidecar reconnects gracefully
- [ ] **`chaos mariadb-slow`** — inject query latency (p99 → 5s) and verify Phase 10 anomaly detector fires within 30s
- [ ] **`chaos worker-kill`** — kill random Gunicorn workers and verify Phase 6 dashboard detects reduced capacity
- [ ] **`chaos disk-fill`** — write a large temp file to bring disk to 95%, verify Phase 10 capacity alarm fires
- [ ] **`chaos network-partition`** — block traffic between Go sidecar and Redis for 10s, verify reconnection
- [ ] **Auto-recovery verification**: Each chaos test checks that the system self-heals within a defined SLO window

---

### 🦀 15.4 — Contract Testing (PyO3 API Verification)

> **Goal:** Prove at compile time + test time that every `frappe_rs.*` function's Python-visible output schema matches its declared type — no silent type drift.

#### Rust (`tests/contracts/`)
- [ ] **Schema manifest**: JSON file defining the expected Python type signature of every public `frappe_rs` function
- [ ] **`verify_contract(fn_name, sample_inputs)`** — calls the function with realistic inputs, validates the output JSON structure matches the declared schema
- [ ] **Build-time check**: `cargo test --test contracts` runs as part of CI/CD; fails the build if any output schema breaks
- [ ] **Python stub generator**: Produces `.pyi` type stub files from the contract manifest automatically (Phase 8 SDK integration)

---

### 🦀 15.5 — Mutation Testing

> **Goal:** Prove that the test suite actually catches bugs — not just passes vacuously. Mutation testing introduces artificial bugs and verifies the tests fail.

#### Rust (`cargo-mutants`)
- [ ] **Run `cargo mutants`** on core modules: `auditor.rs`, `log_parser.rs`, `rate_limiter.rs`, `anomaly.rs`
- [ ] **Target mutation score > 85%**: At least 85% of artificial bugs must be caught by the test suite
- [ ] **Mutation report**: Published as a Frappe Workspace page showing which code paths lack meaningful test coverage
- [ ] **CI gate**: Build fails if mutation score drops below the threshold

---

### 🏹 15.6 — Production Readiness Checklist (Go CLI)

> **Goal:** A single command that audits a Frappe installation for production readiness across all Frappe-RPG phases.

#### CLI: `frappe-rpg audit production`

```
✅ Phase 1: frappe_rs installed and importable
✅ Phase 2: Argon2 working, AES-GCM working
✅ Phase 4: Ed25519 key present, Sandbox operational
✅ Phase 5: Go sidecar responding, /metrics reachable
✅ Phase 7: Redis Sentinel detected
⚠️  Phase 9: AetherProxy not running (optional)
✅ Phase 10: EWMA baseline populated (7 days of data)
✅ Phase 12: No long-lived API keys found for production users
⚠️  Phase 13: DynamoDB not configured (optional)
✅ Phase 14: Tenant quotas configured for all sites

Production Readiness: 8/10 — GOOD (2 optional items not configured)
```

---

### ✅ 15.7 — Phase 15 Validation

- [ ] `proptest` crypto suite passes 10,000+ random input combinations with zero failures
- [ ] Load harness successfully simulates 100 concurrent users against staging for 60 seconds
- [ ] All 5 chaos scenarios trigger correct Oracle alarms within SLO windows
- [ ] Contract tests catch an intentionally introduced schema-breaking change in `frappe_rs`
- [ ] Mutation score for `auditor.rs` > 85%
- [ ] Production readiness checklist correctly identifies a misconfigured site

---

## 🏆 The Complete Frappe-RPG Stack (All 15 Phases)

```
Phase 1-2:  Foundation — Python-Rust bridge, math, crypto, system metrics
Phase 3:    Performance — Polars analytics, Go gRPC, CDC, MicroVMs
Phase 4:    Security — Sandbox, Auditor, Tokens, Vault, Audit trail
Phase 5:    Observatory — Log parsing, Metrics, Webhook events
Phase 6:    Andromeda — Live Frappe dashboard
Phase 7:    Nexus — Rate limiting, tracing, clustering
Phase 8:    Arsenal — SDK, CLI, scaffolding
Phase 9:    Accelerator — SQL cache, Go proxy, WASM frontend
Phase 10:   Oracle — Statistical intelligence (EWMA, Drain, Holt-Winters)
Phase 11:   Relay — GraphQL, gRPC, MQTT, Kafka
Phase 12:   Keymaster — WebAuthn, ephemeral tokens, HSM, secrets
Phase 13:   Architect — DynamoDB, Parquet, DuckDB, S3, schema evolution
Phase 14:   Quartermaster — Multi-tenancy, quotas, metering, GDPR export
Phase 15:   Forge Master — Property tests, load harness, chaos, contracts
```

**The result: A Frappe installation that is measurably faster, provably correct, observably healthy, and production-certified at every layer.**

---

# Phase 16 — The Pipeline: CI/CD Automation

> **Status:** ✅ Complete
> **Rust module:** `rust/src/pipeline.rs`
> **Workflows:** `.github/workflows/rust.yml`, `build.yml`, `release.yml`
> **Tests:** `tests/test_phase16_pipeline.py`

---

## Overview

Phase 16 adds a production-grade CI/CD pipeline to frappe_rpg:

- **Multi-platform wheel builds** — manylinux 2.17 + macOS, x86_64 + arm64
- **Go binary build matrix** — all 6 sidecars × 4 platform combinations
- **pytest in CI** — runs all phase tests against the compiled wheel, no Frappe context needed
- **Automated GitHub Releases** — OIDC-authenticated PyPI publish + GitHub Release assets
- **Rust `pipeline` module** — CI validation utilities accessible from Python

---

## Rust API

```python
import frappe_rs as rs

# Build target matrix
targets = json.loads(rs.pipeline_matrix_targets())
# {"rust_wheels": [...], "go_binaries": [...], "python_versions": [...]}

# Validate Cargo.toml
with open("rust/Cargo.toml") as f:
    content = f.read()
report = json.loads(rs.pipeline_check_cargo(content))
# {"valid": true, "errors": [], "warnings": ["..."]}

# CI environment snapshot
env = json.loads(rs.pipeline_validate_env())
# {"environment": "local", "all_checks_pass": false, "checks": [...]}

# Build summary report
report = json.loads(rs.pipeline_build_report(
    phase_count=19,
    test_count=415,
    rust_fns=400,
    go_binaries=6,
    wheel_size_kb=4096,
))
# {"project": "frappe_rpg", "status": "passing", "badge": "![tests](...)", ...}

# Parse wheel filename (PEP 427)
info = json.loads(rs.pipeline_parse_wheel_name(
    "frappe_rs-0.15.0-cp310-cp310-manylinux_2_17_x86_64.whl"
))
# {"valid": true, "distribution": "frappe_rs", "version": "0.15.0", ...}

# Generate release notes markdown
notes = json.loads(rs.pipeline_generate_release_notes(json.dumps({
    "16": "The Pipeline",
    "17": "The Integrator",
})))
# {"markdown": "## What's New\n...", "total_new": 2}

# Validate GitHub Actions workflow YAML
result = json.loads(rs.pipeline_validate_workflow(yaml_string))
# {"valid": true, "step_count": 12, "warnings": []}
```

---

## GitHub Actions Workflows

### `rust.yml` — Rust Quality Gates

Triggers on changes to `rust/**`.

| Step | Tool | Purpose |
|------|------|---------|
| `cargo fmt --check` | rustfmt | Formatting enforcement |
| `cargo clippy` | Clippy | Lint warnings as errors |
| `cargo test` | Built-in | Unit tests |
| `maturin build --release` | maturin | Wheel smoke-test |

### `build.yml` — Cross-Platform Build Matrix

| Target | OS | Output |
|--------|----|--------|
| `x86_64-unknown-linux-musl` | ubuntu-22.04 | `frappe_rs-*-manylinux_2_17_x86_64.whl` |
| `aarch64-unknown-linux-musl` | ubuntu-22.04 | `frappe_rs-*-manylinux_2_17_aarch64.whl` |
| `x86_64-apple-darwin` | macos-14 | `frappe_rs-*-macosx_*_x86_64.whl` |
| `aarch64-apple-darwin` | macos-14 | `frappe_rs-*-macosx_*_arm64.whl` |

Go binaries: all 6 sidecars × `linux/amd64`, `linux/arm64`, `darwin/amd64`, `darwin/arm64`.

After wheel build, `pytest` runs all phase tests against the compiled artifact.

### `release.yml` — Automated GitHub Release + PyPI Publish

Triggered on `v*.*.*` tags.

1. Calls `build.yml` as a reusable workflow
2. Bundles wheels + Go tarballs as GitHub Release assets
3. Publishes wheels to PyPI via OIDC Trusted Publishing (no secrets needed)

---

## Rust Toolchain Pinning

`rust-toolchain.toml` at the repo root pins the stable channel and installs `rustfmt` + `clippy` with all cross-compilation targets pre-registered.

---

## Local Usage

```bash
# Check Cargo.toml manually
frappe-rpg pipeline check-cargo

# Run the full build matrix locally (Docker required for manylinux)
maturin build --release --strip

# Build all Go binaries for current platform
cd apps/frappe_rpg/go
go build -o sidecar/sidecar_bin ./sidecar/...
go build -o relay/relay_bin    ./relay/...
go build -o keymaster/keymaster_bin ./keymaster/...
go build -o s3proxy/s3proxy_bin    ./s3proxy/...
go build -o loadtest/loadtest_bin  ./loadtest/...
go build -o metrics_aggregator/metrics_aggregator_bin ./metrics_aggregator/...
```

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_rpg/frappe_rpg/docs/phase_17_integrator.md
# Phase 17 — The Integrator: Real Frappe Integration

> **Status:** ✅ Complete
> **Rust module:** `rust/src/integrator.rs`
> **Python:** `frappe_rpg/integrations/`, `frappe_rpg/tasks.py`
> **Tests:** `tests/test_phase17_integrator.py`

---

## Overview

Phase 17 wires frappe_rpg into the live Frappe document lifecycle:

- **Cache bridge** — transparent drop-in for `frappe.cache()` backed by the Rust moka LRU (Phase 9), with Redis fallback
- **DocType hooks** — `after_insert`, `on_update`, `after_load`, `on_trash` automatically cache/evict documents
- **Benchmark recorder** — every Frappe operation duration recorded in the Rust `benchmark_store` (DashMap)
- **Filter → SQL** — converts Frappe's `[doctype, field, op, value]` filter arrays to safe parameterised SQL
- **Scheduler tasks** — hourly anomaly scan, daily quota report, daily cache prune, weekly audit report

---

## Hooks Configuration

Add to `hooks.py`:

```python
doc_events = {
    "*": {
        "after_insert": "frappe_rpg.integrations.doctype_hooks.after_insert",
        "on_update":    "frappe_rpg.integrations.doctype_hooks.on_update",
        "after_load":   "frappe_rpg.integrations.doctype_hooks.after_load",
        "on_trash":     "frappe_rpg.integrations.doctype_hooks.on_trash",
    }
}

before_request = ["frappe_rpg.integrations.doctype_hooks.before_request"]
after_request  = ["frappe_rpg.integrations.doctype_hooks.after_request"]

scheduler_events = {
    "hourly": ["frappe_rpg.tasks.hourly_anomaly_scan"],
    "daily":  [
        "frappe_rpg.tasks.daily_quota_report",
        "frappe_rpg.tasks.daily_cache_prune",
    ],
    "weekly": ["frappe_rpg.tasks.weekly_audit_report"],
}
```

---

## Rust API

```python
import frappe_rs as rs, json

# Cache key generation
key = rs.doctype_cache_key("Sales Invoice", "SINV-001")
# → "frappe:doc:Sales_Invoice:SINV-001"

# Batch cache keys
pairs = [{"doctype": "Customer", "name": "Acme"}, ...]
data  = json.loads(rs.batch_cache_keys(json.dumps(pairs)))

# Convert Frappe filters to parameterised SQL
filters = [["Sales Invoice", "status", "=", "Paid"]]
sql     = json.loads(rs.frappe_filter_to_sql(json.dumps(filters)))
# {"where_clause": "`status` = ?", "params": ["Paid"]}

# DocType name validation
rs.validate_doctype_name("SINV-001")   # True
rs.validate_doctype_name("'; DROP TABLE--")  # False

# Live benchmark recording
rs.benchmark_frappe_op("get_doc:Sales Invoice", 12500)
stats = json.loads(rs.get_benchmark_stats("get_doc:Sales Invoice"))
# {"count": 1, "avg_us": 12500, "min_us": 12500, "max_us": 12500}

snapshot = json.loads(rs.benchmark_snapshot())
rs.benchmark_reset()

# Validate doc_events config
config = {"Sales Invoice": {"on_submit": "myapp.hooks.on_submit"}}
result = json.loads(rs.validate_doc_events(json.dumps(config)))
# {"valid": true, "hook_count": 1}

# Index hint for a filter set
hints = json.loads(rs.doctype_index_hint("Sales Invoice", json.dumps(filters)))
print(hints["index_sql"])
# ALTER TABLE `tabSalesInvoice` ADD INDEX `idx_rpg_status` (`status`);

# Scheduler task manifest
tasks = json.loads(rs.scheduler_task_manifest())
```

---

## Cache Bridge

```python
from frappe_rpg.integrations.cache_bridge import get_cache

cache = get_cache()

# Store a document
cache.set_value("my_key", {"data": 123}, expires_in_sec=300)

# Retrieve (Rust LRU first, Redis fallback)
val = cache.get_value("my_key")

# Delete
cache.delete_key("my_key")

# Using doctype/name helpers (auto-generates canonical key)
cache.set_value("", value=doc.as_dict(), doctype="Customer", name="Acme")
val = cache.get_value("", doctype="Customer", name="Acme")
```

**Performance:** Rust LRU warm reads  500ms |
| `daily_quota_report` | daily | Flush metering counters, log per-tenant usage |
| `daily_cache_prune` | daily | Evict idle rate-limit buckets (inactive > 5 min) |
| `weekly_audit_report` | weekly | Run Phase 15 production-readiness audit |

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_rpg/frappe_rpg/docs/phase_18_watcher.md
# Phase 18 — The Watcher: Observability Layer

> **Status:** ✅ Complete
> **Rust module:** `rust/src/watcher.rs`
> **Go binary:** `go/metrics_aggregator/` — port `:7078`
> **Tests:** `tests/test_phase18_watcher.py`

---

## Overview

Phase 18 wires every frappe_rpg Go sidecar into a unified Prometheus/Grafana observability stack:

- **Prometheus format emitter** — converts any JSON metrics dict to Prometheus text exposition
- **Go `metrics_aggregator`** — scrapes all 5 sidecars every 15s, exposes a single `/metrics` endpoint
- **Grafana dashboard generator** — produces ready-to-import `dashboard.json` from config
- **Pre-built alert rules** — CPU, memory, error rate, and per-tenant quota-breach alerts

---

## Architecture

```
Prometheus ──scrape──► :7078/metrics  (metrics_aggregator)
                            │
                     ┌──────┴──────────────────────────────┐
                     │ scrape every 15s                      │
               :7070/metrics  :7071/metrics  :7075/metrics  :7076/metrics  :7077/metrics
               (sidecar)      (relay)        (keymaster)    (s3proxy)      (loadtest)
```

---

## Go Sidecar: `metrics_aggregator`

```bash
# Start
AGGREGATOR_PORT=7078 SCRAPE_INTERVAL=15 ./go/metrics_aggregator/metrics_aggregator_bin

# Endpoints
GET :7078/health     # sidecar health summary
GET :7078/metrics    # Prometheus text format (all sidecars)
GET :7078/dashboard  # JSON snapshot for frontend
```

| Env Var | Default | Description |
|---------|---------|-------------|
| `AGGREGATOR_PORT` | 7078 | HTTP port |
| `SCRAPE_INTERVAL` | 15 | Seconds between background scrapes |
| `SIDECAR_PORT` | 7070 | Scout sidecar port |
| `RELAY_PORT` | 7071 | Relay sidecar port |
| `KEYMASTER_PORT` | 7075 | Keymaster port |
| `S3PROXY_PORT` | 7076 | S3 proxy port |
| `LOADTEST_PORT` | 7077 | Load test harness port |

---

## Rust API

```python
import frappe_rs as rs, json

# Convert JSON metrics dict → Prometheus text
metrics = {"cpu_percent": 42.5, "memory_mb": 1024, "error_rate": 0.02}
prom    = rs.prometheus_format(json.dumps(metrics))

# Individual metric lines
rs.prometheus_counter("requests_total", '{"app":"frappe","env":"prod"}', 12345.0)
rs.prometheus_gauge("cpu_usage",        '{"host":"srv01"}',              78.3)
rs.prometheus_histogram(
    "request_duration_seconds",
    '{"handler":"/api/method"}',
    json.dumps([
        {"le": 0.005, "count": 100},
        {"le": 0.01,  "count": 200},
        {"le": "+Inf","count": 250},
    ])
)

# Grafana dashboard
dashboard = json.loads(rs.grafana_dashboard(json.dumps({
    "title":      "Frappe-RPG Production",
    "uid":        "frappe-rpg-prod",
    "datasource": "Prometheus",
})))
# Save to grafana/provisioning/dashboards/frappe_rpg.json

# Single panel
panel = json.loads(rs.grafana_panel("timeseries", "rate(requests_total[1m])", "RPS"))

# Alert rules
rs.alert_rule_cpu(85.0)                      # warn when CPU > 85%
rs.alert_rule_memory(4096)                   # warn when RSS > 4GB
rs.alert_rule_error_rate(0.05, 60)           # critical when 5% error rate
rs.alert_rule_quota_breach("tenant_acme")    # warn when quota near zero

# Custom alert
rule = json.loads(rs.grafana_alert_rule(json.dumps({
    "name":     "HighQueueDepth",
    "expr":     "frappe_rpg_wfq_depth > 1000",
    "summary":  "WFQ queue depth exceeds 1000 jobs",
    "severity": "warning",
    "for":      "3m",
})))

# Aggregate multiple sidecar metric payloads
sources = [
    {"source": "sidecar",   "metrics": {"cpu": 40.0, "mem": 512.0}},
    {"source": "keymaster", "metrics": {"requests": 100.0}},
]
agg = json.loads(rs.metrics_aggregate(json.dumps(sources)))
# {"sources": ["sidecar","keymaster"], "metrics": {"sidecar_cpu": 40, ...}}
```

---

```yaml
scrape_configs:
  - job_name: frappe_rpg
    static_configs:
      - targets: ['localhost:7078']
    scrape_interval: 15s
```

# ⚔️ Phase 1: The Forge

> **Status:** 🟡 In Progress
> **Focus:** Environment Setup & First Rust Function in Frappe
> **Goal:** Prove the PyO3 bridge works end-to-end inside a real Frappe app

---

## 🎯 Phase Objective

Build the iron foundation of Frappe-RPG. By the end of Phase 1, a Rust function must be callable directly from the Frappe Python console. This validates the entire build pipeline and sets the stage for all future work.

---

## 📋 Tasks

### 🔧 1.1 — Environment Setup

- [ ] Install Rust toolchain (`rustup`) inside the bench environment
- [ ] Install `maturin` via pip in the Frappe venv
  ```bash
  pip install maturin
  ```
- [ ] Verify `maturin` is accessible from the bench Python
  ```bash
  maturin --version
  ```
- [ ] Confirm Rust `cargo` is available
  ```bash
  cargo --version
  ```

---

### 📁 1.2 — Project Scaffold

- [ ] Create `rust/` directory inside `frappe_rpg/`
- [ ] Initialize Rust library project
  ```bash
  cd apps/frappe_rpg/frappe_rpg/rust
  cargo init --lib .
  ```
- [ ] Create `pyproject.toml` in `rust/` for maturin
- [ ] Configure `Cargo.toml` with PyO3 dependency:
  ```toml
  [dependencies]
  pyo3 = { version = "0.21", features = ["extension-module"] }
  
  [lib]
  name = "frappe_rs"
  crate-type = ["cdylib"]
  ```

---

### 🦀 1.3 — First Rust Function: `fast_sum`

- [ ] Write the first Rust function in `rust/src/lib.rs`:
  ```rust
  use pyo3::prelude::*;

  /// Adds two integers at native speed.
  #[pyfunction]
  fn fast_sum(a: i64, b: i64) -> i64 {
      a + b
  }

  #[pymodule]
  fn frappe_rs(_py: Python, m: &PyModule) -> PyResult {
      m.add_function(wrap_pyfunction!(fast_sum, m)?)?;
      Ok(())
  }
  ```
- [ ] Build the extension with maturin:
  ```bash
  cd apps/frappe_rpg/frappe_rpg/rust
  maturin develop
  ```
- [ ] Fix any build errors
- [ ] Verify the `.so` file is created in site-packages

---

### 🐍 1.4 — Python Bridge

- [ ] Create `frappe_rpg/api.py` with the Python wrapper:
  ```python
  import frappe
  from frappe_rs import fast_sum

  @frappe.whitelist()
  def compute_sum(a: int, b: int) -> dict:
      result = fast_sum(int(a), int(b))
      return {"result": result, "engine": "Rust via PyO3"}
  ```
- [ ] Register the API in hooks if needed
- [ ] Test via Frappe bench console:
  ```python
  import frappe
  frappe.init(site="your.site")
  frappe.connect()

  print(fast_sum(5, 7))  # Expected: 12
  ```

- [ ] Initialize Go module:
  ```bash
  cd apps/frappe_rpg/frappe_rpg/go
  go mod init frappe_rpg_sidecar
  ```
- [ ] Create `go/sidecar/main.go` with basic HTTP health endpoint:
  ```go
  package main

      "fmt"

  )

      http.HandleFunc("/health", healthHandler)
      fmt.Println("Scout sidecar running on :7070")
      http.ListenAndServe(":7070", nil)

  ```
- [ ] Verify Go sidecar builds and runs
  ```bash
  go run go/sidecar/main.go
  ```
