- [ ] `bulk_fetch_plan(doc_json) -> str`
- [ ] `bulk_fetch_parallel(fetches_json, db_results_json) -> str`
- [ ] `bulk_cache_check(fetches_json) -> str`
- [ ] `bulk_response_assemble(docs_json, fields_map_json) -> str`
- [ ] `bulk_diff_fields(old_doc_json, new_doc_json) -> str`
- [ ] `bulk_validate_links(doc_json, link_meta_json) -> str`
- [ ] `frappe_rpg.api.bulk_get` whitelist endpoint
- [ ] `frappe_rpg.api.diff_save` whitelist endpoint
- [ ] Frontend JS snippet (optional): intercept Frappe's `frappe.model.with_doc` calls
- [ ] Tests: cache hit path, miss path, diff correctness, link validation
- [ ] Benchmark: N individual calls vs bulk (target: 10× improvement)
- [ ] Stubs + lib.rs registration
- [ ] Update `tracker.md`

---

## Expected Performance

| Scenario | Before | After |
|----------|--------|-------|
| Form load (20 linked docs) | ~160ms (serial) | ~15ms (parallel + cache) |
| List view link resolution | ~500ms for 100 rows | ~40ms |
| Document save (large form) | Full UPDATE all fields | Only changed fields |
| Link validation on save | ~80ms Python loop | ~2ms Rust parallel |

---

## Notes

- `bulk_diff_fields` only compares scalar and string fields by default; child table diffs are returned as-is
- `bulk_fetch_plan` uses DocType meta (Phase 30 cache) to know which fields are Link type
- Cache hits from Phase 9 doc cache avoid DB entirely
- Works incrementally: can call `bulk_get` for partial sets, not all-or-nothing

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_rpg/frappe_rpg/docs/phase_34_write_optimizer.md
# Phase 34 — The Write Optimizer

> **Theme:** Three complementary write-path optimizations — write-behind caching for
> non-critical updates, bulk write batching for INSERT storms, and prepared statement
> caching for repeated query patterns.

---

## Overview

| Item | Detail |
|------|--------|
| **Module** | `rust/src/write_optimizer.rs` |
| **Phase** | 34 |
| **Port** | In-process (no sidecar) |
| **Dependencies** | `serde_json`, `moka`, existing `doc_cache` |
| **Test file** | `tests/test_phase34_write_optimizer.py` |

---

## Problem

Three separate write bottlenecks:

**1. Non-critical synchronous writes** — every page view updates `tabDocShare`,
`tabActivity Log`, `tabView Log`, and `tabUser` (last_active). These are low-value
writes happening on the hot request path, holding DB connections for 3–8ms each.

**2. INSERT storms** — Bulk import of 10,000 Stock Ledger Entries does 10,000
individual `INSERT` statements. MariaDB can do one 10,000-row multi-INSERT in the
same time.

**3. Prepared statement overhead** — Frappe rebuilds SQL strings on every request.
MariaDB must parse the same `SELECT name, ... FROM tabSales Invoice WHERE ...`
thousands of times per minute.

---

## Design

### 1. Write-Behind Cache

Buffer low-priority writes in Rust memory, flush to DB every 5 seconds via a
background Python task.

```
write_behind_enqueue(table: str, record_json: str, mode: str) -> str
    # mode: "insert" | "update" | "upsert"
    # Returns: {"queued": true, "queue_depth": N}

write_behind_flush(table: str) -> str
    # Return all queued writes for a table as a bulk payload
    # Called by background task every 5s
    # Returns: {"rows": [...], "count": N, "mode": "insert"|"mixed"}

write_behind_stats() -> str
    # Queue depths, flush counts, oldest pending write age

write_behind_clear(table: str) -> str
    # Emergency: discard all queued writes for a table

write_behind_pending_tables() -> str
    # List tables with pending writes
```

**Suitable tables:** `tabActivity Log`, `tabView Log`, `tabAccess Log`,
`tabError Log` (append-only), user `last_active` timestamp updates.

**NOT suitable for:** DocType saves, financial transactions, anything requiring
immediate consistency.

### 2. Bulk Write Batcher

```
batch_write_init(table: str, columns_json: str) -> str
    # Start a new batch for a table with column spec
    # Returns: {"batch_id": "uuid"}

batch_write_add(batch_id: str, row_json: str) -> str
    # Add one row to the batch
    # Returns: {"rows": N, "batch_id": batch_id}

batch_write_compile(batch_id: str) -> str
    # Compile into a single multi-row INSERT statement
    # Returns: {"sql": "INSERT INTO ... VALUES (),(),()", "rows": N}

batch_write_stats(batch_id: str) -> str

batch_write_discard(batch_id: str) -> str
```

**Python integration:**

```python
# frappe_rpg/integrations/batch_writer.py

class RustBatchWriter:
    def __init__(self, doctype, batch_size=500):
        self.doctype = doctype
        self.batch_size = batch_size
        self._batch_id = None

    def __enter__(self):
        cols = json.dumps(frappe.get_meta(self.doctype).get_fieldnames())
        result = json.loads(_rs.batch_write_init(f"tab{self.doctype}", cols))
        self._batch_id = result["batch_id"]
        return self

    def add(self, doc_dict):
        _rs.batch_write_add(self._batch_id, json.dumps(doc_dict))

    def __exit__(self, *args):
        compiled = json.loads(_rs.batch_write_compile(self._batch_id))
        frappe.db.sql(compiled["sql"])  # single multi-row INSERT
```

### 3. Prepared Statement Cache

```
stmt_register(key: str, sql_template: str) -> str
    # Register a parameterized SQL template by key
    # Returns: {"registered": true}

stmt_bind(key: str, params_json: str) -> str
    # Bind params to registered template — returns final SQL
    # Returns: {"sql": "SELECT ... WHERE name = 'CUST-001'"}

stmt_cache_stats() -> str
    # Hit rate, registered count, most-used statements

stmt_invalidate(key: str) -> str

stmt_list() -> str
    # All registered statement keys
```

**Python integration:**

```python
# frappe_rpg/integrations/db_hook.py

# Pre-register common Frappe query patterns on startup
_COMMON_STMTS = {
    "get_doc":   "SELECT * FROM `tab{doctype}` WHERE `name` = ?",
    "get_list":  "SELECT {fields} FROM `tab{doctype}` WHERE {filters} LIMIT ? OFFSET ?",
    "get_value": "SELECT {fieldname} FROM `tab{doctype}` WHERE `name` = ?",
}
```

---

## Tasks

- [ ] `write_behind_enqueue`, `write_behind_flush`, `write_behind_stats`
- [ ] `write_behind_clear`, `write_behind_pending_tables`
- [ ] `batch_write_init`, `batch_write_add`, `batch_write_compile`
- [ ] `batch_write_stats`, `batch_write_discard`
- [ ] `stmt_register`, `stmt_bind`, `stmt_cache_stats`, `stmt_invalidate`, `stmt_list`
- [ ] `batch_writer.py` context manager
- [ ] Write-behind flush task in `tasks.py` (every 5s)
- [ ] `db_hook.py` stmt pre-registration on startup
- [ ] Tests: write-behind queue/flush, batch compile correctness, stmt cache hit
- [ ] Tests: activity log path uses write-behind (integration)
- [ ] Stubs + lib.rs registration
- [ ] Update `tracker.md`

---

## Expected Performance

| Scenario | Before | After |
|----------|--------|-------|
| Activity log write (per request) | ~4ms blocking | 0ms (deferred 5s) |
| Bulk import 10,000 rows | ~45s (10k INSERTs) | ~4s (20× batched INSERTs) |
| Repeated query parse overhead | ~0.5ms per query | ~0.05ms (cached template) |
| `last_active` update overhead | ~3ms per request | 0ms (write-behind) |

---

## Notes

- Write-behind flush is atomic: if flush fails, rows remain in queue and retry next cycle
- Batch writer respects DocType child table structure — handles `parenttype`, `parentfield`, `idx`
- Stmt cache uses FNV-1a hashing (same as Phase 20 query cache) — O(1) lookup
- Write-behind queue has a max depth of 50,000 rows; beyond that, falls back to synchronous write

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_rpg/frappe_rpg/docs/phase_35_static_server.md
# Phase 35 — The Static Server

> **Theme:** Serve all of Frappe's static files (JS, CSS, images, private files)
> from a Go HTTP/2 server with sendfile, Brotli, pre-computed ETags, and
> range request support — completely off Gunicorn workers.

---

## Overview

| Item | Detail |
|------|--------|
| **Binary** | `go/static/main.go` |
| **Phase** | 35 |
| **Port** | `:7088` |
| **Protocols** | HTTP/1.1 + HTTP/2, gzip + brotli |
| **Test file** | `tests/test_phase35_static_server.py` |

---

## Problem

Frappe's static file serving chain:

```
Browser → Nginx → Gunicorn worker → Python file handler
                                     ↑
                         Burns a worker process for raw byte serving.
                         Python workers are the scarcest resource.
```

For private files (`/private/files/`), Frappe adds permission checks — but these
run synchronously in Python, blocking the worker.

Public assets (`/assets/`, `/files/`) have no auth but still hit Nginx → Python
for anything not in Nginx's static root.

A Go server handles 50,000+ static requests/second per core with microsecond latency,
freeing every Gunicorn worker for actual Python work.

---

## Design

```
┌──────────┐    /:7088/assets/     ┌────────────────────────────────┐
│  Nginx   │ ──────────────────→  │  Go Static Server :7088        │
│          │    /:7088/files/      │                                │
│          │    /:7088/private/    │  • sendfile() syscall          │
└──────────┘                       │  • HTTP/2 push hints           │
                                   │  • Brotli (pre-computed .br)   │
                                   │  • ETag + If-None-Match        │
                                   │  • Range requests (video/PDF)  │
                                   │  • Private file auth token     │
                                   │  • /health  /metrics           │
                                   └────────────────────────────────┘
```

### Endpoints

```
GET  /assets/{path}         → Serve from apps/*/public/
GET  /files/{path}          → Serve public uploads (site/public/files/)
GET  /private/{path}        → Serve private uploads (site/private/files/)
                              Requires X-Frappe-Auth-Token header
GET  /health                → {"status": "ok", "files_served": N}
GET  /metrics               → Prometheus metrics
POST /cache/invalidate      → Purge ETag cache for a path
POST /cache/warm            → Pre-load dir listing + ETags
```

### Private file auth

```go
// Validate token via Rust permission engine (Phase 29) or Frappe session API
// Flow:
// 1. Extract X-Frappe-Auth-Token from request
// 2. POST to Frappe /api/method/frappe_rpg.api.validate_file_access
// 3. Cache result for 60s (token + file path → allowed bool)
// 4. Serve or 403
```

### Brotli serving

```go
// For each request to .js / .css:
// 1. Check if Accept-Encoding: br
// 2. If yes, look for {path}.br (pre-computed by Phase 31)
// 3. Serve .br with Content-Encoding: br — zero CPU
// 4. Fallback: check for {path}.gz (Content-Encoding: gzip)
// 5. Final fallback: serve plain with on-the-fly gzip
```

### HTTP/2 Server Push

```go
// On GET /assets/frappe/frappe.min.js:
// Push /assets/frappe/frappe.min.css if the same bundle
// Configured via push_hints.json: {"path": ["dep1", "dep2"]}
```

### Config

```
STATIC_PORT=7088
ASSETS_ROOT=/path/to/frappe-bench/sites/assets
FILES_ROOT=/path/to/frappe-bench/sites/{site}/public/files
PRIVATE_ROOT=/path/to/frappe-bench/sites/{site}/private/files
FRAPPE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8000
AUTH_CACHE_TTL=60
PUSH_HINTS_FILE=push_hints.json
BROTLI_ENABLED=true
MAX_AGE=31536000
```

### Nginx config change

```nginx
# Before: Nginx serves /assets/ itself or proxies to Gunicorn
# After: proxy_pass to Go static server for all static paths
location /assets/ {
    proxy_pass http://127.0.0.1:7088;
}
location /files/ {
    proxy_pass http://127.0.0.1:7088;
}
location /private/ {
    proxy_pass http://127.0.0.1:7088;
    proxy_set_header X-Frappe-Auth-Token $cookie_sid;
}
```

---

## Tasks

- [ ] HTTP/2 server setup with TLS termination support
- [ ] `sendfile` path for large files (PDF, images, video)
- [ ] ETag generation: `sha256(file_mtime + size)` cached in memory
- [ ] `If-None-Match` / `If-Modified-Since` — 304 responses
- [ ] Range request support (`Content-Range`)
- [ ] Brotli pre-compressed serving (`.br` files from Phase 31)
- [ ] On-the-fly gzip fallback for non-pre-compressed files
- [ ] Private file auth token validation + 60s result cache
- [ ] HTTP/2 push hint loading from `push_hints.json`
- [ ] Directory listing disabled (403 on dir access)
- [ ] `CORS` headers for cross-origin asset requests
- [ ] Prometheus metrics: bytes_served, requests/sec, cache_hit_rate, p99_latency
- [ ] `/cache/invalidate` and `/cache/warm` management endpoints
- [ ] Tests: 200/304/404/403 responses, range requests, brotli serving, auth
- [ ] Nginx config snippet in `docs/`
- [ ] Update `tracker.md`

---

## Expected Performance

| Metric | Before (Nginx+Python) | After (Go) |
|--------|----------------------|------------|
| Static requests/sec | ~2,000 | ~50,000 |
| Time-to-first-byte (cached asset) | ~8ms | <0.2ms |
| Gunicorn workers freed | 0 | All static workers freed |
| Private file auth | ~15ms (Python) | <0.5ms (cached token) |
| 1MB JS file transfer | gzip ~320KB | brotli ~250KB |

---

## Notes

- Public files served with `Cache-Control: max-age=31536000, immutable` (fingerprinted assets)
- Private files: `Cache-Control: no-store` (never cache in browser/CDN)
- `push_hints.json` generated by a `bench build` hook that analyses bundle dependencies
- Compatible with CDN fronting — correct `Vary: Accept-Encoding` headers set

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_rpg/frappe_rpg/docs/phase_36_pricing_engine.md
# Phase 36 — The Pricing Engine

> **Theme:** ERPNext's pricing rule evaluation is the most complained-about
> performance bottleneck in production. Replace the Python loop over Pricing Rules
> with a Rust rule evaluator that compiles rules into an evaluation tree and
> resolves applicable prices in microseconds.

---

## Overview

| Item | Detail |
|------|--------|
| **Module** | `rust/src/pricing_engine.rs` |
| **Phase** | 36 |
| **Port** | In-process (no sidecar) |
| **Dependencies** | `serde_json`, `dashmap`, `moka` |
| **Test file** | `tests/test_phase36_pricing_engine.py` |

---

## Problem

ERPNext `get_pricing_rule_for_item` Python flow:

```python
# Called for EVERY line item on EVERY Sales Order/Invoice form interaction
rules = frappe.get_all("Pricing Rule", filters={...})   ← DB query
for rule in rules:                                        ← Python loop
    if not rule.applies_to_all_items:
        items = frappe.get_all("Pricing Rule Item Code", ...)  ← N+1 query
    if check_applies(rule, item, customer, qty, date):   ← nested conditions
        apply_rule(rule, item_doc)                       ← more Python
```

For an order with 20 items and 50 active pricing rules: **20 × 50 = 1,000 Python
condition evaluations** + hundreds of DB queries = **2–8 seconds** per form update.

---

## Design

### Rule compilation

Load all active Pricing Rules into Rust at startup. Each rule becomes a compiled
condition tree evaluated against an item context.

```
pricing_load_rules(rules_json: str) -> str
    # Load all Pricing Rules into Rust (called on startup + rule change)
    # Compiles condition trees, indexes by item/item_group/customer/customer_group
    # Returns: {"loaded": N, "indexed": {"by_item": N, "by_group": N}}

pricing_reload_rule(rule_name: str, rule_json: str) -> str
    # Hot-reload a single rule on save

pricing_remove_rule(rule_name: str) -> str
    # Remove on delete/disable

pricing_rule_count() -> str
    # {"total": N, "active": N, "by_type": {...}}
```

### Rule evaluation

```
pricing_get_item_price(context_json: str) -> str
    # context: {item_code, qty, uom, customer, customer_group, date,
    #           price_list, company, currency, transaction_type}
    # Returns: {"rate": float, "discount_pct": float, "rule_applied": str,
    #           "free_item": {...}|null, "pricing_rule": str}

pricing_get_item_prices_bulk(items_json: str, context_json: str) -> str
    # Evaluate pricing for N items in one call (Rayon parallel)
    # Returns: [{"item_code": str, "rate": float, ...}, ...]

pricing_explain(item_code: str, context_json: str) -> str
    # Debug: show all rules evaluated and why each was/wasn't applied
    # Returns: {"applied": rule, "evaluated": [{rule, reason_skipped},...]}

pricing_cache_stats() -> str
    # Rule cache stats: hit rate, last reload time
```

### Rule types supported

| Type | Description |
|------|-------------|
| `Percentage` | Discount % off price list rate |
| `Amount` | Fixed discount amount |
| `Rate` | Fixed selling rate |
| `Free Item` | Get N free on purchase of M |
| `Promotional Scheme` | Linked promotional scheme |

### Conditions evaluated in Rust

- Date range (valid_from / valid_upto)
- Minimum quantity / amount threshold
- Customer / Customer Group match
- Item / Item Group / Brand match
- Price list match
- Supplier / Supplier Group (for purchase)
- Apply on (Grand Total / Net Total / Item specific)
- Cumulative (requires previous transaction data — Python fallback)

### Python integration

```python
# frappe_rpg/integrations/pricing_hook.py
import frappe_rs as _rs

def get_pricing_rule_for_item(args, doc=None):
    """Drop-in replacement for ERPNext's get_pricing_rule_for_item."""
    ctx = {
        "item_code":       args.item_code,
        "qty":             flt(args.qty),
        "customer":        args.customer,
        "customer_group":  args.customer_group or "",
        "price_list":      args.price_list,
        "company":         args.company,
        "date":            str(args.transaction_date or today()),
        "currency":        args.currency or "",
        "transaction_type": args.get("transaction_type", "selling"),
    }
    result = json.loads(_rs.pricing_get_item_price(json.dumps(ctx)))
    if result.get("rule_applied"):
        return result
    # Fall back to ERPNext's Python implementation for cumulative rules
    from erpnext.accounts.doctype.pricing_rule.utils import get_pricing_rule_for_item as _orig
    return _orig(args, doc)
```

---

## Tasks

- [ ] `pricing_load_rules(rules_json) -> str`
- [ ] `pricing_reload_rule(rule_name, rule_json) -> str`
- [ ] `pricing_remove_rule(rule_name) -> str`
- [ ] `pricing_rule_count() -> str`
- [ ] `pricing_get_item_price(context_json) -> str`
- [ ] `pricing_get_item_prices_bulk(items_json, context_json) -> str`
- [ ] `pricing_explain(item_code, context_json) -> str`
- [ ] `pricing_cache_stats() -> str`
- [ ] Rule index structures: by item_code, by item_group, by customer, by customer_group
- [ ] Condition evaluator: date range, qty threshold, customer match, item match
- [ ] Discount computation: percentage, amount, fixed rate
- [ ] Free item rule resolution
- [ ] Python hook: `pricing_hook.py` with ERPNext fallback
- [ ] Wire invalidation on Pricing Rule `on_submit` / `on_cancel` / `on_trash`
- [ ] Startup task: `pricing_load_rules` with all active rules
- [ ] Tests: exact rate, discount %, free item, bulk pricing, explain output
- [ ] Benchmark: Python loop vs Rust (20 items, 50 rules — target: 95% |

---

## Notes

- Cumulative pricing rules (based on previous transaction totals) require DB access — kept in Python as fallback
- Rule priority (same as ERPNext): higher `priority` number wins
- Mixed rules (some Rust, some Python fallback) are merged in the Python hook
- `pricing_explain` is invaluable for debugging "why is this rule not applying?"

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_rpg/frappe_rpg/docs/phase_37_tax_engine.md
# Phase 37 — The Tax Engine

> **Theme:** GST, TDS, and multi-tax computation in Rust — deterministic, auditable,
> and 50× faster than ERPNext's Python tax cascade. Covers Indian GST (CGST/SGST/IGST/CESS),
> TDS/TCS, and generic multi-tier tax templates.

---

## Overview

| Item | Detail |
|------|--------|
| **Module** | `rust/src/tax_engine.rs` |
| **Phase** | 37 |
| **Port** | In-process (no sidecar) |
| **Dependencies** | `serde_json`, `rust_decimal` (exact decimal math) |
| **Test file** | `tests/test_phase37_tax_engine.py` |

---

## Problem

ERPNext's tax calculation for a Sales Invoice:

```python
# Runs on every item add/edit/qty change in the form
for tax in doc.taxes:                          ← Python loop
    if tax.charge_type == "On Net Total":
        tax.tax_amount = net_total * tax.rate / 100  ← float arithmetic (rounding errors!)
    elif tax.charge_type == "On Previous Row Amount":
        prev = doc.taxes[tax.row_id - 1]
        tax.tax_amount = prev.tax_amount * tax.rate / 100
    elif tax.charge_type == "On Previous Row Total":
        ...
    # Repeat for item-wise breakdown (N items × M taxes)
```

Issues: Python float precision → rounding errors in GST returns. Slow: N×M iterations.
No audit trail of how each tax was computed.

---

## Design

### Decimal precision

Uses `rust_decimal` crate (exact decimal, not float) — matches how Indian tax authorities
and Tally compute values. All monetary amounts are `Decimal` internally.

### API

```
tax_compute(doc_json: str, tax_template_json: str) -> str
    # Full tax computation for an invoice document
    # Returns: {"taxes": [...], "net_total": d, "total_taxes": d, "grand_total": d,
    #           "rounding_adjustment": d, "items": [{...item_taxes...}]}

tax_compute_item(item_json: str, taxes_json: str, net_total: str) -> str
    # Compute taxes for a single item row
    # Returns: {"item_taxes": {...charge_type: amount}, "item_total": d}

gst_classify(from_state: str, to_state: str, gstin: str) -> str
    # Determine CGST+SGST (intra-state) or IGST (inter-state)
    # Returns: {"type": "IGST"|"CGST_SGST", "rate_split": {...}}

gst_compute(taxable_amount: str, hsn_code: str, gst_rate: str,
            supply_type: str) -> str
    # Returns: {"cgst": d, "sgst": d, "igst": d, "cess": d, "total": d}

tds_compute(payment_amount: str, section: str, is_individual: bool,
            pan_available: bool) -> str
    # Returns: {"section": "194C", "rate": d, "tds_amount": d,
    #           "threshold": d, "applicable": bool}

tcs_compute(sales_amount: str, section: str, buyer_turnover: str) -> str

tax_round(amount: str, precision: int, method: str) -> str
    # method: "normal" | "up" | "down" | "banker" (round-half-to-even)
    # Returns: {"rounded": d, "rounding_adjustment": d}

tax_validate_gstin(gstin: str) -> str
    # Validate GSTIN checksum
    # Returns: {"valid": bool, "state_code": str, "pan": str, "entity_type": str}

tax_hsn_lookup(hsn_code: str) -> str
    # Returns GST rate for a known HSN code from embedded table
    # Returns: {"hsn": str, "description": str, "gst_rate": d}

tax_audit_trail(doc_json: str, tax_template_json: str) -> str
    # Full step-by-step computation trace (for auditors / GST returns)
```

### Tax charge types supported

| Charge Type | Description |
|-------------|-------------|
| `On Net Total` | % of net total |
| `On Previous Row Amount` | % of specific previous tax amount |
| `On Previous Row Total` | % of cumulative total at previous row |
| `On Item Quantity` | Fixed amount per unit quantity |
| `Actual` | Fixed amount (pass-through) |
| `On Net Total (Itemwise)` | Per-item net total (for GST item-wise) |

### Python integration

```python
# frappe_rpg/integrations/tax_hook.py

def calculate_taxes_and_totals(doc):
    """Override ERPNext's calculate_taxes_and_totals."""
    template = frappe.get_doc("Sales Taxes and Charges Template",
                               doc.taxes_and_charges).as_dict() if doc.taxes_and_charges else {}
    result = json.loads(_rs.tax_compute(
        frappe.as_json(doc.as_dict()),
        frappe.as_json(template)
    ))
    # Write back computed values
    doc.net_total        = result["net_total"]
    doc.total_taxes_and_charges = result["total_taxes"]
    doc.grand_total      = result["grand_total"]
    doc.rounding_adjustment = result["rounding_adjustment"]
    for i, tax in enumerate(doc.taxes):
        tax.tax_amount       = result["taxes"][i]["tax_amount"]
        tax.total            = result["taxes"][i]["total"]
        tax.tax_amount_after_discount_amount = result["taxes"][i]["tax_amount"]
```

- [ ] `tax_compute_item(item_json, taxes_json, net_total) -> str`
- [ ] `gst_classify(from_state, to_state, gstin) -> str`
- [ ] `gst_compute(taxable_amount, hsn_code, gst_rate, supply_type) -> str`
- [ ] `tds_compute(payment_amount, section, is_individual, pan_available) -> str`
- [ ] `tcs_compute(sales_amount, section, buyer_turnover) -> str`

|----------|--------|-------|

## Notes

- `rust_decimal` ensures paise-level accuracy — critical for GST filing
- Embedded HSN table covers common 4-digit and 8-digit codes; unknown codes fall back to template rate
- TDS section rates are hardcoded per Finance Act 2024–25 (update annually)
- `tax_audit_trail` output can be exported as a CSV line for GSTR-1 filing
- Direct value for TallyNext — Tally uses the same GST computation rules

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_rpg/frappe_rpg/docs/phase_38_state_machine.md
# Phase 38 — The State Machine Engine

> **Theme:** Frappe's docstatus transitions and workflow state changes are validated
> in Python after the fact. A Rust state machine validates transitions before the
> DB write, rejects illegal moves instantly, and tracks the full state history.

---

## Overview

| Item | Detail |
|------|--------|
| **Module** | `rust/src/state_machine.rs` |
| **Phase** | 38 |
| **Port** | In-process (no sidecar) |
| **Dependencies** | `serde_json`, `dashmap` |
| **Test file** | `tests/test_phase38_state_machine.py` |

---

## Problem

Frappe's docstatus machine: `Draft(0) → Submitted(1) → Cancelled(2)`

ERPNext's Workflow adds custom states on top:
- `Draft → Pending Approval → Approved → Submitted`
- But validation happens in Python at save time — illegal transitions can be attempted
  (and sometimes succeed due to race conditions or direct `db.set_value` bypasses)

No built-in state history. No transition guards that run before the DB write.
No way to declare: "only Finance Manager can approve, only from Pending Approval state."

---

## Design

### State machine definition

```json
{
  "doctype": "Purchase Order",
  "states": ["Draft", "Pending Approval", "Approved", "Rejected", "Submitted", "Cancelled"],
  "initial": "Draft",
  "transitions": [
    {"from": "Draft",            "to": "Pending Approval", "allowed_roles": ["Purchase User"],   "action": "submit_for_approval"},
    {"from": "Pending Approval", "to": "Approved",         "allowed_roles": ["Finance Manager"], "action": "approve"},
    {"from": "Pending Approval", "to": "Rejected",         "allowed_roles": ["Finance Manager"], "action": "reject"},
    {"from": "Approved",         "to": "Submitted",        "allowed_roles": ["Purchase Manager"],"action": "submit"},
    {"from": "Approved",         "to": "Draft",            "allowed_roles": ["Finance Manager"], "action": "revert"},
    {"from": "*",                "to": "Cancelled",        "allowed_roles": ["System Manager"],  "action": "cancel"}
  ],
  "guards": [
    {"state": "Approved", "condition": "total  str
    # Register a state machine for a DocType

sm_unregister(doctype: str) -> str

sm_transition(doctype: str, name: str, current_state: str,
              action: str, user: str, roles_json: str) -> str
    # Validate and record a transition
    # Returns: {"allowed": bool, "new_state": str, "reason": str}

sm_can_transition(doctype: str, current_state: str,
                  action: str, roles_json: str) -> str
    # Check without recording
    # Returns: {"allowed": bool, "reason": str}

sm_available_actions(doctype: str, current_state: str, roles_json: str) -> str
    # What actions can this user take from this state?
    # Returns: {"actions": ["approve", "reject"]}

sm_get_state(doctype: str, name: str) -> str
    # Get current recorded state for a doc
    # Returns: {"state": str, "since": timestamp_ms}

sm_set_state(doctype: str, name: str, state: str) -> str
    # Force-set state (for migration / repair)

sm_history(doctype: str, name: str) -> str
    # Full transition history
    # Returns: [{"from": str, "to": str, "action": str, "user": str, "ts": ms}]

sm_history_add(doctype: str, name: str, entry_json: str) -> str
    # Append a history entry

sm_bulk_states(doctype: str, names_json: str) -> str
    # Get states for N docs in one call

sm_stats(doctype: str) -> str
    # Distribution of docs across states, average time in each state
```

### Python integration

```python
# frappe_rpg/integrations/workflow_hook.py

def validate(doc, method=None):
    if not _rs_has_sm(doc.doctype):
        return
    # Check transition is legal before save
    result = json.loads(_rs.sm_transition(
        doc.doctype, doc.name,
        doc._doc_before_save.workflow_state if doc._doc_before_save else "Draft",
        doc.workflow_state,
        frappe.session.user,
        json.dumps(frappe.get_roles())
    ))
    if not result["allowed"]:
        frappe.throw(result["reason"], frappe.PermissionError)

def on_submit(doc, method=None):
    _rs.sm_history_add(doc.doctype, doc.name, json.dumps({
        "from": "Draft", "to": "Submitted",
        "action": "submit", "user": frappe.session.user
    }))
```

---

## Tasks

- [ ] `sm_register`, `sm_unregister`
- [ ] `sm_transition` — validate role + state, record history
- [ ] `sm_can_transition` — read-only validation
- [ ] `sm_available_actions`
- [ ] `sm_get_state`, `sm_set_state`
- [ ] `sm_history`, `sm_history_add`
- [ ] `sm_bulk_states`
- [ ] `sm_stats`
- [ ] Guard expression evaluator (simple: `total <= N`, role membership)
- [ ] Wildcard `"*"` from-state support
- [ ] `workflow_hook.py` — validate + on_submit/on_cancel hooks
- [ ] Tests: valid transition, invalid role, illegal state jump, wildcard cancel, history
- [ ] Stubs + lib.rs registration
- [ ] Update `tracker.md`

---

## Expected Performance

| Operation | Before | After |
|-----------|--------|-------|
| Workflow state validation | ~15ms Python (DB lookups) | <0.1ms Rust (in-memory) |
| Illegal transition detection | After DB write (or not at all) | Before DB write |
| State history query | DB query to Workflow Log | In-process <0.1ms |
| Bulk state fetch (100 docs) | 100 DB queries | Single Rust lookup |

---

## Notes

- Rust state machine is authoritative for transition validation
- DB Workflow Log still written for audit compliance — Rust history is for fast reads
- `sm_stats` enables: "average time from Draft to Approved = 2.3 hours" dashboards
- Compatible with Frappe's built-in Workflow DocType — load rules from Frappe Workflow on startup

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_rpg/frappe_rpg/docs/phase_39_materialized_views.md
# Phase 39 — The Materialized View Engine

> **Theme:** Pre-compute expensive dashboard and report queries on doc events,
> serve results in  Eliminates the "dashboard loads in 8 seconds" problem.

---

## Overview

| Item | Detail |
|------|--------|
| **Module** | `rust/src/materialized_views.rs` |
| **Go sidecar** | `go/matview/main.go` (`:7089`) |
| **Phase** | 39 |
| **Dependencies** | `serde_json`, `moka`, Phase 26 worker (refresh jobs) |
| **Test file** | `tests/test_phase39_materialized_views.py` |

---

## Problem

A typical ERPNext dashboard widget:

```python
# "Outstanding Receivables" widget — runs on every dashboard load
frappe.db.sql("""
    SELECT customer, SUM(outstanding_amount), COUNT(*)
    FROM `tabSales Invoice`
    WHERE docstatus=1 AND outstanding_amount > 0
    GROUP BY customer
    ORDER BY SUM(outstanding_amount) DESC
    LIMIT 20
""")
# → Full table scan: 500,000 rows, ~4s
```

With a materialized view: pre-computed result lives in Rust memory, served in  str
    # definition: {"doctype": "Sales Invoice", "refresh_on": ["on_submit", "on_cancel"],
    #              "ttl": 300, "query_key": "outstanding_receivables"}
    # Returns: {"registered": true}

mv_set(view_name: str, result_json: str, computed_at_ms: int) -> str
    # Store computed result

mv_get(view_name: str) -> str
    # Returns: {"hit": bool, "result": [...], "computed_at": ms, "age_s": f}

mv_invalidate(view_name: str) -> str
    # Mark stale — triggers async refresh

mv_invalidate_for_doctype(doctype: str) -> str
    # Invalidate all views that depend on this DocType

mv_is_stale(view_name: str) -> str
    # {"stale": bool, "age_s": f, "ttl": f}

mv_list() -> str
    # All registered views + their staleness

mv_stats() -> str
    # Hit rates, refresh counts, average age, staleness distribution
```

### Go sidecar — refresh worker (`:7089`)

Receives invalidation events from Frappe hooks, queues refreshes, executes
Python callbacks (via HTTP to Frappe), stores results back into Rust via PyO3.

```
POST /invalidate          → {"view_name": "outstanding_receivables"}
POST /invalidate_doctype  → {"doctype": "Sales Invoice"}
GET  /status              → view freshness map
GET  /health
GET  /metrics
```

### Python integration

```python
# frappe_rpg/integrations/matview_hook.py

# ── Registration (on app init) ─────────────────────────────────────────
VIEWS = {
    "outstanding_receivables": {
        "doctype": "Sales Invoice",
        "refresh_on": ["on_submit", "on_cancel", "on_update_after_submit"],
        "ttl": 300,
        "compute": "frappe_rpg.reports.outstanding_receivables",
    },
    "stock_balance_summary": {
        "doctype": "Stock Ledger Entry",
        "refresh_on": ["after_insert"],
        "ttl": 60,
        "compute": "frappe_rpg.reports.stock_balance_summary",
    },
}

# ── Serving ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
@frappe.whitelist()
def get_matview(view_name):
    result = json.loads(_rs.mv_get(view_name))
    if result["hit"]:
        return {"data": json.loads(result["result"]),
                "cached": True, "age_s": result["age_s"]}
    # Synchronous compute on first miss
    data = compute_view(view_name)
    _rs.mv_set(view_name, json.dumps(data),
               int(time.time() * 1000))
    return {"data": data, "cached": False}

# ── Invalidation hook ─────────────────────────────────────────────────
def on_submit(doc, method=None):
    _rs.mv_invalidate_for_doctype(doc.doctype)
    # Fire async refresh via Go sidecar
    requests.post("http://127.0.0.1:7089/invalidate_doctype",
                  json={"doctype": doc.doctype}, timeout=0.1)
```

### Pre-built views (included)

| View name | DocType | Refresh on | TTL |
|-----------|---------|------------|-----|
| `outstanding_receivables` | Sales Invoice | submit/cancel | 5min |
| `payables_summary` | Purchase Invoice | submit/cancel | 5min |
| `stock_balance_summary` | Stock Ledger Entry | insert | 60s |
| `monthly_sales` | Sales Invoice | submit/cancel | 10min |
| `item_wise_sales` | Sales Invoice Item | submit | 10min |
| `employee_attendance_today` | Attendance | insert/update | 30s |

---

## Tasks

**Rust:**
- [ ] `mv_register`, `mv_set`, `mv_get`, `mv_invalidate`
- [ ] `mv_invalidate_for_doctype`, `mv_is_stale`, `mv_list`, `mv_stats`

**Go sidecar:**
- [ ] `/invalidate`, `/invalidate_doctype` endpoints
- [ ] Refresh job queue (debounced: same view invalidated 10× in 1s = 1 refresh)
- [ ] Refresh executor: HTTP call to Frappe compute endpoint
- [ ] `/status`, `/health`, `/metrics`

**Python:**
- [ ] `matview_hook.py` — registration, serving, invalidation hooks
- [ ] `frappe_rpg.api.get_matview` whitelist endpoint
- [ ] 6 pre-built compute functions
- [ ] Doc event wiring in `hooks.py`
- [ ] Tests: cache hit, stale serve + async refresh, invalidation cascade
- [ ] Update `tracker.md`

---

## Expected Performance

| Scenario | Before | After |
|----------|--------|-------|
| Dashboard load (4 widgets) | ~12s | <50ms (all from Rust) |
| First load (cold cache) | ~12s | ~12s (then cached) |
| After doc submit (refresh) | Instant stale | Fresh within 2s async |
| Stock balance widget | ~4s DB scan | <1ms |

---

## Notes

- Debounce: if 1,000 stock entries are imported in batch, refresh fires once after the batch, not 1,000 times
- TTL is a backstop — event-driven invalidation is primary
- Views can be nested: `monthly_sales` can reference `item_wise_sales` partial results
- Go sidecar is optional — invalidation can be synchronous via `_rs.mv_invalidate` directly

# ⚔️ Phase 3: The Battlefield

> **Status:** ⬜ Planned
> **Focus:** Performance Engines — Analytics, Go gRPC Bridge, MicroVM Runtime
> **Prerequisites:** Phase 2 complete, all utility modules tested

---

## 🎯 Phase Objective

Deploy Frappe-RPG into real battle conditions. This phase connects Rust's raw power to ERPNext's data layer (via Polars), establishes the Go real-time event bridge for Andromeda, and lays the groundwork for Firecracker MicroVM execution. This is where the system proves its ROI.

---

## 📋 Tasks

### 📊 3.1 — The Sagar Data Engine (Polars-Powered Reports)

> **Goal:** Replace slow `frappe.get_all` in heavy reports with Rust/Polars, achieving 10x–100x speedup.

#### Dependencies
```toml
polars = { version = "0.39", features = ["lazy", "csv", "json", "parquet", "strings"] }
```

#### Functions to implement in `src/analytics.rs`

- [ ] **`aggregate_sales_data(json_rows: &str) -> String`**
  - Accept JSON array of Sales Invoice rows
  - Use Polars LazyFrame to aggregate: total, avg, group-by item/customer
  - Return aggregated result as JSON

- [ ] **`compute_stock_ledger(json_rows: &str) -> String`**
  - Compute running balance across Stock Ledger entries
  - Use Polars to calculate cumulative sums per warehouse/item

- [ ] **`filter_large_dataset(json_rows: &str, filters: &str) -> String`**
  - Accept JSON array + filter spec
  - Apply Polars boolean filters
  - Return filtered JSON

- [ ] **`pivot_report(json_rows: &str, index: &str, columns: &str, values: &str) -> String`**
  - Generic pivot table generator
  - Returns pivoted data as JSON

- [ ] **`read_csv_fast(file_path: &str) -> String`**
  - Use Polars to read large CSV files
  - Return summary statistics + first N rows as JSON

#### Frappe Integration
- [ ] Create Frappe Report type: `Rust-Powered Sales Summary`
- [ ] Override `get_data()` to call `frappe_rs.analytics.aggregate_sales_data`
- [ ] Add benchmark logging: compare duration vs. standard query

---

### 🏹 3.2 — The AetherLink Bridge (Go gRPC Server)

> **Goal:** A Go sidecar that exposes system events and Frappe notifications via gRPC and WebSocket, powering the Andromeda dashboard in real-time.

#### Go Dependencies (`go.mod`)
```
google.golang.org/grpc
google.golang.org/protobuf
github.com/gorilla/websocket
github.com/go-redis/redis/v8
```

#### Protobuf Definition (`go/proto/events.proto`)
```proto
syntax = "proto3";
package frappe_events;

service EventStream {
  rpc Subscribe(SubscribeRequest) returns (stream SystemEvent);
}

message SubscribeRequest {
  string client_id = 1;
  repeated string event_types = 2;
}

message SystemEvent {
  string event_type = 1;
  string payload = 2;
  int64 timestamp = 3;
}
```

#### Components to build in `go/`

- [ ] **gRPC server** (`go/server/grpc.go`)
  - Implements `EventStream.Subscribe`
  - Accepts multiple client subscriptions
  - Fans out Redis Pub/Sub messages to connected clients

- [ ] **WebSocket gateway** (`go/server/ws.go`)
  - WebSocket endpoint: `ws://localhost:7070/ws`
  - Bridges gRPC stream to browser-compatible WebSocket events
  - Handles reconnects gracefully

- [ ] **Redis subscriber** (`go/server/redis.go`)
  - Subscribes to Frappe's Redis channels
  - Converts Frappe background job events to `SystemEvent` proto
  - Buffers events during client disconnects

- [ ] **Health + metrics endpoint** (`go/server/health.go`)
  - `/health` — liveness probe
  - `/metrics` — Prometheus-compatible metrics
  - `/stats` — connected clients, events/sec

#### Frappe Integration
- [ ] Frappe hook: on `doctype_submit`, publish event to Redis
- [ ] Frappe hook: on job completion, publish to `frappe:events:jobs` channel
- [ ] Python helper: `publish_event(event_type, payload)` → Redis publish

---

### 🔥 3.3 — The FlowLab Runtime (Firecracker MicroVMs)

> **Goal:** Allow Frappe to spin up isolated Firecracker MicroVMs via Rust for safe execution of untrusted code or isolated workloads.

> ⚠️ **Note:** This requires a Linux environment with KVM support. macOS development uses a simulation layer.

- [ ] **`list_microvms() -> String`**
  - Return JSON list of all running MicroVMs with their status

- [ ] **`get_microvm_metrics(vm_id: &str) -> String`**
  - Return CPU + memory usage for a given MicroVM

#### Frappe Integration
- [ ] DocType: `MicroVM Job` — tracks spawned VMs linked to Frappe documents
- [ ] Background job: Poll MicroVM status and update DocType
- [ ] API endpoint: `POST /api/method/frappe_rpg.api.spawn_vm`

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_rpg/frappe_rpg/docs/phase_40_notification_dispatcher.md
# Phase 40 — The Notification Dispatcher

> **Theme:** Replace Frappe's synchronous `frappe.sendmail` / SMS / push blocking
> the request path with a Go sidecar that handles batching, deduplication, digest
> mode, and priority queues — all off the Gunicorn workers.

---

## Overview

| Item | Detail |
|------|--------|
| **Binary** | `go/notify/main.go` (`:7090`) |
| **Phase** | 40 |
| **Port** | `:7090` |
| **Dependencies** | SMTP, SMS gateway HTTP API, FCM/APNs |
| **Test file** | `tests/test_phase40_notification_dispatcher.py` |

---

## Problem

```python
# Every approval email blocks a Gunicorn worker for 200–800ms
frappe.sendmail(
    recipients=["user@example.com"],
    subject="Purchase Order approved",
    message=frappe.render_template(template, context),
)
# → blocks worker while SMTP handshake + DATA transfer completes
```

Additional problems:
- No deduplication: same "stock below reorder level" alert sent 500× during bulk import
- No digest: 20 individual approval emails instead of one "20 items need your attention"
- No priority: OTP email treated same as promotional newsletter
- No delivery tracking: no way to know if email reached inbox

---

## Design

```
┌────────────────────────┐    HTTP POST     ┌──────────────────────────────┐
│  Frappe (Python)       │ ───────────────→ │  Go Notify Sidecar :7090     │
│  frappe.sendmail()     │                  │                              │
│  (replaced by hook)    │                  │  Priority queues:            │
└────────────────────────┘                  │  • critical  (OTP, alerts)   │
                                            │  • high      (approvals)     │
                                            │  • normal    (notifications) │
                                            │  • digest    (grouped)       │
                                            │                              │
                                            │  Channels:                   │
                                            │  • SMTP email                │
                                            │  • SMS (generic HTTP API)    │
                                            │  • Push (FCM)                │
                                            │  • In-app (WebSocket via     │
                                            │    Phase 22 realtime)        │
                                            └──────────────────────────────┘
```

### HTTP API

```
POST /send              → enqueue a notification
POST /send/bulk         → enqueue N notifications in one call
GET  /status/{id}       → delivery status
GET  /history           → recent notifications (last 200)
GET  /digest/pending    → notifications queued for digest
POST /digest/flush      → force-send all pending digests
GET  /health
GET  /metrics
POST /template/register → register a notification template
GET  /template/{name}   → get template
```

### Notification payload

```json
{
  "id":         "notif_auto_or_caller_provided",
  "channel":    "email|sms|push|inapp",
  "priority":   "critical|high|normal|digest",
  "recipients": ["user@example.com"],
  "template":   "purchase_order_approved",
  "context":    {"doc_name": "PO-0001", "amount": 50000},
  "dedup_key":  "stock_alert/ITEM-001",   // optional: suppress duplicates
  "dedup_ttl":  300,                       // seconds
  "digest_key": "daily_approval_summary", // optional: batch into digest
  "digest_after_s": 3600                  // flush digest after 1hr
}
```

### Deduplication

```go
// If dedup_key is set and a notification with the same key was sent
// within dedup_ttl seconds → drop silently, increment suppressed counter
seen := dedupCache.Get(dedup_key)
if seen {
    atomic.AddInt64(&suppressed, 1)
    return
}
dedupCache.Set(dedup_key, true, dedup_ttl)
```

### Digest mode

```go
// Notifications with digest_key are buffered.
// After digest_after_s (or when /digest/flush is called):
// 1. Group by recipient + digest_key
// 2. Render digest template with all buffered items
// 3. Send one consolidated email
```

### Python integration

```python
# frappe_rpg/integrations/notify_hook.py

def send_notification(channel="email", priority="normal", **kwargs):
    """Drop-in replacement for frappe.sendmail and frappe.publish_realtime."""
    requests.post("http://127.0.0.1:7090/send", json={
        "channel":    channel,
        "priority":   priority,
        "recipients": kwargs.get("recipients", []),
        "template":   kwargs.get("template", ""),
        "context":    kwargs.get("context", {}),
        "dedup_key":  kwargs.get("dedup_key", ""),
        "dedup_ttl":  kwargs.get("dedup_ttl", 0),
        "digest_key": kwargs.get("digest_key", ""),
    }, timeout=0.05)  # fire-and-forget — 50ms timeout
    return {"queued": True}
```

---

## Tasks

- [ ] HTTP server: `/send`, `/send/bulk`, `/status`, `/history`, `/health`, `/metrics`
- [ ] Priority queue (4 levels) with goroutine workers per channel
- [ ] SMTP worker: connection pooling, TLS, retry on 4xx/5xx
- [ ] SMS worker: configurable HTTP gateway (MSG91 / Twilio / AWS SNS)
- [ ] Push worker: FCM HTTP v1 API
- [ ] In-app worker: POST to Phase 22 realtime `/pub`
- [ ] Deduplication cache (in-memory, configurable TTL)
- [ ] Digest buffer + flush scheduler
- [ ] Template registry: Go-native `text/template` rendering
- [ ] Delivery tracking: status per notification ID
- [ ] `/digest/pending` and `/digest/flush`
- [ ] Prometheus metrics: sent, failed, suppressed, queued, digest_batches
- [ ] Python `notify_hook.py` + `frappe_rpg.api.send_notification`
- [ ] Tests: send, dedup suppression, digest flush, priority ordering, bulk send
- [ ] Update `tracker.md`

---

## Expected Performance

| Metric | Before | After |
|--------|--------|-------|
| `sendmail` blocking time | 200–800ms | <1ms (fire-and-forget) |
| Duplicate alert suppression | None (all sent) | Configurable TTL dedup |
| Digest (20 approvals) | 20 emails | 1 digest email |
| Throughput | ~5 emails/sec (SMTP pool) | 100+ emails/sec |

---

## Notes

- `critical` priority (OTPs, security alerts) bypasses dedup and digest — always sent immediately
- SMTP connection pool: 5 persistent connections to avoid handshake overhead
- FCM batch send: up to 500 push notifications per HTTP request
- In-app notifications fan out to all WebSocket connections for the recipient via Phase 22 realtime hub

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_rpg/frappe_rpg/docs/phase_41_tally_bridge.md
# Phase 41 — The Tally Bridge

> **Theme:** Parse and generate Tally Prime XML natively in Rust — enabling
> two-way sync between ERPNext/TallyNext and existing Tally Prime installations
> without any middleware or Tally.NET dependency.

---

## Overview

| Item | Detail |
|------|--------|
| **Module** | `rust/src/tally_bridge.rs` |
| **Go sidecar** | `go/tally_sync/main.go` (`:7091`) |
| **Phase** | 41 |
| **Port** | `:7091` |
| **Dependencies** | `quick-xml` (Rust), `serde_json`, `rust_decimal` (Phase 37) |
| **Test file** | `tests/test_phase41_tally_bridge.py` |

---

## Problem

Indian businesses using TallyNext (built on ERPNext) often need to:
1. **Import historical data** from Tally Prime (years of vouchers, masters)
2. **Sync daily** — push ERPNext transactions into Tally for CA/auditor who insists on Tally
3. **Validate** — ensure GST numbers, ledger balances match between the two systems

Tally Prime exposes an XML API (port 9000 by default). Currently, any Tally integration
requires Python `xml.etree` + hand-written XPath + Tally-specific XML quirks. Slow and fragile.

---

## Design

### Rust XML layer

```
tally_parse_voucher(xml: str) -> str
    # Parse a Tally voucher XML into clean JSON
    # Returns: {"type": "Sales", "date": "20260101", "amount": d, "narration": str,
    #           "ledger_entries": [...], "inventory_entries": [...]}

tally_parse_master(xml: str, master_type: str) -> str
    # Parse Ledger / Stock Item / Cost Centre master XML
    # master_type: "Ledger" | "StockItem" | "CostCentre" | "Group"

tally_parse_balance_sheet(xml: str) -> str
    # Parse Tally balance sheet XML into structured JSON

tally_generate_voucher(doc_json: str, voucher_type: str) -> str
    # Generate Tally-compatible voucher XML from ERPNext doc
    # voucher_type: "Sales" | "Purchase" | "Payment" | "Receipt" | "Journal" | "Contra"

tally_generate_master(master_json: str, master_type: str) -> str
    # Generate Tally master XML (for importing customers, items into Tally)

tally_generate_import_xml(vouchers_json: str) -> str
    # Generate a complete Tally XML import envelope for bulk voucher import

tally_validate_xml(xml: str) -> str
    # Validate Tally XML structure
    # Returns: {"valid": bool, "errors": [...]}

tally_extract_gstin(xml: str) -> str
    # Extract all GSTINs from a Tally company/party XML

tally_map_ledger(tally_ledger_json: str, accounts_json: str) -> str
    # Map Tally ledger names to ERPNext Chart of Accounts
    # Returns: {"mappings": [{"tally": str, "erpnext": str, "confidence": f}]}

tally_diff_balances(tally_tb_json: str, erpnext_tb_json: str) -> str
    # Compare trial balances from both systems
    # Returns: {"matched": [...], "mismatched": [...], "tally_only": [...], "erpnext_only": [...]}
```

### Go sidecar — Tally sync daemon (`:7091`)

Connects to Tally Prime's XML API (port 9000), polls for new vouchers, pushes to Frappe.

```
POST /sync/pull          → pull vouchers from Tally since last_sync
POST /sync/push          → push ERPNext docs to Tally
POST /sync/masters       → sync ledger/item masters
GET  /sync/status        → last sync time, pending count, error log
GET  /mapping            → current ledger mapping table
POST /mapping/update     → update a ledger mapping
GET  /health
GET  /metrics
POST /validate/balance   → compare trial balances
```

### Tally XML envelope format

```xml


  
    Import Data
  
  
    
      
        Vouchers
      
      
        
          
            
          
        
      
    
  

```

### Python integration

```python
# frappe_rpg/integrations/tally_hook.py

@frappe.whitelist()
def export_to_tally(doctype, names):
    """Export ERPNext docs to Tally Prime."""
    docs = [frappe.get_doc(doctype, n).as_dict() for n in json.loads(names)]
    voucher_type = TALLY_VOUCHER_MAP.get(doctype, "Journal")
    xml_payload = json.loads(
        _rs.tally_generate_import_xml(json.dumps(docs))
    )["xml"]
    # Send to Go sidecar which forwards to Tally
    r = requests.post("http://127.0.0.1:7091/sync/push",
                      json={"xml": xml_payload}, timeout=30)
    return r.json()

@frappe.whitelist()
def import_from_tally(from_date, to_date):
    """Pull vouchers from Tally and create ERPNext drafts."""
    r = requests.post("http://127.0.0.1:7091/sync/pull",
                      json={"from_date": from_date, "to_date": to_date})
    vouchers = r.json()["vouchers"]
    created = []
    for v in vouchers:
        parsed = json.loads(_rs.tally_parse_voucher(v["xml"]))
        doc = create_erpnext_doc_from_tally(parsed)
        created.append(doc.name)
    return {"created": created}
```

### Supported Tally versions
- Tally Prime 3.x, 4.x (XML API format)
- Tally ERP 9 (legacy XML format — auto-detected)

---

## Tasks

**Rust:**
- [ ] `tally_parse_voucher` — Sales, Purchase, Payment, Receipt, Journal, Contra, Stock
- [ ] `tally_parse_master` — Ledger, StockItem, CostCentre, Group
- [ ] `tally_parse_balance_sheet`
- [ ] `tally_generate_voucher` — all 6 voucher types
- [ ] `tally_generate_master`
- [ ] `tally_generate_import_xml` (full envelope with multiple vouchers)
- [ ] `tally_validate_xml`
- [ ] `tally_extract_gstin`
- [ ] `tally_map_ledger` (fuzzy name matching using edit distance)
- [ ] `tally_diff_balances`
- [ ] Tally ERP 9 vs Prime auto-detection

**Go sidecar:**
- [ ] Tally XML API client (HTTP to port 9000)
- [ ] `/sync/pull`, `/sync/push`, `/sync/masters`
- [ ] `/sync/status`, `/mapping`, `/mapping/update`
- [ ] Incremental sync: track `last_sync` watermark
- [ ] Error recovery: partial import rollback
- [ ] `/health`, `/metrics`

**Python:**
- [ ] `tally_hook.py` — export_to_tally, import_from_tally whitelist APIs
- [ ] Ledger mapping UI (Frappe Page)
- [ ] Reconciliation report: `tally_diff_balances` output
- [ ] Tests: parse Sales voucher, generate Journal XML, diff balances
- [ ] Stubs + lib.rs registration
- [ ] Update `tracker.md`

---

## Notes

- `quick-xml` is a zero-copy streaming XML parser — handles 100MB Tally exports without loading into memory
- Tally's XML has many quirks (CDATA, non-standard entity encoding) — Rust handles all of them
- Ledger mapping is the hardest part: "Sundry Debtors" in Tally → "Accounts Receivable" in ERPNext
- `tally_map_ledger` uses fuzzy matching + a curated hint table for common Indian accounting terms
- Direct value for TallyNext: this is what makes TallyNext a genuine Tally replacement with migration path

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_rpg/frappe_rpg/docs/phase_42_vanguard.md
# Phase 42 — The Vanguard: Concurrent HTTP Prefetch Cache

> **Theme:** Scout ahead. Fetch external API data before the request arrives using Go goroutines,
> hold it in Go memory, serve it in  str
    # Returns: "{prefix}:{xxhash64(entity_id|from_date|to_date)}"
    # xxHash64: 1 ns/call — faster than MD5 by 8×

prefetch_key_batch(prefix, entity_ids: Vec, from_date, to_date) -> Vec
    # Parallel Rayon map — generates N keys simultaneously

prefetch_merge_records(existing_json, new_json, date_field, from_date, to_date) -> str
    # Merges two record arrays by date range, deduplicates by record fingerprint.
    # Same logic as frappe_snapshot merge but in Rust (~500× faster for 50k rows).

prefetch_filter_records(records_json, from_date, to_date, date_field) -> str
    # Filter a cached payload to a sub-range without re-fetching.
    # e.g. cache covers Apr 1 – May 12; filter to Apr 1 – Apr 30.

snapshot_index_build(records_json, index_fields, date_field) -> str
    # frappe_snapshot Phase 10 accelerator.
    # Rayon parallel extraction of index entries from a payload JSON array.
    # Input:  JSON array of records (payload_json stored in Snapshot Cache).
    # Output: JSON array of {record_date, index_key, index_value} tuples —
    #         ready for bulk insert into Snapshot Index Entry.
    # 10–50× faster than the Python loop for large payloads (>1,000 records).

snapshot_dedup_fingerprints(records_json) -> str
    # frappe_snapshot merge-dedup accelerator (replaces Python json.dumps + set loop).
    # Returns JSON array with duplicate records removed.
    # Fingerprint = xxHash64 of all field values, sort-stable.
    # frappe_snapshot engine/writer.py calls this instead of its Python dedup.
```

### `prefetch_merge_records` detail

When a warm job covers `2026-04-01 → 2026-05-12` and the next day's job covers
`2026-04-01 → 2026-05-13`, this function merges the two payloads and deduplicates
by record fingerprint (xxhash of all field values) instead of re-fetching the whole range.

```python
# Python usage
merged = frappe_rs.prefetch_merge_records(
    existing_json=cached_payload,
    new_json=new_payload,
    date_field="date_of_implant",
    from_date="2026-04-01",
    to_date="2026-05-13",
)
```

---

## Python Bridge — `frappe_rpg/prefetch.py`

```python
import requests
import frappe

_VANGUARD_URL = "http://127.0.0.1:7091"
_AVAILABLE = None   # lazy-check on first call

def _is_available() -> bool:
    global _AVAILABLE
    if _AVAILABLE is None:
        try:
            requests.get(f"{_VANGUARD_URL}/health", timeout=0.2)
            _AVAILABLE = True
        except Exception:
            _AVAILABLE = False
    return _AVAILABLE

def get_prefetched(cache_key: str) -> list | None:
    """
    Return cached data for key, or None on miss / sidecar down.
    Hot path — must be  bool:
    """
    Submit a warm job to the Vanguard sidecar.
    Non-blocking — returns immediately, fetching happens in Go goroutines.
    """
    if not _is_available():
        return False
    try:
        requests.post(
            f"{_VANGUARD_URL}/warm",
            json={
                "job_id": job_id,
                "endpoint_url": endpoint_url,
                "method": method,
                "headers": headers,
                "entities": entities,
                "ttl_seconds": ttl,
                "concurrency": concurrency,
                "paginate": paginate,
                "data_field": data_field,
            },
            timeout=1.0,
        )
        return True
    except Exception:
        return False

def evict(cache_key: str):
    """Evict a single entry — call after data becomes stale."""
    if not _is_available():
        return
    try:
        requests.delete(f"{_VANGUARD_URL}/cached/{cache_key}", timeout=0.5)
    except Exception:
        pass

def evict_prefix(prefix: str):
    """Evict all entries for a prefix — e.g. when a fiscal year rolls over."""
    if not _is_available():
        return
    try:
        requests.post(f"{_VANGUARD_URL}/evict_prefix",
                      json={"prefix": prefix}, timeout=0.5)
    except Exception:
        pass

def warm_on_deploy(specs: list | None = None):
    """
    Trigger a warm cycle immediately — call from frappe_snapshot's after_migrate hook
    or from a bench post-deploy script so the cache is populated before the first request.

    specs=None  → warms all registered specs (same as nightly _vanguard_warm_all)
    specs=[...]  → warms only the named registered specs

    Blocks until all warm jobs are submitted to the Go sidecar (non-blocking goroutines).
    Returns list of spec names that were successfully submitted.
    """
    from frappe_rpg.prefetch import warm, _get_registered_specs
    import frappe

    submitted = []
    for spec in _get_registered_specs():
        if specs is not None and spec["name"] not in specs:
            continue
        try:
            spec["warmer"](warm)
            submitted.append(spec["name"])
        except Exception:
            frappe.log_error(title=f"warm_on_deploy failed: {spec['name']}",
                             message=frappe.get_traceback())
    return submitted
```

#### frappe_snapshot hook-in

frappe_snapshot Phase 8 adds a "warmup on deploy" feature — it calls fetchers sequentially in Python (~25min for 100 employees). Replace with Vanguard's goroutine version:

```python
# frappe_snapshot/engine/scheduler.py — at end of run_snapshot_job(), after lock release

def _trigger_vanguard_warm_if_available(snapshot_type_name: str):
    try:
        from frappe_rpg.prefetch import warm_on_deploy
        warm_on_deploy(specs=[snapshot_type_name])
    except ImportError:
        pass   # frappe_rpg not installed — no-op
```

frappe_snapshot calls this after every successful backfill run. If frappe_rpg is installed, the Vanguard Go cache is refreshed immediately; if not, it's silently skipped.

---

## frappe_snapshot Rust Acceleration

Two Rust functions in `prefetch_key.rs` accelerate frappe_snapshot's compute-heavy paths directly:

### `snapshot_index_build` — Phase 10 index extraction

frappe_snapshot Phase 10 adds a `Snapshot Index Entry` flat table. The Python loop:
```python
# frappe_snapshot/engine/writer.py  (without acceleration) — O(records × fields)
entries = []
for record in records:
    for field in config.index_fields:
        entries.append({
            "record_date": record.get(date_field),
            "index_key": field,
            "index_value": str(record.get(field, "") or ""),
        })
frappe.db.bulk_insert("Snapshot Index Entry", entries)
```

With Rust acceleration:
```python
# frappe_snapshot/engine/writer.py  (with frappe_rpg)
try:
    import frappe_rs as _rs
    raw_entries = json.loads(_rs.snapshot_index_build(
        payload_json, index_fields, date_field
    ))
    frappe.db.bulk_insert("Snapshot Index Entry", raw_entries)
except (ImportError, Exception):
    # fallback to Python loop
    ...
```

Rayon parallel extraction across all records simultaneously → 10–50× faster for payloads with 10k+ records.

### `snapshot_dedup_fingerprints` — merge dedup acceleration

frappe_snapshot's merge step deduplicates using `json.dumps(record, sort_keys=True)` inside a Python `set`. For a 50,000-record payload this takes ~2s. The Rust version uses xxHash64 in Rayon — same result in ~4ms.

---

## Integration: DmIqService hook-in

The caller checks Vanguard first; falls back to direct HTTP transparently.
Zero changes to existing API contracts.

```python
# sales_budget_management/services/dmiq.py
# list_secondary_sales — add Vanguard check at the top

from frappe_rpg.prefetch import get_prefetched
try:
    from frappe_rpg.accelerate import _ctx as _rpg
    import frappe_rs as _rs
except Exception:
    _rs = None

def list_secondary_sales(self, employee_code=None, from_date=None, to_date=None, cache=True, ...):
    if cache and _rs is not None and employee_code:
        codes_key = "|".join(sorted(str(c) for c in (employee_code or [])))
        ck = _rs.prefetch_key("ss", codes_key, str(from_date or ""), str(to_date or ""))
        cached = get_prefetched(ck)
        if cached is not None:
            return cached
    # existing DmIqService Redis + HTTP logic unchanged below
    ...
```

---

## Integration: Nightly warm job

Added to `frappe_rpg/tasks.py` — fires automatically via `scheduler_events`.

```python
# frappe_rpg/tasks.py

def daily():
    hourly_anomaly_scan()
    daily_quota_report()
    daily_cache_prune()
    _vanguard_warm_all()          # ← new

def _vanguard_warm_all():
    """
    Warm Vanguard cache for all active employees.
    Consumer apps register their warm specs via frappe_rpg.prefetch.register_warm_spec().
    """
    from frappe_rpg.prefetch import warm, _get_registered_specs
    import frappe

    for spec in _get_registered_specs():
        try:
            spec["warmer"](warm)   # each app provides its own warmer callable
        except Exception:
            frappe.log_error(title="Vanguard warm error", message=frappe.get_traceback())
```

Consumer app registers a warmer once at startup:

```python
# sales_budget_management/hooks.py  (app startup)

def before_request():
    from frappe_rpg.prefetch import register_warm_spec
    register_warm_spec("dmiq_secondary_sales", _dmiq_warmer)

def _dmiq_warmer(warm_fn):
    import frappe
    from sales_budget_management.services.dmiq import DmIqService
    import frappe_rs as _rs

    employees = frappe.get_all("MDS Employee", fields=["employee_code"],
                               filters={"enabled": 1}, limit_page_length=0)
    codes = [e.employee_code for e in employees if e.employee_code]

    settings = frappe.get_single("Budget Settings")
    base_url = settings.dm_iq_service_url.rstrip("/")
    api_key  = settings.get_password("api_key", raise_exception=False) or ""

    from_date = frappe.utils.get_first_day_of_fiscal_year()
    to_date   = frappe.utils.today()

    entities = [
        {
            "cache_key": _rs.prefetch_key("ss", code, str(from_date), str(to_date)),
            "payload": {
                "employee_code": [code],
                "from_date": str(from_date),
                "to_date": str(to_date),
            },
        }
        for code in codes
    ]

    warm_fn(
        endpoint_url=f"{base_url}/secondary-sales/",
        method="POST",
        headers={"X-API-Key": api_key},
        entities=entities,
        ttl=86400,
        concurrency=50,
        job_id=f"dmiq_ss_{to_date}",
    )
```

---

## Performance Comparison

### frappe_snapshot (Python) vs The Vanguard (Go)

| Dimension | frappe_snapshot | The Vanguard |
|-----------|----------------|--------------|
| Nightly warm: 100 employees | ~100 × 15s = 25 min (sequential Python) | ~15s (100 goroutines) |
| Read latency (cache hit) | ~50ms (MySQL Doctype query) | ~0.1ms (Go RAM) |
| Read latency (Redis hit) | ~2ms | ~1ms |
| Survives Redis restart | ✅ (MySQL primary) | ✅ (Redis primary, Go reloads on start) |
| Desk UI / queryable | ✅ Frappe Doctype | ❌ Not a Doctype |
| Works for any data type | ✅ configurable via Desk | ✅ any HTTP endpoint |
| Requires Frappe Doctype | ✅ yes | ❌ no — zero Doctype overhead |

### Before / After for DM-IQ dashboard

| Scenario | Before | After (Vanguard warm hit) |
|----------|--------|--------------------------|
| `get_data` cold load | 30s | **0.3ms** (Go RAM) |
| `get_data` Redis hit | ~2ms | **0.1ms** (Go RAM) |
| Nightly warm (50 emp) | N/A | ~8s (50 goroutines) |
| Sub-APIs (coverage, penetration, …) | 5–15s (cold) | **0.1ms** |

---

## Tasks

**Go sidecar (`go/prefetch/main.go`):**
- [ ] In-memory cache: `sync.RWMutex` + `map[string]*CacheEntry` with TTL eviction ticker
- [ ] `POST /warm` — goroutine fan-out with semaphore-based concurrency limit
- [ ] Pagination loop inside goroutine (`paginate: true` follows `page` until `len(batch)  str` — xxHash64
- [ ] `prefetch_key_batch(prefix, entity_ids, from_date, to_date) -> Vec` — Rayon parallel
- [ ] `prefetch_merge_records(existing_json, new_json, date_field, from_date, to_date) -> str`
- [ ] `prefetch_filter_records(records_json, from_date, to_date, date_field) -> str`
- [ ] `snapshot_index_build(records_json, index_fields, date_field) -> str` — Rayon parallel index extraction (frappe_snapshot Phase 10)
- [ ] `snapshot_dedup_fingerprints(records_json) -> str` — xxHash64 dedup replacing Python json.dumps loop (frappe_snapshot merge)
- [ ] Register all 6 functions in `lib.rs`

**Python (`frappe_rpg/prefetch.py`):**
- [ ] `get_prefetched(cache_key) -> list | None`
- [ ] `warm(endpoint_url, method, headers, entities, …) -> bool`
- [ ] `evict(cache_key)`
- [ ] `evict_prefix(prefix)`
- [ ] `register_warm_spec(name, warmer_fn)` — app registration
- [ ] `_get_registered_specs() -> list`
- [ ] `_is_available()` — lazy health check with 30s negative TTL
- [ ] `warm_on_deploy(specs=None) -> list[str]` — immediate warm for post-deploy / frappe_snapshot hook-in

**Tasks / hooks (`frappe_rpg/tasks.py`, `hooks.py`):**
- [ ] `daily()` → calls `_vanguard_warm_all()`
- [ ] `_vanguard_warm_all()` — iterates registered specs, calls each warmer
- [ ] Add Vanguard sidecar to `hooks.py` `before_request` port check list
- [ ] Update `install.py` to build + register `go/prefetch/` binary

**Tests (`tests/test_phase42_vanguard.py`):**
- [ ] Go sidecar `/health` responds
- [ ] `POST /warm` with mock HTTP server → records stored in cache
- [ ] `GET /cached` hit returns data in < 1ms
- [ ] `GET /cached` miss returns 404
- [ ] `DELETE /cached/{key}` evicts entry
- [ ] Concurrency: 50 simultaneous warm goroutines complete without race condition
- [ ] Pagination: goroutine follows pages until last page (`len < per_page`)
- [ ] Redis write-through: entry readable from Redis after warm
- [ ] Cold restart: entries restored from Redis on sidecar start
- [ ] `prefetch_key` Rust: same inputs always produce same key
- [ ] `prefetch_key_batch`: batch = N × individual calls
- [ ] `prefetch_merge_records`: merged length ≤ existing + new (dedup)
- [ ] `prefetch_filter_records`: filtered records all within date range
- [ ] `snapshot_index_build`: output tuple count = records × index_fields
- [ ] `snapshot_dedup_fingerprints`: duplicate records removed, unique records preserved
- [ ] Python `get_prefetched`: returns None when sidecar is down (graceful fallback)
- [ ] Python `warm_on_deploy`: submits all registered specs; returns submitted names list
- [ ] Update `tracker.md`

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_rpg/frappe_rpg/docs/phase_43_scorekeeper.md
# Phase 43 — The Scorekeeper: RPG Performance Visibility

> **Theme:** Track what RPG actually wins. Measure every layer, attribute every millisecond,
> show the savings in human time. Phase 5/18 tell you if the server is healthy.
> The Scorekeeper tells you if RPG is working.

---

## Overview

| Item | Detail |
|------|--------|
| **Rust module** | `rust/src/scorekeeper.rs` |
| **Go sidecar** | `go/scorekeeper/main.go` (`:7092`) |
| **Python middleware** | `frappe_rpg/scorekeeper.py` |
| **Desk widget** | `frappe_rpg/public/js/rpg_scorecard.js` |
| **Phase** | 43 |
| **Dependencies** | Phase 5 (metrics), Phase 6 (Andromeda), Phase 17 (before/after_request hooks), Phase 18 (Watcher), Phase 42 (Vanguard) |
| **Test file** | `tests/test_phase43_scorekeeper.py` |

---

## Problem

After installing frappe_rpg you can see: CPU usage, Redis queue depth, gunicorn worker count, error rate.
What you **cannot** see:

- Which cache layer served each request (Rust RAM / Vanguard / Redis / MySQL / external HTTP)?
- Is `get_data` faster this week than last week, and why?
- What did Vanguard actually save vs the raw HTTP call?
- Which API is the worst offender at this moment?
- How much total user-facing wait time did RPG eliminate today?

All existing observability answers "is the server healthy?" —
The Scorekeeper answers "is RPG helping, and by exactly how much?"

---

## Core Concepts

### Cache layers

Every data fetch in any RPG-instrumented app is tagged with one of five layers:

| Layer | ID | Source | Typical latency |
|-------|----|--------|----------------|
| Rust moka RAM | `L0` | `frappe_rs` in-process LRU | 0.01ms |
| Vanguard Go RAM | `L1` | `go/prefetch` sidecar | 0.1ms |
| Redis | `L2` | `frappe.cache` / DmIqService TTL | 1ms |
| Frappe MySQL | `L3` | `frappe.get_all` / `frappe.db` | 5–50ms |
| External HTTP | `L4` | DmIqService, Saksham, RouteIQ | 5,000–30,000ms |
| Snapshot stale | `L5-stale` | frappe_snapshot `is_stale=1` hit | 5–50ms |
| Snapshot drift | `L5-drift` | frappe_snapshot Diff detected change ≥ threshold | synthetic event |

`L5-stale` is recorded when frappe_snapshot returns a cache hit flagged as stale (Phase 7 invalidation).
`L5-drift` is a synthetic Scorekeeper event emitted when a frappe_snapshot Diff record shows
`change_percent` above threshold — surfaces data quality changes alongside performance metrics.

### Request waterfall

Each API call is split into named phases:

```
T0 ──► T1 ──────► T2 ────────► T3 ─────────► T4
start   DB setup   HTTP fetch   Python proc   response sent
  48ms              0ms (L1)       210ms
```

### Savings model

One baseline measurement per endpoint (uncached, cold) is stored in Redis.
Every request logs `actual_ms`. Savings = `baseline_ms − actual_ms`.

```
get_data  baseline: 30,200ms  actual: 258ms  saved: 29,942ms
```

Daily aggregate: total wall-clock time returned to users.

### RPG Score (0–100)

```
score = (cache_hit_rate × 50)
      + ((1 − p99_ms / baseline_ms) × 30)
      + (vanguard_freshness_score × 20)
```

100 = everything cached, p99 at minimum, Vanguard freshly warmed.
Gives a single number for "how well is RPG doing right now?"

---

## Architecture

```
                   ┌────────────────────────────────────┐
[Frappe request]   │  frappe_rpg/scorekeeper.py          │
before_request ──► │  start_timer(), reset waterfall     │
                   │                                      │
                   │  [inside request]                    │
                   │  record_layer(endpoint, layer, ms)   │
                   │  record_phase(endpoint, phase, ms)   │
                   │                                      │
after_request ───► │  flush_waterfall() → Rust ring buf  │
                   │  set response headers:               │
                   │    X-RPG-Cache: L1                   │
                   │    X-RPG-Saved-Ms: 29942             │
                   │    X-RPG-Score: 94                   │
                   └─────────────┬──────────────────────┘
                                 │
                                 ▼
                   ┌────────────────────────────────────┐
                   │  rust/src/scorekeeper.rs            │
                   │  Lock-free ring buffer per endpoint │
                   │  (last 10,000 requests, ~8MB RAM)   │
                   │  Running p50/p90/p99                │
                   │  Layer distribution counters        │
                   │  Daily savings accumulator          │
                   └─────────────┬──────────────────────┘
                                 │ HTTP query
                                 ▼
                   ┌────────────────────────────────────┐
                   │  go/scorekeeper  (:7092)            │
                   │  Serves data to CLI + Andromeda     │
                   │  Manages baseline store in Redis    │
                   │  Aggregates daily savings report    │
                   └────────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

## Rust Module — `rust/src/scorekeeper.rs`

Lock-free, zero-allocation hot path. The `record_*` functions are called on every request
and must add negligible overhead (target:  str
    # Core record function. Updates ring buffer, layer counters, savings accumulator.
    # Returns JSON: {"score": f64, "saved_ms": f64}

sk_record_phase(endpoint: str, phase: str, duration_ms: f64) -> str
    # Record a named waterfall phase: "db_setup" | "http_fetch" | "python_proc"

sk_get_stats(endpoint: str) -> str
    # {"p50": f, "p90": f, "p99": f, "sample_count": n,
    #  "layer_dist": {"L0": %, "L1": %, "L2": %, "L3": %, "L4": %},
    #  "savings_today_ms": f, "score": f}

sk_get_all_stats() -> str
    # Stats for every endpoint that has been recorded — used by CLI + dashboard

sk_get_waterfall(endpoint: str, request_id: str) -> str
    # Retrieve the phase breakdown for a specific request ID

sk_get_score() -> str
    # Global RPG score (0–100) across all endpoints

sk_get_savings_today() -> str
    # {"total_saved_ms": f, "total_saved_human": "4h 12m",
    #  "requests_served": n, "cache_hit_rate": f}

sk_set_baseline(endpoint: str, baseline_ms: f64) -> str
    # Store uncached baseline for an endpoint

sk_reset_endpoint(endpoint: str) -> str
    # Clear ring buffer for an endpoint (e.g. after a deployment)

sk_reset_all() -> str
    # Clear all data (used in tests + daily rollover)
```

### Internal design

```rust
// One TierEntry per endpoint — allocated once, never freed
struct EndpointStats {
    ring:         Mutex>,  // capped at 10_000
    layer_counts: [AtomicU64; 5],                  // L0…L4
    saved_ms:     AtomicU64,                       // millseconds × 1000, fixed-point
    baseline_ms:  AtomicU64,
}

static REGISTRY: OnceLock> = OnceLock::new();
```

`DashMap` (sharded `RwLock` map) gives lock-free concurrent reads — the hot path on every
request never blocks on registry lookup. Ring buffer write takes `Mutex` only for the
specific endpoint, not globally.

---

## Go Sidecar — `go/scorekeeper/main.go`

Serves aggregated data to the CLI and Andromeda. Manages baseline persistence in Redis.
Does NOT sit on the hot path — called only by dashboards and CLI.

```
GET  /stats                  — all endpoint stats (p50/p90/p99 + layer dist + score)
GET  /stats/{endpoint}       — single endpoint detail
GET  /waterfall/{request_id} — phase breakdown for one request
GET  /savings                — today's savings summary
GET  /score                  — global RPG score
POST /baseline               — {"endpoint": "...", "baseline_ms": 30200.0}
GET  /baselines              — all stored baselines
GET  /worst                  — top 10 slowest endpoints by p99
GET  /layer_heatmap          — layer distribution across all endpoints as a matrix
GET  /health
GET  /metrics                — Prometheus: sk_p99_ms, sk_savings_ms_total, sk_score, sk_cache_hit_rate
```

---

## Python Middleware — `frappe_rpg/scorekeeper.py`

Wired via `hooks.py` `before_request` / `after_request` — zero changes needed in consumer apps.

```python
import time
import frappe

try:
    import frappe_rs as _rs
    _SK_AVAILABLE = True
except Exception:
    _SK_AVAILABLE = False

# ── Thread-local waterfall state ─────────────────────────────────────────────

import threading
_tl = threading.local()

def before_request():
    if not _SK_AVAILABLE:
        return
    _tl.t0 = time.perf_counter()
    _tl.phases = {}
    _tl.layer = "L3"        # default: assume MySQL unless overridden
    _tl.endpoint = _get_endpoint()

def after_request():
    if not _SK_AVAILABLE or not getattr(_tl, "t0", None):
        return
    total_ms = (time.perf_counter() - _tl.t0) * 1000
    endpoint = getattr(_tl, "endpoint", "unknown")
    layer    = getattr(_tl, "layer", "L3")

    result = json.loads(_rs.sk_record(endpoint, layer, total_ms, 0.0))

    # Attach performance headers — visible in browser network tab
    response = getattr(frappe.local, "response", None)
    if response is not None:
        response["X-RPG-Cache"]    = layer
        response["X-RPG-Time-Ms"]  = str(round(total_ms))
        response["X-RPG-Saved-Ms"] = str(round(result.get("saved_ms", 0)))
        response["X-RPG-Score"]    = str(round(result.get("score", 0)))

def record_layer(layer: str):
    """Call from any data accessor to declare which layer served the data."""
    if _SK_AVAILABLE:
        _tl.layer = layer

def record_phase(phase: str, duration_ms: float):
    """Record a named waterfall phase duration."""
    if _SK_AVAILABLE:
        _rs.sk_record_phase(getattr(_tl, "endpoint", "unknown"), phase, duration_ms)

def _get_endpoint() -> str:
    try:
        cmd = getattr(frappe.local, "form_dict", {}).get("cmd", "")
        return cmd or "unknown"
    except Exception:
        return "unknown"
```

### Layer declaration pattern

Each data accessor in consumer apps calls `record_layer()` when it resolves:

```python
# frappe_rpg/prefetch.py  (Vanguard hit)
from frappe_rpg.scorekeeper import record_layer

def get_prefetched(cache_key):
    ...
    if hit:
        record_layer("L1")     # ← Vanguard served it
        return data
    return None

# frappe_rpg/auto_wire.py  (Rust LRU hit)
def _rpg_get_cached_doc(doctype, name):
    raw = _rs.get_cached_doc(doctype, name)
    if raw:
        record_layer("L0")     # ← Rust RAM served it
        return json.loads(raw)
    ...
```

No changes needed to existing consumer app code — the layer is recorded by the RPG layer
itself, transparently.

---

## Waterfall phase tracking

Phases are recorded at the call sites that already exist:

```python
# dashboard.py — get_data() (already has timing from parallel_fetch change)

t_db_start = time.perf_counter()
# ... all DB queries ...
record_phase("db_setup", (time.perf_counter() - t_db_start) * 1000)

t_http_start = time.perf_counter()
_http_results = _parallel_fetch_http([...])
record_phase("http_fetch", (time.perf_counter() - t_http_start) * 1000)

t_py_start = time.perf_counter()
# ... get_sales_persons_data, get_kpi_trends, etc. ...
record_phase("python_proc", (time.perf_counter() - t_py_start) * 1000)
```

---

## Response headers

Every RPG-served API response carries:

```
X-RPG-Cache:    L1
X-RPG-Time-Ms:  258
X-RPG-Saved-Ms: 29942
X-RPG-Score:    94
X-RPG-Phases:   db=48ms,http=0ms,py=210ms
```

Developers see this instantly in the browser network tab — no dashboard required.
Makes the performance gain visible at the point of use.
