  # Print current global RPG score + per-endpoint breakdown
```

## frappe_snapshot Integration

Scorekeeper provides two utilities that frappe_snapshot phases can call directly —
no circular dependency, no coupling; both are opt-in with `try/except ImportError`.

### `adaptive_batch_size` — Phase 12 EWMA helper

frappe_snapshot Phase 12 needs an EWMA-smoothed batch size calculator.
Phase 10 Oracle already has `ewma()` in Rust — Scorekeeper exposes a Python wrapper:

```python
# frappe_rpg/scorekeeper.py  (add to existing)

def adaptive_batch_size(
    target_s: float,
    actual_s: float,
    current_size: int,
    alpha: float = 0.3,
    min_size: int = 10,
    max_size: int = 500,
) -> int:
    """
    EWMA-smoothed adaptive batch size for frappe_snapshot Phase 12.

    Formula:  raw = current_size * (target_s / actual_s)
              smoothed = ewma(raw, alpha)   # Rust Oracle EWMA
    Returns:  clamp(smoothed, min_size, max_size)

    frappe_snapshot calls this in engine/scheduler.py:
        new_batch = frappe_rpg.scorekeeper.adaptive_batch_size(
            target_s=config.target_batch_duration_s,
            actual_s=elapsed,
            current_size=config.get_effective_batch_size(),
        )
    """
    import frappe_rs as _rs
    if actual_s = 10.0:   # configurable threshold
            _emit_drift(
                snapshot_type=snapshot_type,
                entity_id=entity_id,
                change_percent=change_percent,
            )
    except ImportError:
        pass
```

```python
# frappe_rpg/scorekeeper.py  (new helper)

def _emit_drift(snapshot_type: str, entity_id: str, change_percent: float):
    """
    Synthetic L5-drift event — recorded in the Scorekeeper ring buffer as a
    data quality signal. Appears in the Andromeda Performance panel alongside
    latency metrics so operators see perf + data drift in one view.
    """
    endpoint = f"snapshot::{snapshot_type}"
    _rs.sk_record(endpoint, "L5-drift", 0.0, 0.0)
    if abs(change_percent) >= 25.0:
        import frappe
        frappe.log_error(
            title=f"Snapshot drift: {snapshot_type}/{entity_id}",
            message=f"change_percent={change_percent:.1f}% — data quality alert",
        )
```

The Andromeda "⚡ Performance" panel shows `L5-drift` events in the layer heatmap as a
separate row — operators see when source data drift is spiking alongside cache hit rates.

---

## Oracle Integration (extend Phase 10)

Wire Phase 10 Oracle EWMA anomaly detector to p99 latency data:

```python
# frappe_rpg/tasks.py — hourly_anomaly_scan() extension

def _scorekeeper_anomaly_check():
    stats = json.loads(_rs.sk_get_all_stats())
    for endpoint, data in stats.items():
        p99 = data["p99"]
        baseline = data.get("baseline_ms", 0)
        if baseline and p99 > baseline * 0.5:
            # Endpoint is running at >50% of cold baseline — alert
            frappe.log_error(
                title=f"RPG perf regression: {endpoint}",
                message=f"p99={p99}ms vs baseline={baseline}ms "
                        f"(layer: {data['dominant_layer']})"
            )
```

Alert text: *"get_data p99 increased from 280ms to 2,400ms — L4 HTTP fetch rate rose from 1% to 47%"*

Root cause is embedded: the layer distribution shift tells you exactly why.

- [ ] Fetches `frappe_rpg.scorekeeper.get_scorecard` every 30s
- [ ] Displays: score, hit rate, avg response, savings today, Vanguard freshness, slow API count

**Hooks (`frappe_rpg/hooks.py`):**
- [ ] Add `frappe_rpg.scorekeeper.before_request` to `before_request`
- [ ] Add `frappe_rpg.scorekeeper.after_request` to `after_request`
- [ ] Add Scorekeeper sidecar to port check list in `install.py`

**Tasks (`frappe_rpg/tasks.py`):**
- [ ] `hourly_anomaly_scan()` → add `_scorekeeper_anomaly_check()`
- [ ] `daily()` → add `_scorekeeper_daily_rollover()` (persist day's savings to Redis, reset ring buffer)

**Tests (`tests/test_phase43_scorekeeper.py`):**
- [ ] `sk_record` — recorded entry appears in `sk_get_stats` p50/p90/p99
- [ ] `sk_record` × 10,001 — ring buffer caps at 10,000 (oldest evicted)
- [ ] Layer distribution: 100 L1 records → layer_dist L1 = 100%
- [ ] Savings: `sk_get_savings_today` total matches sum of recorded saved_ms
- [ ] `sk_get_score` — varies correctly with hit rate and baseline
- [ ] `sk_set_baseline` → `sk_get_stats` reflects baseline_ms
- [ ] Phase recording: waterfall for request_id contains all phases
- [ ] Python `before_request` / `after_request` — `X-RPG-*` headers present on response
- [ ] `record_layer` in `after_request` → correct layer in stats
- [ ] CLI `perf report` — outputs table with endpoint rows
- [ ] Go `/stats` — returns JSON with all recorded endpoints
- [ ] Go `/worst` — returns top 10 sorted by p99 desc
- [ ] Go `/baseline` POST → GET `/baselines` returns the stored value
- [ ] Oracle integration: p99 > 50% of baseline triggers `frappe.log_error`
- [ ] `adaptive_batch_size`: target=actual → returns current_size; target > actual → size increases; clamps to min/max
- [ ] `_emit_drift`: change_percent ≥ 10 → L5-drift recorded in ring buffer; < 10 → no event
- [ ] Layer heatmap includes `L5-drift` row when drift events exist
- [ ] Update `tracker.md`

---

## Expected overhead

| Operation | Target |
|-----------|--------|
| `sk_record()` per request | < 2µs |
| `sk_record_phase()` per phase | < 1µs |
| `after_request` total | < 5µs |
| `X-RPG-*` header injection | < 1µs |
| Ring buffer read (p50/p90/p99) | < 10µs |

The Scorekeeper adds effectively zero overhead to request processing.
Rust lock-free `DashMap` + `AtomicU64` counters ensure no contention even under 500 rps.

## What becomes visible after Phase 43

| Question | Before | After |
|----------|--------|-------|
| Is RPG helping? | Unknown | `frappe-rpg perf score` → 94/100 |
| Where does get_data spend time? | Unknown | Waterfall: db=48ms, http=0ms (L1), py=210ms |
| Did Vanguard actually serve that? | Unknown | `X-RPG-Cache: L1` in browser network tab |
| How much did we save today? | Unknown | Dashboard: 4h 12m returned to users |
| Which API regressed after deploy? | Unknown | Oracle alert: p99 +757% on get_data |
| Is L4 HTTP still being hit? | Unknown | Layer heatmap: L4 = 1% (good) or 45% (warm missed) |

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_rpg/frappe_rpg/docs/phase_44_fastapi_lane.md
# Phase 44 — The FastAPI Lane

> **Theme:** Open an async ASGI lane alongside Frappe's Werkzeug WSGI server for the
> handful of endpoints where Python-level `await` actually wins — SSE streaming,
> external-API fan-out, schema-validated write APIs. Shares the same MySQL pool,
> Rust caches (`frappe_rs`), and Go sidecars; doesn't replace Frappe.

---

## Overview

| Item | Detail |
|------|--------|
| **Package** | `frappe_rpg/fastapi_lane/` |
| **Process** | `uvicorn frappe_rpg.fastapi_lane.app:app` on `:7094` |
| **Phase** | 44 |
| **Port** | `:7094` (behind Phase 25 gateway at `:7080`) |
| **Dependencies** | `fastapi>=0.115`, `uvicorn[standard]`, `pydantic>=2`, `httpx`, `sse-starlette` |
| **Test file** | `tests/test_phase44_fastapi_lane.py` |

---

## Problem

Frappe runs on Werkzeug (sync WSGI) under Gunicorn. Every request occupies a worker
for its full duration. This is fine for short DB-bound endpoints but expensive for:

- **Streaming responses** (SSE, long-lived chunked) — block a worker for minutes
- **External-API fan-out** — `requests.post(a); requests.post(b); requests.post(c)` is
  serialized; threadpool helps a bit but still costs workers
- **Heavy schema validation** — Frappe's per-field Python validation loop is ~10×
  slower than Pydantic v2's compiled Rust core
- **WebSocket upgrades** — Werkzeug can't do them; Phase 22 covers this in Go but
  not as Python APIs

We don't want to replace Frappe — the ORM, permission system, doctype layer, and
desk all depend on its WSGI stack. Instead, add a **parallel async lane** for
endpoints where async actually pays.

---

## Design

```
                    ┌──────────────────────────────────┐
  HTTP request  ──► │   Go Gateway :7080 (Phase 25)    │
                    │   route by path prefix:           │
                    │   • /api/*       → Frappe :8000   │   (Werkzeug, sync)
                    │   • /fapi/*      → FastAPI :7094  │   (uvicorn, async)
                    │   • /assets/*    → Static :7088   │
                    └──────────────────────────────────┘

  FastAPI lane shares with Frappe:
    • MySQL pool          (via Phase 32 multiplexer :7086)
    • Redis               (read-only — auth tokens, rate limit, vanguard cache)
    • Rust frappe_rs      (in-process PyO3, same wheel)
    • Frappe DocType meta (read-only, via Phase 30 session cache)
    • Auth                (validates Frappe session cookie / JWT — does NOT
                            run Frappe's permission stack, see below)
```

### Auth model

FastAPI lane runs **without `frappe.local`** — no Frappe app context, no implicit
permission checks. Authentication is handled by a Pydantic dependency:

```python
async def current_user(request: Request) -> User:
    """
    Validate Frappe session cookie OR API key/secret OR Phase 12 JWT.
    Returns (user_id, roles). Does NOT load Frappe doc-level permissions.
    """
```

Endpoints that need doctype-level permission checks must explicitly call
`frappe_rs.permission_check(...)` (Phase 29) — fast, lock-free, no Frappe boot.
This keeps the lane fast but pushes the responsibility onto the endpoint author.

### What goes here

Strict rules — keep the lane small and focused:

✅ **Good fit:**
- SSE / chunked streaming (live tail of jobs, scorekeeper feed, notifications)
- Async fan-out (`httpx.gather` 5 external APIs in parallel)
- Bulk read endpoints with Pydantic validation in/out
- Long-poll / WebSocket bridges that need Python business logic

❌ **Not allowed:**
- DocType CRUD — use Frappe REST `/api/resource/...`
- Anything calling `frappe.get_doc()` — pulls in the WSGI request stack
- Endpoints that need transactions across multiple doctypes
- Workflow/state-machine writes (Phase 38 runs in Frappe's `validate`)

---

## Initial endpoint set (3 representative)

### 1. `GET /fapi/stream/scorekeeper` — SSE live feed

```python
@app.get("/fapi/stream/scorekeeper")
async def scorekeeper_stream(user=Depends(current_user)):
    async def event_gen():
        while True:
            stats = json.loads(_rs.sk_get_all_stats())
            yield {"event": "stats", "data": json.dumps(stats)}
            await asyncio.sleep(1.0)
    return EventSourceResponse(event_gen())
```

Werkzeug equivalent: blocks a Gunicorn worker forever. FastAPI: one event loop,
1000s of concurrent streams on one process.

### 2. `POST /fapi/bulk/external_lookup` — async fan-out

```python
class LookupRequest(BaseModel):
    gstins: list[str] = Field(..., max_length=100)
    sources: list[Literal["gst_portal", "mca", "iec"]] = ["gst_portal"]

@app.post("/fapi/bulk/external_lookup")
async def lookup(req: LookupRequest, user=Depends(current_user)) -> dict:
    async with httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=10) as client:
        tasks = [client.post(SOURCES[s], json={"gstin": g})
                 for g in req.gstins for s in req.sources]
        responses = await asyncio.gather(*tasks, return_exceptions=True)
    return {"results": [_unwrap(r) for r in responses]}
```

100 GSTINs × 3 sources = 300 external calls. WSGI threadpool (workers × threads):
~30s. FastAPI: ~2s (bounded by slowest single response).

### 3. `POST /fapi/validate/invoice` — Pydantic-validated write surface

```python
class InvoiceLine(BaseModel):
    item_code: str = Field(..., max_length=140)
    qty: Decimal = Field(..., gt=0)
    rate: Decimal = Field(..., ge=0)
    discount_pct: Decimal = Field(0, ge=0, le=100)

class InvoicePayload(BaseModel):
    customer: str
    posting_date: date
    lines: list[InvoiceLine] = Field(..., min_length=1, max_length=500)
    supplier_gstin: str = Field(..., pattern=r"^\d{2}[A-Z]{5}\d{4}[A-Z][1-9A-Z]Z[0-9A-Z]$")

@app.post("/fapi/validate/invoice")
async def validate_invoice(p: InvoicePayload, user=Depends(current_user)):
    # Pydantic already validated structure + GSTIN regex. Now run business rules
    # in Rust (Phase 37) — exact decimal GST.
    summary = json.loads(_rs.tax_invoice_summary(p.model_dump_json()))
    return summary
```

Frappe's validation: ~25ms for 500 lines (Python loop). Pydantic v2: ~2ms.

---

## Process model

| Aspect | Choice |
|--------|--------|
| Server | `uvicorn` with `--workers 4 --loop uvloop --http httptools` |
| Lifespan | Async startup hook warms MySQL pool, prefetches DocType meta |
| Shutdown | Drains in-flight requests on SIGTERM (default uvicorn behavior) |
| Supervisor | Frappe's `Procfile` / `supervisor.conf` — same as Go sidecars |
| Logging | structlog → stdout → Phase 18 metrics_aggregator |
| Errors | `RequestValidationError` → 422 JSON; everything else → 500 JSON + Phase 5 log |

### Sharing the MySQL pool

We do **not** open a fresh connection pool. The FastAPI lane proxies SQL through
the Phase 32 connection multiplexer at `:7086`. This means:

- Async DB I/O via `httpx` → `:7086` → backend MySQL — no `asyncio` DBAPI drama
- Same pool sizing, same read-replica routing
- No `aiomysql` / `asyncmy` dependency to maintain

Alternative considered: use `aiomysql` directly. Rejected — adds another connection
pool to manage and bypasses Phase 32's load balancing.

---

## Integration with existing phases

| Phase | How FastAPI lane uses it |
|-------|-------------------------|
| **Phase 25 Gateway** | Routes `/fapi/*` prefix → :7094 |
| **Phase 29 Permission** | Endpoints call `permission_check()` directly (no Frappe boot) |
| **Phase 30 Session Cache** | DocType meta is hot from the start |
| **Phase 32 Multiplexer** | All SQL goes through :7086 |
| **Phase 37 Tax Engine** | Pydantic + Rust = fastest invoice validation in the stack |
| **Phase 40 Notify** | SSE replaces frontend polling for delivery status |
| **Phase 42 Vanguard** | `httpx` fan-out endpoints check Vanguard cache first |
| **Phase 43 Scorekeeper** | ASGI middleware records `record_layer()` per request |

---

## Tasks

**Package scaffold (`frappe_rpg/fastapi_lane/`):**
- [ ] `app.py` — FastAPI app instance, lifespan hook, CORS config
- [ ] `auth.py` — `current_user` dependency (cookie / API key / JWT)
- [ ] `db.py` — async HTTP client wrapper for Phase 32 multiplexer
- [ ] `middleware.py` — Scorekeeper integration, request ID injection
- [ ] `errors.py` — JSON exception handlers
- [ ] `settings.py` — pydantic-settings config

**Endpoints (initial 3):**
- [ ] `stream/scorekeeper.py` — SSE feed from `sk_get_all_stats()`
- [ ] `bulk/external_lookup.py` — async GSTIN/MCA lookup fan-out
- [ ] `validate/invoice.py` — Pydantic + `tax_invoice_summary()`

**Process / deployment:**
- [ ] `Procfile` entry: `fastapi: uvicorn frappe_rpg.fastapi_lane.app:app --workers 4 --loop uvloop --port 7094`
- [ ] Gateway route registration for `/fapi/*` → `:7094` in Phase 25 config
- [ ] `install.py` — pip install `fastapi uvicorn[standard] pydantic httpx sse-starlette`

**Observability:**
- [ ] ASGI middleware emits Phase 43 `sk_record(endpoint, "L0|L1|L3", ms, baseline)`
- [ ] OpenAPI schema served at `/fapi/openapi.json` + Swagger UI at `/fapi/docs`
- [ ] Health check at `/fapi/health` — checks Rust `__version__`, MySQL multiplexer

**Tests (`tests/test_phase44_fastapi_lane.py`):**
- [ ] App boots without Frappe context (`fastapi.testclient.TestClient`)
- [ ] `current_user` rejects bad auth (401)
- [ ] `current_user` accepts valid Frappe session cookie
- [ ] SSE endpoint streams events; client disconnect cleans up
- [ ] External lookup endpoint fans out N requests concurrently (timed)
- [ ] Invoice validate: bad GSTIN → 422 with Pydantic error detail
- [ ] Invoice validate: good payload → matches `tax_invoice_summary` output
- [ ] Middleware records to Scorekeeper on every request
- [ ] OpenAPI schema generates (smoke test)
- [ ] Update `tracker.md`

---

## Performance comparison (target)

| Endpoint | Frappe (Werkzeug) | FastAPI lane |
|----------|-------------------|--------------|
| Bulk lookup (100 × 3 sources) | ~30s (threadpool, blocks workers) | ~2s (asyncio.gather) |
| Invoice validation (500 lines) | ~25ms (Python loop) | ~2ms (Pydantic v2 + Rust) |
| SSE stream (1000 concurrent) | 1000 blocked workers | 1 event loop |
| Empty endpoint baseline | ~8ms (Frappe boot overhead) | ~0.5ms |

---

## Non-goals

- ❌ Not a Frappe replacement
- ❌ Not migrating any existing endpoints — only adding new ones
- ❌ Not exposing `frappe.get_doc`-style ORM access
- ❌ Not handling sessions, workflows, or doctype CRUD
- ❌ Not running async migrations or background jobs (Phase 26 worker owns that)

---

## Open questions

- **Should the FastAPI process see the Frappe site config?** Probably yes — read-only.
  Use `frappe.get_conf()` once at startup, cache; never call again.
- **Should we ship uvloop?** Yes on Linux; falls back to asyncio on macOS dev.
- **How to handle Frappe's CSRF token for state-changing endpoints?**
  Validate the same header `X-Frappe-CSRF-Token` against the Redis session store.

---

## Why this is Phase 44 and not Phase 1

A year ago, the natural answer to "Frappe is slow" would be "swap WSGI for ASGI."
With Phases 1–43 in place, that's no longer the right answer — most of the wins
are already extracted by Rust + Go. FastAPI is now a **surgical addition** for
specific async-native patterns, not a wholesale rewrite. That's why it lands at
the end, not the beginning.

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_rpg/frappe_rpg/docs/phase_4_citadel.md
# 🏰 Phase 4: The Citadel

> **Status:** ⬜ Planned
> **Focus:** Security, Sandboxing & Formal Verification
> **Prerequisites:** Phase 3 complete, MicroVM runtime operational

---

## 🎯 Phase Objective

Harden the system. Phase 4 transforms Frappe-RPG from a performance tool into a **security platform**. This phase delivers a safe sandbox for executing untrusted code, formal verification of the accounting ledger, secure token infrastructure, and end-to-end encryption for sensitive Frappe data.

---

## 📋 Tasks

### 🛡️ 4.1 — The Secure Sandbox

> **Goal:** Allow Frappe to safely execute user-submitted scripts (e.g., formulas, custom logic) in an isolated, resource-limited environment.

#### Architecture
```
Frappe Python
    ↓
frappe_rs.sandbox.spawn(code, timeout, memory_limit)
    ↓
Rust creates a Firecracker MicroVM (Phase 3)
    ↓
Code runs inside the VM with no network, no filesystem access
    ↓
Result returned to Frappe within timeout
```

#### Rust (`src/sandbox.rs`)

- [ ] **`spawn_sandbox(code: &str, lang: &str, timeout_ms: u64, memory_mb: u64) -> String`**
  - Launch a restricted MicroVM with the given code
  - Supported languages: Python, JavaScript (Node), Bash
  - Return `{stdout, stderr, exit_code, duration_ms}`

- [ ] **`validate_script(code: &str, lang: &str) -> bool`**
  - Static analysis of submitted code
  - Reject obvious dangerous patterns (network calls, file system access)
  - Returns `{safe: bool, reason: String}`

- [ ] **Resource Caps**
  - CPU time limit per execution
  - Memory hard limit (OOM kill)
  - No network interface in VM
  - Read-only rootfs

#### Frappe Integration
- [ ] DocType: `Sandbox Execution Log` — stores every execution with code, result, duration
- [ ] API: `POST /api/method/frappe_rpg.sandbox.run`
- [ ] Permission check: only specified roles can execute sandbox code

---

### 🔏 4.2 — The Ghost Auditor (Ledger Formal Verification)

> **Goal:** Use Rust to formally verify that ERPNext's General Ledger entries are mathematically correct and internally consistent.

#### What It Verifies

- [ ] **Double-entry balance**: For every GL Entry, `sum(debit) == sum(credit)` per transaction
- [ ] **Orphan detection**: Identify GL Entries not linked to any valid voucher
- [ ] **Temporal integrity**: Detect backdated entries that violate period-close rules
- [ ] **Account tree integrity**: Verify parent-child account balances reconcile correctly
- [ ] **Currency consistency**: Flag entries with mismatched currency codes

#### Rust (`src/auditor.rs`)

- [ ] **`verify_trial_balance(gl_entries_json: &str) -> String`**
  - Accept JSON array of GL entries
  - Return `{valid: bool, errors: Vec}`

- [ ] **`detect_orphan_entries(gl_entries_json: &str, vouchers_json: &str) -> String`**
  - Cross-reference GL with voucher list
  - Return list of unmatched entry names

- [ ] **`build_account_tree(accounts_json: &str, gl_entries_json: &str) -> String`**
  - Construct balance aggregate per account
  - Return tree with running balances

#### Frappe Integration
- [ ] DocType: `Audit Report` — stores verification results per period
- [ ] Scheduled job: Run Ghost Auditor nightly, flag discrepancies
- [ ] Alert system: Email Finance Manager if critical violations found

---

### 🔑 4.3 — Secure Token & Signing Infrastructure

> **Goal:** Replace Python-based JWT/token operations with blazing-fast, memory-safe Rust implementations.

#### Dependencies
```toml
jsonwebtoken = "9"
hmac = "0.12"
ed25519-dalek = "2"
```

#### Rust (`src/tokens.rs`)

- [ ] **`sign_jwt(claims_json: &str, secret: &str, expiry_secs: u64) -> String`**
  - Sign a JWT with HMAC-SHA256
  - Returns signed token string

- [ ] **`verify_jwt(token: &str, secret: &str) -> String`**
  - Verify and decode a JWT
  - Returns `{valid: bool, claims: JSON}` or error

- [ ] **`sign_ed25519(message: &str, private_key_hex: &str) -> String`**
  - Sign a message using Ed25519 (for API-to-API authentication)

- [ ] **`verify_ed25519(message: &str, sig_hex: &str, pub_key_hex: &str) -> bool`**
  - Verify an Ed25519 signature

- [ ] **`generate_keypair() -> (String, String)`**
  - Generate Ed25519 keypair
  - Returns `(private_key_hex, public_key_hex)`

---

### 🔒 4.4 — Encryption at Rest

> **Goal:** Allow Frappe to encrypt sensitive DocType fields at the database level using Rust's AES-GCM implementation.

- [ ] Design encryption key management strategy (per-site master key from environment)
- [ ] **`encrypt_field(value: &str, master_key_hex: &str, field_name: &str) -> String`**
  - Derive a field-specific key from the master key (HKDF)
  - Encrypt with AES-256-GCM
  - Return base64 ciphertext

- [ ] **`decrypt_field(ciphertext_b64: &str, master_key_hex: &str, field_name: &str) -> String`**
  - Reverse of the above

- [ ] Frappe hook: Auto-encrypt/decrypt `Encrypted Data` field type on save/load
- [ ] DocType: `Encrypted Field Registry` — tracks which fields are encrypted

---

### 🛂 4.5 — Security Audit Logging (Go Sidecar)

> **Goal:** Use the Go sidecar to stream Frappe security events to an immutable audit log.

- [ ] Go: Intercept and log all events from `frappe:security:*` Redis channels
- [ ] Events to capture: Failed logins, permission errors, sudo calls, API key usage
- [ ] Store in append-only format (no delete, no update)
- [ ] Expose `/audit/query` HTTP endpoint for filtered log search
- [ ] Optional: Forward to external SIEM (Elastic/Splunk) via syslog

---

### ✅ 4.6 — Phase 4 Validation

- [ ] Sandbox execution returns correct output for a Python script in < 500ms
- [ ] Sandbox cannot access network or filesystem (verify with test scripts)
- [ ] Ghost Auditor correctly identifies a seeded double-entry error
- [ ] JWT round-trip (sign → verify) works from Frappe Python
- [ ] Encrypted field survives DB save/reload cycle correctly
- [ ] Security audit log captures failed login event

---

## 📚 Resources

- [Firecracker Security Model](https://github.com/firecracker-microvm/firecracker/blob/main/docs/security-policy.md)
- [jsonwebtoken Rust crate](https://docs.rs/jsonwebtoken/latest/jsonwebtoken/)
- [ed25519-dalek docs](https://docs.rs/ed25519-dalek/latest/ed25519_dalek/)
- [HKDF RFC 5869](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc5869)
- [Frappe Double-Entry Accounting](https://frappeframework.com/docs/user/en/accounting)

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_rpg/frappe_rpg/docs/phase_5_observatory.md
# 🔭 Phase 5: The Observatory

> **Status:** ✅ Complete
> **Focus:** Observability, Real-Time Monitoring & Event Streaming
> **Prerequisites:** Phase 4 complete, Go sidecar and security layer operational

---

## 📋 What Was Built

### 📡 5.1 — The Metrics Collector (Rust + Go)

#### Rust (`src/metrics.rs`) — ✅ Complete
- **`collect_worker_metrics() -> String`**: Scans all OS processes for `gunicorn` and `rq:worker` signatures via `sysinfo`. Returns JSON array with `process_count`, `total_memory_mb`, and `total_cpu_usage` per worker group.

#### Go (`go/sidecar/server/metrics.go`) — ✅ Complete
- **`MetricsHandler`**: HTTP handler at `/metrics` serving Prometheus OpenMetrics format.
- Queries `Rdb` (shared Redis pool) for `rq:queue:default/short/long` queue lengths.
- Reports `frappe_redis_up`, `frappe_redis_latency_seconds`, `frappe_worker_queue_length`.

**Frappe API:** `frappe_rpg.api.collect_worker_metrics()`

---

### 📰 5.2 — The Log Parser (Rust) — ✅ Complete

**Dependency:** `rayon = "1.10"` for parallel line iteration.

#### Rust (`src/log_parser.rs`)
- **`parse_frappe_errors(log_path)`**: Parallel-greps Frappe worker logs for `ERROR`/`CRITICAL` and `Traceback` lines. Structured JSON output with `line_number`, `timestamp`, `severity`, `message`.
- **`parse_nginx_access_log(log_path)`**: Tokenizes standard Nginx combined log format. Returns only `4xx`/`5xx` rows as JSON.

**Benchmark:** 10,000 line log parsed in **~12ms** using Rayon across 14 cores.

**Frappe API:**
```python
frappe_rpg.api.scan_frappe_errors(log_path="./logs/frappe.log")
frappe_rpg.api.scan_nginx_access_log(log_path="./logs/nginx-access.log")
```

---

### 📺 5.3 — The Event Stream & Webhook Broadcaster (Go) — ✅ Complete

#### Go (`go/sidecar/server/webhook.go`)
- **`WebhookQueue`**: Buffered Go channel (1000 capacity) for zero-blocking event injection.
- **`StartWebhookDispatcher()`**: Background goroutine consuming the channel and HTTP-POSTing to `AETHERLINK_WEBHOOK_URL` env variable asynchronously.
- **`DispatchWebhook(url, payload)`**: 3-second timeout HTTP client, Slack-compatible JSON envelope `{"text": "🚨 AETHERLINK ALERT [channel]\npayload"}`.

**Redis Integration:** The existing `redis.go` PubSub listener now fans all `frappe:security:*` events into `WebhookQueue` immediately after recording them to the immutable Audit Ring Buffer.

---

### ✅ 5.6 — Validation Results

| Test | Result | Latency |
|------|--------|---------|
| Frappe log parse (10k lines) | ✅ Pass | **12ms** |
| Nginx log parse (10k lines) | ✅ Pass | **17ms** |
| Rust worker metrics collection | ✅ Pass | <1ms |
| Go Prometheus `/metrics` format | ✅ Pass (requires `bench start`) | — |
| Webhook Redis trap + dispatch | ✅ Gracefully skipped (no Redis) | — |

---

## 📚 Key Files

| File | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `rust/src/log_parser.rs` | Rayon parallel log scanner |
| `rust/src/metrics.rs` | Frappe worker process extractor |
| `go/sidecar/server/metrics.go` | Prometheus OpenMetrics HTTP endpoint |
| `go/sidecar/server/webhook.go` | Async Redis→HTTP webhook broadcaster |
| `frappe_rpg/tests/test_phase5_log_parser.py` | Log parser E2E tests |
| `frappe_rpg/tests/test_phase5_metrics.py` | Metrics E2E tests |
| `frappe_rpg/tests/test_phase5_webhook.py` | Webhook broadcaster E2E tests |

# 🖥️ Phase 6: Andromeda — The Live Dashboard

> **Status:** ⬜ Planned
> **Focus:** Real-time Frappe monitoring dashboard powered by the full Phase 1–5 engine
> **Prerequisites:** Phase 5 complete — Observatory pipeline operational

---

## 🎯 Phase Objective

Build the **visual control layer** of Frappe-RPG. Phase 6 connects all the data streams, metrics, log parsers, and event buses built in previous phases into a beautiful, live Frappe Workspace page — no third-party tools required.

This is **Andromeda** — a native Frappe dashboard that rivals Grafana/Datadog for visibility over your own Frappe/ERPNext instance.

---

## 📋 Tasks

### 🎛️ 6.1 — The System Health Panel

> **Goal:** A live, auto-refreshing Frappe page showing OS-level and worker metrics.

- [ ] **Frappe Workspace Page:** `Andromeda Dashboard` (custom page)
- [ ] **CPU Gauge**: Pulls `frappe_rs.get_full_snapshot()` every 2 seconds
- [ ] **Memory Bar**: Used / Total MB for the Frappe server node
- [ ] **Worker Tiles**: Per-Gunicorn-process CPU + memory tile grid
- [ ] **Disk Usage Ring**: Per-mount usage ring charts
- [ ] **Data source:** Go `/metrics` endpoint → frappe API → Vue component

---

### 📰 6.2 — The Error Rate Chart

> **Goal:** Display Frappe log error density in real time — catch spikes before users notice.

- [ ] **Rolling 60-minute error chart** (bar chart per minute)
- [ ] **Data source:** `frappe_rs.parse_frappe_errors(log_path)` called every 30s
- [ ] **Spike alert badge**: Turns red if errors-per-minute exceed threshold
- [ ] **Click to drill down**: Opens a log snippet table showing the raw error lines

---

### 📡 6.3 — The Live Event Feed

> **Goal:** Show every meaningful Frappe action in real time — doc saves, job completions, login attempts.

- [ ] **WebSocket subscription** to Go sidecar `:7072`
- [ ] **Event types shown:** `doc.save`, `doc.submit`, `job.complete`, `frappe:security:*`
- [ ] **Scrolling feed table**: timestamp, event_type, user, doctype/payload
- [ ] **Severity color coding**: green = normal, amber = warning, red = security event
- [ ] **Auto-pause on hover**: Stop scroll so users can read entries

---

### 🔒 6.4 — The Security Audit Panel

> **Goal:** Surface Phase 4's Ghost Auditor and Security Ring Buffer visually.

- [ ] **Last 50 security events** queried from Go `/audit/query`
- [ ] **Trial Balance widget**: One-click `verify_trial_balance()` run with pass/fail badge
- [ ] **Orphaned Entry count** with a link to expand the list
- [ ] **Webhook Config**: UI to set `AETHERLINK_WEBHOOK_URL` from within Frappe

---

### 🛠️ 6.5 — Worker Queue Monitor

> **Goal:** Show background RQ queue depths and job latency without needing RQ Dashboard.

- [ ] **Redis Queue depth bars**: `default`, `short`, `long` queue sizes (from `/metrics`)
- [ ] **Failed Job count** from `frappe_rs.collect_worker_metrics()`
- [ ] **Queue latency estimate**: queue_depth × avg_job_duration
- [ ] **Manual drain button**: Calls `frappe.utils.background_jobs.get_jobs` and purges failed queue

---

### ✅ 6.6 — Phase 6 Validation

# 🌐 Phase 7: The Nexus — Distributed Scale

> **Status:** ⬜ Planned
> **Focus:** Making frappe_rpg production-grade at scale — multi-node, rate limiting, tracing, clustering
> **Prerequisites:** Phase 6 complete — Andromeda dashboard operational

---

## 🎯 Phase Objective

Phase 7 takes the Frappe-RPG stack from a **single-node powerhouse** to a **distributed, production-grade system**. Real ERPNext deployments are not single servers — they run across load-balanced workers, Redis clusters, and multiple database nodes.

The Nexus hardens the architecture for scale without sacrificing any of the speed or safety guarantees earned in Phases 1–6.

---

## 📋 Tasks

### ⚡ 7.1 — Rust API Rate Limiter

> **Goal:** Replace Frappe's Python-based rate limiter with a zero-latency Rust implementation.

#### Rust (`src/rate_limiter.rs`)

- [ ] **Token Bucket algorithm** in Rust with `DashMap` for concurrent per-IP/per-user tracking
- [ ] **`check_rate_limit(key, max_requests, window_secs)`** — returns `(allowed: bool, remaining: u32, reset_at: u64)`
- [ ] **Sliding window variant**: More accurate than fixed-window for burst traffic
- [ ] **Frappe integration**: Frappe `before_request` hook calls Rust rate limiter
- [ ] **Auto-whitelist**: Configured API keys bypass limit check

#### Benchmark Target
- Python `ratelimit` lib: ~50µs per check
- Rust `DashMap` + token bucket: ** **Goal:** See the full request lifecycle — from Python hook through Rust function to Go sidecar response.

- [ ] **Span generation in Rust**: Each `frappe_rs.*` call generates a trace span with timing
- [ ] **Trace context propagation**: Go sidecar reads `traceparent` header and appends child spans
- [ ] **Frappe Python bridge**: `frappe_rpg.tracing.start_span()` and `end_span()` context manager
- [ ] **OTLP exporter**: Ships traces to any OpenTelemetry-compatible collector (Jaeger, Tempo)
- [ ] **Andromeda integration**: Phase 6 dashboard shows slowest traces panel

---

### 🛰️ 7.3 — Redis Cluster Awareness

> **Goal:** The Go scout sidecar must handle Redis Sentinel and Redis Cluster setups correctly.

- [ ] **Sentinel support** in `go/sidecar/server/redis.go`: auto-failover to replica on master down
- [ ] **Cluster mode sharding**: Route pub/sub and queue keys to correct slot
- [ ] **Connection pool health monitor**: Detect stale connections and reconnect automatically
- [ ] **Multi-site mode**: Each Frappe site gets its own Redis channel namespace (`frappe::*`)

---

### 🏗️ 7.4 — Multi-Worker Coordination

> **Goal:** All Gunicorn workers should share state without race conditions.

- [ ] **Atomic counters in Rust**: Use `AtomicU64` with `Arc` sharing for cross-worker request counts
- [ ] **Shared memory segment**: Rust memory-mapped file for ultra-fast worker → worker signalling
- [ ] **Leader election**: Go sidecar elects a "leader worker" via Redis lock for singleton tasks (e.g., metric snapshot)

---

### 🔐 7.5 — mTLS Between Go Sidecars

> **Goal:** All Go sidecar-to-sidecar communication is encrypted and mutually authenticated.

- [ ] **Certificate Authority**: Lightweight Rust-generated CA per deployment
- [ ] **Auto-cert provisioning**: Each scout binary generates its own cert on first boot, signed by the CA
- [ ] **gRPC mTLS**: All gRPC streams between Go sidecars require mutual certificate verification
- [ ] **Key rotation**: Automatic cert renewal every 24 hours without restart

---

### ✅ 7.6 — Phase 7 Validation

- [ ] Rate limiter rejects > 100 req/s per IP and allows whitelisted keys through
- [ ] Distributed trace visible in Jaeger from a single `bench` request end-to-end
- [ ] Redis Sentinel failover handled without Frappe restart
- [ ] Go sidecars communicate with mTLS on a fresh install of `bench install-app frappe_rpg`

---

## 📚 Resources

- [OpenTelemetry Rust SDK](https://docs.rs/opentelemetry/latest/opentelemetry/)
- [DashMap concurrent HashMap](https://docs.rs/dashmap/latest/dashmap/)
- [go-redis Sentinel support](https://pkg.go.dev/github.com/go-redis/redis/v8#NewFailoverClient)
- [gRPC mTLS in Go](https://grpc.io/docs/guides/auth/)

---

# 🧰 Phase 8: The Arsenal — SDK, CLI & Developer Tooling

> **Status:** ⬜ Planned
> **Focus:** Making frappe_rpg a first-class developer platform — SDK, CLI, scaffolding tools, app marketplace
> **Prerequisites:** Phase 7 complete — production hardening done

---

## 🎯 Phase Objective

Phase 8 transforms Frappe-RPG from an **internal performance layer** into a **developer ecosystem**. Other Frappe apps — including your own custom apps, ERPNext modules, and third-party additions — should be able to import and consume `frappe_rpg` as a batteries-included SDK.

The Arsenal delivers the tools, CLIs, and scaffolding that make Frappe-RPG the obvious choice for any serious Frappe developer.

---

## 📋 Tasks

### 📦 8.1 — The frappe_rpg Python SDK

> **Goal:** Any Frappe app can `import frappe_rpg` and instantly get Rust-level performance without knowing Rust.

- [ ] **Stable public API**: Freeze and document `frappe_rpg.api.*` as a versioned SDK
- [ ] **Type stubs (`.pyi` files)**: Full Python type hints for all `frappe_rs.*` functions — IDE autocomplete works out of the box
- [ ] **Lazy import guards**: `frappe_rs` falls back to pure Python equivalents if the `.so` is not compiled — graceful degradation
- [ ] **SDK versioning**: `frappe_rpg.__version__` + `frappe_rs.__version__` reported together
- [ ] **Frappe app installer hook**: `install.py` auto-runs `maturin develop` if Rust is present

---

### 🔧 8.2 — The frappe-rpg CLI Tool

> **Goal:** A single command-line tool for benchmarking, profiling, and introspecting any Frappe installation.

#### Commands

```bash
# System health snapshot
frappe-rpg snapshot

# Parse and summarise the last 500 errors in frappe.log
frappe-rpg logs parse --last 500 --site mysite.local

# Benchmark a whitelisted API against Python baseline
frappe-rpg bench frappe_rpg.api.compute_stock_ledger --file data.csv --runs 10

# Verify the trial balance of a Frappe company
frappe-rpg audit verify-balance --company "Acme Corp" --site mysite.local

# Check Go sidecar health
frappe-rpg scout status
frappe-rpg scout restart
```

- [ ] **CLI framework**: Built in Python with `click` — ships as a `frappe_rpg` console script
- [ ] **Rust sub-commands**: Heavy commands (benchmark, log parse) call `frappe_rs` in-process
- [ ] **Output formats**: `--format json|table|csv` for all commands

---

### 🏗️ 8.3 — Rust Module Scaffolding Generator

> **Goal:** Let developers add a new Rust module to `frappe_rpg` without knowing PyO3 boilerplate.

```bash
frappe-rpg generate module my_module --functions "fast_validate, parallel_map"
```

- [ ] Generates `rust/src/my_module.rs` with PyO3 boilerplate and doc comments
- [ ] Inserts `mod my_module;` into `lib.rs` automatically
- [ ] Adds a Python wrapper skeleton to `frappe_rpg/api.py`
- [ ] Creates `frappe_rpg/tests/test_my_module.py` with pass/fail assertion template
- [ ] Runs `maturin develop` to verify the scaffolded module compiles cleanly

---

### 🧪 8.4 — Benchmark Registry

> **Goal:** A persistent, queryable log of all frappe_rpg performance benchmarks over time.

- [ ] **DocType: `RPG Benchmark`** — stores function name, duration_ms, input_size, date
- [ ] **`frappe-rpg bench` writes results** to this DocType after each run
- [ ] **Frappe Report: Performance Trends** — chart of benchmark durations over time
- [ ] **Regression detector**: Alert if a benchmark is >20% slower than its 30-day average
- [ ] **Exportable**: `frappe-rpg bench export --format csv` for CI/CD integration

---

### 📖 8.5 — Public Documentation Site

> **Goal:** A standalone docs site for frappe_rpg that developers outside the project can reference.

- [ ] **Auto-generate API reference** from Python docstrings + Rust `///` comments
- [ ] **Interactive examples**: Code snippets runnable directly in the docs page (via Pyodide or similar)
- [ ] **Integration guides**: ERPNext, f_watcher, Andromeda, Sagar — with copy-paste examples
- [ ] **Changelog**: One-command `frappe-rpg changelog` generates a formatted release log from git history

---

### ✅ 8.6 — Phase 8 Validation

- [ ] `pip install frappe-rpg` on a vanilla Frappe instance works without compilation errors
- [ ] `frappe-rpg snapshot` returns valid JSON on macOS and Linux
- [ ] Scaffolding generator produces a compilable Rust module in < 5 seconds
- [ ] Benchmark Registry DocType saves results and the Frappe Report renders correctly
- [ ] Type stubs enable full IDE autocomplete for all public `frappe_rs.*` functions

---

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_rpg/frappe_rpg/docs/phase_9_accelerator.md
# 🚀 Phase 9: The Accelerator — Query, HTTP & Frontend Speed Layer

> **Status:** ⬜ Planned
> **Focus:** Making `frappe.get_doc()`, list views, and the browser UI genuinely faster — without changing ERPNext/Frappe business logic
> **Prerequisites:** Phase 7 complete — distributed infrastructure hardened

---

## 🎯 Phase Objective

Phases 1–5 made **Python's own computation** faster. Phase 9 tackles the two layers we previously said were out of reach:

1. **The Database Layer** — `frappe.get_list()`, `frappe.get_doc()`, SQL queries
2. **The HTTP & Frontend Layer** — page loads, API round-trips, browser rendering

The strategy is a combination of a **Rust in-process cache**, a **Go smart proxy**, and **Rust compiled to WebAssembly** running directly in the browser.

---

## 📋 Tasks

### 🦀 9.1 — The Rust Document Cache (`moka` LRU)

> **Goal:** Make repeated `frappe.get_doc()` calls return from RAM instead of MariaDB — zero SQL overhead.

#### Rust (`src/doc_cache.rs`)

- [ ] **`cache_doc(doctype, name, json, ttl_secs)`** — store a serialized document in a thread-safe in-process LRU cache
- [ ] **`get_cached_doc(doctype, name)`** — return cached JSON if fresh, `None` if expired/missing
- [ ] **`invalidate_doc(doctype, name)`** — called on `doc_events.after_save` to evict stale entries
- [ ] **`cache_stats()`** — return hit rate, total entries, memory usage in bytes

#### Cache Design
- Library: **`moka`** — purpose-built Rust concurrent cache with TTL + size eviction
- Keyed by `"{doctype}:{name}"` — identical to Frappe's Redis doc cache pattern
- Max size: configurable, default 5,000 entries / 256MB

#### Frappe Integration
```python
# frappe_rpg/api.py
def get_cached_doc(doctype, name, ttl=30):
    cached = frappe_rs.get_cached_doc(doctype, name)
    if cached:
        return json.loads(cached)   # ← no SQL, ~0.01ms
    doc = frappe.get_doc(doctype, name).as_dict()
    frappe_rs.cache_doc(doctype, name, json.dumps(doc), ttl)
    return doc
```

#### Frappe Hook
```python
# hooks.py
doc_events = {
    "*": {
        "after_save": "frappe_rpg.cache.invalidate_doc"
    }
}
```

#### Benchmark Target
| | Standard `frappe.get_doc()` | With Rust cache (hit) |
|--|--|--|
| Cold (first call) | ~8ms (SQL + Python) | ~8ms (SQL + store) |
| Warm (repeated call) | ~8ms (SQL every time) | **~0.01ms (RAM)** |

---

### 🦀 9.2 — Parallel Multi-DocType Fetcher

> **Goal:** Fetch data from multiple DocTypes simultaneously using Rust threads instead of sequentially in Python.

#### Rust (`src/parallel_fetch.rs`)

- [ ] **`parallel_db_fetch(queries: Vec)`** — executes N SQL queries in parallel Rayon threads against a `mysql_async` pool, returns results as JSON array
- [ ] **Connection pool**: `mysql_async` persistent pool — eliminates per-query TCP handshake overhead

#### Python Usage
```python
from frappe_rpg.api import parallel_db_fetch

results = parallel_db_fetch([
    ("SELECT name, customer_name FROM `tabCustomer` WHERE territory=%s", ["Mumbai"]),
    ("SELECT name, item_name FROM `tabItem` WHERE item_group=%s", ["Products"]),
    ("SELECT name, grand_total FROM `tabSales Order` WHERE status=%s", ["Submitted"]),
])
# Returns all 3 in parallel — total time = max(q1, q2, q3) not sum
```

**Benchmark Target:** 3 × 200ms queries → **~210ms** instead of ~600ms

---

### 🏹 9.3 — Go Smart Reverse Proxy (`AetherProxy`)

> **Goal:** A lightweight Go HTTP proxy in front of Gunicorn with intelligent caching, coalescing, and HTTP/2.

#### Go (`go/proxy/main.go`)

- [ ] **Response Cache**: Cache `GET /api/resource/*` and `GET /api/method/*` (read-only methods) with configurable TTL per route
- [ ] **Request Coalescing (singleflight)**: If 50 requests arrive for the same endpoint concurrently, only ONE hits Gunicorn — all 50 get the same response
- [ ] **HTTP/2 Upstream**: Speak HTTP/2 to Nginx + use connection multiplexing — multiple requests on one TCP connection
- [ ] **Brotli Compression**: Compress API JSON responses using Brotli (15–30% smaller than gzip, faster to decompress in browser)
- [ ] **Cache Invalidation via Redis**: When `frappe_rpg.cache.invalidate_doc` fires, it also publishes to `frappe:cache:invalidate` — the proxy subscribes and evicts its own cache entry instantly

#### Route Configuration (`proxy_config.json`)
```json
{
  "routes": [
    { "pattern": "/api/resource/Customer/*", "ttl_seconds": 30 },
    { "pattern": "/api/method/frappe.desk.form.load.getdoc*", "ttl_seconds": 10 },
    { "pattern": "/api/method/frappe.client.get_list*", "ttl_seconds": 15 }
  ]
}
```

#### Impact
| Scenario | Before (Gunicorn) | After (AetherProxy) |
|----------|-------------------|---------------------|
| 50 users load same Customer doc | 50 SQL queries | **1 SQL query** |
| List view refresh (500 rows) | ~300ms | **~5ms from cache** |
| Dashboard with 8 API calls | ~1200ms sequential | **~180ms parallel + cached** |

---

### 🌐 9.4 — Frontend: Rust → WebAssembly (WASM) for Browser Computation

> **Goal:** Compile heavy Frappe client-side logic to WASM so the browser CPU does the work, not the server. Eliminates entire categories of round-trips.

#### What Gets Compiled to WASM

- [ ] **`wasm_validate_form(schema, values)`** — runs Frappe field validation (mandatory, min/max, regex) entirely in the browser with zero API calls
- [ ] **`wasm_aggregate_report(rows, group_by, aggregate)`** — run simple report totals/subtotals in the browser on already-fetched data
- [ ] **`wasm_diff_doc(old_json, new_json)`** — produce a minimal diff of document fields changed since last save — only send changed fields to the server

#### Build Pipeline
```bash
# Compile Rust to WASM
cargo build --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --release
wasm-bindgen --target web --out-dir frappe_rpg/public/js/wasm/ ...
```

#### Frappe Integration
```javascript
// frappe_rpg/public/js/frappe_rs_wasm.js
import init, { wasm_validate_form } from './wasm/frappe_rs.js';

frappe.ui.form.on('Sales Order', {
    validate: async function(frm) {
        await init();
        // Runs in browser — no API call, no Gunicorn worker touched
        const errors = wasm_validate_form(
            JSON.stringify(frm.meta.fields),
            JSON.stringify(frm.doc)
        );
        if (errors.length) throw errors.join(', ');
    }
});
```

#### Impact
| Action | Before | After |
|--------|--------|-------|
| Form field validation | ~50ms (API round-trip) | **~0.2ms (WASM in browser)** |
| Report sub-totals | Server-computed, ~300ms | **~5ms (browser WASM)** |
| Save payload size | Full doc JSON (~20KB) | **Diff only (~0.3KB)** |

---

### 🌐 9.5 — Frontend: Go Service Worker + Asset Acceleration

> **Goal:** Serve Frappe's JS/CSS assets smarter, reducing page load time significantly.

#### Go-Generated Service Worker
- [ ] **`/sw.js` from Go**: The Go proxy generates and serves a custom Service Worker script that knows which API routes to cache in `IndexedDB`
- [ ] **Offline-first list views**: The Service Worker caches the last-fetched data for key doctypes — if the network is slow, it serves stale data instantly and refreshes in background
- [ ] **Background sync**: Form drafts saved locally when offline, synced to Frappe when connectivity returns

#### Asset Optimization (Go Proxy)
- [ ] **Aggressive caching headers**: Set `Cache-Control: immutable, max-age=31536000` on versioned Frappe JS bundles — browser never re-downloads unchanged files
- [ ] **Brotli pre-compression**: Go pre-compresses `frappe.min.js` (typically ~2MB) to Brotli once on startup — serves 1.3MB instead (35% reduction), decompresses faster than gzip in Chrome
- [ ] **HTTP/2 Server Push**: When the browser loads `/app`, Go pushes `frappe.min.js`, `frappe.css`, and the desk icons before the browser even parses the HTML

#### Frontend Rendering — Virtual DOM (`wasm_diff`)
- [ ] **`wasm_diff_vdom(old_html, new_html)`**: Rust WASM virtual DOM differ — identifies only the changed nodes between two rendered Frappe form states and patches the DOM surgically
- [ ] **Target:** Large Frappe forms (100+ fields) currently re-render the entire form on `refresh()`. WASM diff reduces DOM operations by ~90%

#### Benchmark Targets
| | Standard Frappe | With Phase 9 |
|--|--|--|
| Cold page load (Frappe desk) | ~3.2s | **~1.4s** (Brotli + HTTP/2 push) |
| Repeat page load | ~1.8s | **~0.2s** (Service Worker cache) |
| Form field validation API call | ~50ms | **~0.2ms** (WASM) |
| Form re-render (100 fields) | ~120ms | **~15ms** (WASM diff) |

---

### 🔧 9.6 — Updated Philosophy Correction

After Phase 9, the philosophy statement changes:

**Before Phase 9:**
> `frappe.get_doc()` is SQL-bound — frappe_rpg cannot help.

**After Phase 9:**
> frappe_rpg accelerates `frappe.get_doc()` via in-process Rust LRU caching, AetherProxy response caching, and parallel query execution — **turning most repeated reads from ~8ms SQL queries into ~0.01ms RAM lookups.**

---

### ✅ 9.7 — Phase 9 Validation

- [ ] `get_cached_doc()` returns in < 0.1ms on cache hit, confirmed with benchmark
- [ ] `parallel_db_fetch()` of 3 queries runs faster than sequential sum of their durations
- [ ] AetherProxy: 50 concurrent requests for the same doc generate exactly 1 upstream Gunicorn request
- [ ] WASM form validation runs in browser with zero network requests
- [ ] Page load time (cold) measured < 2s on standard Frappe desk with Go proxy active
- [ ] Brotli-compressed `frappe.min.js` verified smaller than gzip equivalent

---

## 📚 Resources

- [moka: high-performance concurrent cache for Rust](https://docs.rs/moka/latest/moka/)
- [mysql_async: async MySQL driver for Rust](https://docs.rs/mysql_async/latest/mysql_async/)
- [Go singleflight: request coalescing](https://pkg.go.dev/golang.org/x/sync/singleflight)
- [wasm-bindgen: Rust to WebAssembly](https://rustwasm.github.io/wasm-bindgen/)
- [Brotli in Go](https://pkg.go.dev/github.com/andybalholm/brotli)
- [Service Workers API](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Service_Worker_API)

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_rpg/frappe_rpg/docs/philosophy.md
# ⚔️ Frappe-RPG: Philosophy

> *"Every great system is built on a philosophy — ours is speed, safety, and clarity."*
> — BluePhoenix

---

## 🌌 The Core Belief

Frappe is one of the most versatile full-stack Python frameworks ever built. But like all Python-based systems, it eventually hits a wall — the wall of **raw computational power**, **true concurrency**, and **systems-level access**.

**Frappe-RPG** (Rust · Python/PyO3 · Go) exists to break through that wall — not by replacing Frappe, but by **arming it**.

---

## 🎭 The RPG Metaphor: Each Language Has a Role

| Role | Language | Responsibility |
|------|----------|----------------|
| 🗺️ **Quest Master** | Python / Frappe | Orchestration, business logic, UI, Doctypes |
| ⚔️ **Warrior** | Rust (via PyO3) | CPU-bound heavy lifting, cryptography, parsing, analytics |
| 🏹 **Scout** | Go (Sidecars) | I/O concurrency, WebSockets, gRPC, real-time streams |

No single language is "better." Each plays its role in the system. The philosophy is **right tool, right job**.

---

## 🧭 Guiding Principles

### 1. Augment, Never Replace
Frappe-RPG does not fork Frappe. It does not patch the core. It operates **alongside** Frappe using clean extension points — Frappe hooks, Python imports, and background jobs.

### 2. Safety First (The Rust Way)
Rust's ownership model eliminates entire categories of bugs before they exist. Memory leaks, data races, null pointer dereferences — all compile-time errors. In a production ERP system, safety is not optional.

### 3. Zero Overhead Abstractions
When we call a Rust function from Python, the only overhead is the PyO3 bridge — no REST APIs, no serialization round-trips, no process spawning. The result is returned **directly in memory** to the Python interpreter.

### 4. Concurrency Without Fear
Python's GIL is a known constraint. Rust's `rayon` and Go's goroutines let us bypass it entirely for the tasks that matter. A report that takes 30 seconds in Python can run in milliseconds when parallelized in Rust.

### 5. Minimal Footprint
Frappe-RPG is a **library and extension layer**, not a platform. It should add capability, not complexity. Every module is optional. Use what you need.

### 6. Developer Joy
Languages should feel like choosing a weapon for a quest — exciting, purposeful, and empowering. Writing Rust inside a Frappe app should feel **powerful**, not painful.

---

## 🏗️ Why This Architecture?

### The Problem With Pure Python
- CPU-bound tasks block the Gunicorn worker
- Large data sets exhaust memory before they finish
- The GIL prevents true parallelism
- No native access to system metrics or binary protocols

### The Problem With Microservices-Everything
- Too many network hops kill latency
- Service coordination becomes its own burden
- Debugging distributed systems is harder than debugging monoliths

### The Frappe-RPG Solution
- **Rust (in-process)**: For hotpaths that cannot afford latency — heavy math, parsing, cryptography, analytics
- **Go (out-of-process sidecars)**: For long-lived, high-concurrency network tasks — WebSocket servers, gRPC bridges, metric collectors
- **Frappe (orchestrator)**: Remains the source of truth — Doctypes, permissions, workflows, events

---

## 🌱 The Bigger Vision

Frappe-RPG is the foundation of something larger:

- A **native analytics engine** that makes ERPNext reports run 100x faster
- A **secure execution runtime** for sandboxed third-party scripts
- An **observability backend** for Frappe-native monitoring tools
- A **real-time processing engine** for live dashboards and event streams
- The **performance core** of the entire Andromeda & Aether Suite

---

## ⚡ Does frappe_rpg Make ERPNext / Frappe APIs Faster?

**Short answer: Yes — for the right kinds of work. No — not automatically for everything.**

Here is a precise breakdown:

### ✅ What frappe_rpg Accelerates

| Workload | Standard Frappe | With frappe_rpg | Speedup |
|----------|-----------------|-----------------|---------|
| Report aggregation (sum, group-by) over 100k rows | `frappe.get_all()` → Python loop: ~30s | `frappe_rs.aggregate_sales_data()` via Polars: ~50ms | **~600x** |
| **`frappe.get_doc()` — repeated reads** | SQL every call: ~8ms | Rust `moka` LRU cache + Go proxy: **~0.01ms** | **~800x** (warm) |
| **Concurrent list view (50 users, same query)** | 50 SQL calls in Gunicorn | Go AetherProxy coalesces to 1 upstream call | **50x fewer DB hits** |
| Password hashing (Argon2) | Python `argon2-cffi`: ~650ms | Rust `argon2` crate: ~640ms | ~same (both wrap same C lib) |
| JSON validation & field extraction | Python `json.loads()` + dict access: ~5ms | Rust `serde_json`: ~0.3ms | **~15x** |
| Log file grep (10,000 lines) | Python loop: ~200ms | Rust Rayon parallel: ~12ms | **~16x** |
| AES-256-GCM encryption | Python `cryptography` lib:  frappe_rpg targets **every layer where Python, the network, or the browser is doing avoidable work**:
> - Python doing heavy loops → **Rust replaces them**
> - SQL called repeatedly for the same doc → **Rust + Go cache it**
> - 50 browser tabs hitting the same API → **Go coalesces to 1 upstream request**
> - Server validating forms the browser could validate → **WASM runs it client-side**
> - 2MB JS asset downloaded every visit → **Brotli + Service Worker caches it**

---

## 🧾 Summary

Frappe-RPG is not just a tech stack choice.

It is a **statement**: that open-source ERP systems deserve the same performance and safety guarantees as the most demanding systems software.

We believe the future of Frappe is **polyglot** — and Frappe-RPG is the first brave step into that future.

---

*Date: March 2026*
*Stack: Rust · Python · PyO3 · Go · Frappe Framework*

# 📜 Frappe-RPG: Master Plan

> **Stack:** Rust (PyO3) · Python · Go
> **Author:** Rahul Sarkar (BluePhoenix)
> **Status:** 🟢 Phases 1–28 Complete — Phases 29–43 Planned
> **Last Updated:** March 2026

---

## 🎯 Mission

To transform the Frappe Framework into an enterprise-grade, high-performance engine by:
- Offloading **CPU-bound** tasks to native **Rust** (via PyO3)
- Delegating **I/O-bound concurrency** to lightweight **Go** sidecars
- Keeping **Python/Frappe** as the clean orchestrator and business logic layer

---

## 🏛️ Architecture Overview

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│         Frappe Framework (Python)            │
│   DocTypes · Hooks · Background Jobs · UI   │
└────────────────┬────────────────────────────┘
                 │
     ┌───────────┴───────────┐
     │                       │
     ▼                       ▼
┌─────────────┐       ┌─────────────────┐
│  frappe_rs  │       │  Go Sidecars    │
│  (Rust/PyO3)│       │  WebSocket      │
│  CPU-bound  │       │  gRPC · Streams │
│  In-process │       │  Out-of-process │
└─────────────┘       └─────────────────┘
```

---

| Language | Role | Use Cases |
|----------|------|-----------|
| 🐍 Python/Frappe | Quest Master · Orchestrator | Business logic, Doctypes, workflows, UI, API |
| ⚔️ Rust (PyO3) | Warrior · Engine | Math, crypto, parsing, analytics, system info |
| 🏹 Go | Scout · Networker | WebSockets, gRPC, real-time streams, proxies |

## ⚡ The PyO3 Bridge: Quick Reference

### Rust Side (`rust/src/lib.rs`)
```rust
use pyo3::prelude::*;

#[pyfunction]
fn fast_sum(a: i64, b: i64) -> i64 {
    a + b
}

#[pymodule]
fn frappe_rs(_py: Python, m: &PyModule) -> PyResult {
    m.add_function(wrap_pyfunction!(fast_sum, m)?)?;
    Ok(())
}
```

### Python Side (`frappe_rpg/api.py`)
```python
from frappe_rs import fast_sum

def compute(a, b):
    return fast_sum(a, b)  # Native Rust speed, callable from anywhere in Frappe
```

  - `GenerateMutualTLS()` — ECDSA P-256, in-memory CA, `RequireAndVerifyClientCert`

# DOC: /Users/bluephoenix/frappe-bench/bench-r2/apps/frappe_rpg/frappe_rpg/docs/usecases.md
# Frappe-RPG: Real-World Use Cases

> **Version:** 0.15.0 · All 15 phases complete

This document describes concrete scenarios where Frappe-RPG delivers measurable value in production Frappe / ERPNext deployments. Each use case names the phases involved, states the problem, and shows the solution pattern.

---

## Table of Contents

1. [ERP Reporting at Scale](#1-erp-reporting-at-scale)
2. [Real-Time Financial Compliance Monitoring](#2-real-time-financial-compliance-monitoring)
3. [SaaS Multi-Tenant ERPNext Platform](#3-saas-multi-tenant-erpnext-platform)
4. [IoT-Connected Manufacturing ERP](#4-iot-connected-manufacturing-erp)
5. [Zero-Trust Security for a Regulated Industry](#5-zero-trust-security-for-a-regulated-industry)
6. [Predictive Maintenance & Capacity Planning](#6-predictive-maintenance--capacity-planning)
7. [High-Throughput E-Commerce Backend](#7-high-throughput-e-commerce-backend)
8. [GDPR-Compliant Multi-Country Deployment](#8-gdpr-compliant-multi-country-deployment)
9. [Production Operations & Incident Response](#9-production-operations--incident-response)
10. [Developer Tooling & CI/CD Pipeline](#10-developer-tooling--cicd-pipeline)
11. [Data Warehouse Integration](#11-data-warehouse-integration)
12. [API Partner Ecosystem](#12-api-partner-ecosystem)

---

## 1. ERP Reporting at Scale

**Phases:** 3, 9, 10, 13
**Problem:** A manufacturing company runs ERPNext with 5 million GL entries, 800k Sales Orders, and 200k Items. Monthly management reports take 45–90 minutes to generate because every report calls `frappe.db.get_list()` in a Python loop, then performs Python-level aggregation.

**Solution:**

```python
import frappe_rs as rs
import json

# Step 1: Fetch raw data in parallel across DocTypes
raw = json.loads(rs.parallel_fetch_docs(json.dumps([
    {"doctype": "GL Entry",    "filters": {"fiscal_year": "2025"}, "fields": ["*"]},
    {"doctype": "Sales Order", "filters": {"status": "Submitted"},  "fields": ["*"]},
])))

# Step 2: Run OLAP aggregations using DuckDB SQL
gl_json = json.dumps(raw["GL Entry"])

pnl = json.loads(rs.duckdb_query(gl_json, """
    SELECT
        account,
        SUM(debit)  AS total_debit,
        SUM(credit) AS total_credit,
        SUM(debit - credit) AS net
    FROM t
    WHERE posting_date BETWEEN '2025-01-01' AND '2025-12-31'
    GROUP BY account
    ORDER BY ABS(net) DESC
"""))

# Step 3: Export to Parquet for archive
rs.frappe_to_parquet(gl_json, "/backups/gl_2025_annual.parquet")

# Step 4: Cache hot DocType data for the next 5 minutes
for so in raw["Sales Order"]:
    rs.cache_doc("Sales Order", so["name"], json.dumps(so))
```

**Result:** 90-minute monthly report → **under 4 seconds**. Subsequent reads hit the Rust moka cache (~0.01ms per doc). The Parquet file is 85% smaller than CSV and can be queried directly next quarter.

---

## 2. Real-Time Financial Compliance Monitoring

**Phases:** 4, 5, 10, 12
**Problem:** A financial services firm using ERPNext must detect accounting anomalies within seconds of posting, not overnight in a batch job. The trial balance must be verified on every GL batch, and any anomaly must trigger an immutable signed audit event.

**Solution:**

```python
import frappe_rs as rs
import json
import frappe

# Frappe hook: fires after every GL Entry batch
def on_submit(doc, method):
    # 1. Verify trial balance in Rust (100k entries in ~0.25s)
    gl_entries = json.dumps(frappe.db.get_list(
        "GL Entry", filters={"voucher_no": doc.name}, fields=["debit", "credit", "account"]
    ))
    check = json.loads(rs.verify_trial_balance_json(gl_entries))

    if not check["valid"]:
        # 2. Classify the anomaly
        anomaly_text = str(check["anomalies"])
        classification = json.loads(rs.classify_failure(anomaly_text))

        # 3. Sign the anomaly report (HMAC request signing for audit trail)
        report = json.dumps({
            "voucher": doc.name,
            "anomalies": check["anomalies"],
            "classification": classification,
            "ts": frappe.utils.now_datetime().isoformat()
        })
        sig = rs.sign_request("POST", "/audit/anomaly", report, frappe.conf.get("audit_signing_key"))

        # 4. Store signed event in keymaster secrets store
        import urllib.request
        req = urllib.request.Request(
            "http://localhost:7075/keymaster/secrets/inject",
            data=json.dumps({"key": f"anomaly:{doc.name}", "value": report}).encode(),
            method="POST"
        )
        urllib.request.urlopen(req)

        frappe.throw(f"Trial balance anomaly detected in {doc.name}. Audit event logged.")
```

**Result:** Every GL batch is verified in real time. Anomalies are cryptographically signed and stored before the user sees the error, making them tamper-proof for regulatory review.

## 3. SaaS Multi-Tenant ERPNext Platform

**Phases:** 7, 9, 14
**Problem:** A SaaS provider runs 400 customer tenants on a shared ERPNext cluster. Several problems arise: one tenant's heavy report job starves all others; tenants exceed storage and API quotas without enforcement; data accidentally leaks across tenants in complex queries.

**Solution — Quota Enforcement (Frappe middleware):**

```python
import frappe_rs as rs
import json
import frappe

def on_api_call():
    """Frappe before_request hook."""
    site = frappe.local.site
    tenant_id = frappe.db.get_value("Tenant", {"site": site}, "name")

    # Consume 1 API call unit
    result = json.loads(rs.quota_consume(tenant_id, "api_calls", 1))
    if not result["allowed"]:
        frappe.throw(
            f"API quota exceeded. Limit resets in {result.get('retry_after', 3600)}s.",
            frappe.PermissionError
        )

    # Record metering (flushed to billing DB every 60s)
    rs.meter_record(tenant_id, "api_calls",    1.0)
    rs.meter_record(tenant_id, "bytes_served", len(frappe.local.response.get("message", "")))
```

**Solution — Fair Job Scheduling:**

```python
# Background job submission (replaces direct RQ enqueue)
def enqueue_report(tenant_id: str, report_params: dict):
    weight = frappe.db.get_value("Tenant", tenant_id, "job_weight") or 1.0
    rs.wfq_set_weight(tenant_id, weight)
    rs.wfq_enqueue(tenant_id, json.dumps({
        "job": "generate_report",
        "params": report_params
    }), priority=5)

# Worker (runs as a single Frappe scheduled job)
def process_fair_queue():
    while True:
        item = json.loads(rs.wfq_dequeue_fair())
        if item.get("empty"):
            break
        # Switch site context to tenant's site, then execute
        frappe.set_user("Administrator")
        execute_job(item["tenant"], item["job"])
```

**Solution — Data Isolation:**

```python
# Frappe has_permission hook
def has_permission(doc, ptype="read", user=None):
    user = user or frappe.session.user
    tenant_id = frappe.db.get_value("User", user, "tenant_id")
    result = json.loads(rs.isolation_check(doc.doctype, doc.name, tenant_id))
    return result["allowed"]

# Validate query filters before GET /api/resource
def before_list(doctype, filters):
    tenant_id = get_current_tenant()
    validation = json.loads(rs.isolation_validate_query(json.dumps(filters), tenant_id))
    if not validation["tenant_scoped"]:
        frappe.throw("All queries must be scoped to your tenant.")
```

**Result:** 400 tenants coexist with strict resource fairness. No single tenant can saturate the job queue. Quota violations are caught at = 0:

            events = json.dumps([{"ts": int(d["creation"].timestamp()), "name": d["name"]} for d in deploys])
            cps = json.dumps([{"ts": int(cp_idx * 10), "index": cp_idx}])  # 10s interval

                wo.description = f"Sensor {sensor['name']} anomaly at index {cp_idx}"
                wo.priority = "High"
                wo.insert(ignore_permissions=True)
                frappe.db.commit()
```

**Result:** Factory detects anomalies in sub-second time. Maintenance work orders are created automatically before failures occur. No LLM, no ML framework — pure statistical algorithms in Rust.

## 5. Zero-Trust Security for a Regulated Industry

**Phases:** 4, 7, 12
**Problem:** A healthcare company using ERPNext must comply with HIPAA. All API calls must be authenticated with short-lived tokens, all service-to-service calls must be cryptographically signed, and all session tokens must be revocable in real time.

**Solution — Short-Lived JWTs:**

```python
# Service A: Obtain a 15-minute token from the keymaster
import urllib.request, json

def get_service_token():
    req = urllib.request.Request(
        "http://keymaster:7075/keymaster/token",
        data=json.dumps({
            "user_id": "ehr-sync-service",
            "email": "ehr-sync@internal",
            "roles": ["EHR Sync"]
        }).encode(),
        method="POST"
    )
    resp = json.loads(urllib.request.urlopen(req).read())
    return resp["token"]

# Service B: Verify the incoming token on every request
def authenticate_request(request_headers):
    token = request_headers.get("Authorization", "").removeprefix("Bearer ")
    verify_req = urllib.request.Request(
        "http://keymaster:7075/keymaster/verify",
        headers={"Authorization": f"Bearer {token}"}
    )
    resp = json.loads(urllib.request.urlopen(verify_req).read())
    if not resp["valid"]:
        raise PermissionError("Invalid or expired token")
    return resp["sub"]  # user_id
```

**Solution — Request Signing (service-to-service):**

```python
import frappe_rs as rs

# Outgoing request from Frappe → EHR system
def post_patient_record(payload: dict):
    body = json.dumps(payload)
    sig_header = rs.sign_request("POST", "https://ehr.internal/api/records", body, SHARED_SECRET)

    import requests
    resp = requests.post(
        "https://ehr.internal/api/records",
        data=body,
        headers={
            "Content-Type": "application/json",
            "X-Signature": sig_header
        }
    )
    return resp.json()

# Incoming request validation at the EHR gateway
def validate_incoming(request):
    body = request.get_data(as_text=True)
    sig_header = request.headers.get("X-Signature", "")
    result = json.loads(rs.verify_request_signature(
        sig_header, request.method, request.url, body, SHARED_SECRET, max_age_secs=300
    ))
    if not result["valid"]:
        abort(401, result["reason"])
```

**Solution — Real-time Token Revocation:**

```python
# On logout or security incident: revoke all tokens immediately
def emergency_revoke_user(user_id: str):
    active_tokens = frappe.db.get_list("Active Token", filters={"user": user_id}, fields=["jti"])
    for token in active_tokens:
        revoke_req = urllib.request.Request(
            "http://keymaster:7075/keymaster/revoke",
            data=json.dumps({"jti": token["jti"]}).encode(),
            method="POST"
        )
        urllib.request.urlopen(revoke_req)  # Stored in Redis immediately
    frappe.db.delete("Active Token", {"user": user_id})
    frappe.db.commit()
```

**Result:** Every token lives at most 15 minutes. Service calls are signed and replay-protected. Compromised accounts are locked out within milliseconds. All of this runs without any external identity provider.

## 6. Predictive Maintenance & Capacity Planning

**Phases:** 10, 13
**Problem:** A data center operator running ERPNext Asset Management needs to predict disk failures, forecast infrastructure costs, and get early warnings before capacities are exceeded — without a dedicated data science team.

**Solution:**

```python
import frappe_rs as rs
import json
import frappe

def run_capacity_forecast():
    """Weekly scheduled job."""

    assets = frappe.db.get_list("Asset", filters={"asset_category": "Server"}, fields=["name"])

    for asset in assets:
        # Fetch last 90 days of disk usage readings from KV store
        readings_json = frappe.db.get_single_value("Asset Metric", f"disk_usage_{asset['name']}")
        if not readings_json:
            continue
        readings = json.loads(readings_json)

        # Holt-Winters forecast for next 30 days
        if len(readings) >= 24:
            best = json.loads(rs.auto_tune_params(readings, period=7))  # weekly seasonality
            model = json.loads(rs.holtwinters_fit(
                readings, alpha=best["alpha"], beta=best["beta"], gamma=best["gamma"], period=7
            ))
            forecast = json.loads(rs.holtwinters_forecast(json.dumps(model), horizon=30))

            # Check scaling signal
            projected_values = [f["value"] for f in forecast]
            signal = json.loads(rs.scale_signal(
                json.dumps(forecast), threshold_high=90.0, threshold_low=20.0
            ))

            # Resource runway
            capacity_info = json.loads(rs.resource_runway(
                current=readings[-1],
                capacity=100.0,
                growth_rate=(readings[-1] - readings[0]) / max(len(readings), 1) / 100
            ))

            if signal.get("action") == "scale_up" or capacity_info.get("days_to_full", 999)  dict:
    """Returns item from Rust cache or DB (800x speedup on warm hit)."""
    cached = rs.get_cached_doc("Item", item_code)
    if cached:
        return json.loads(cached)

    # Cache miss — fetch from DB and warm the cache
    item = frappe.get_doc("Item", item_code).as_dict()
    rs.cache_doc("Item", item_code, json.dumps(item))
    return item

# Cache invalidation hook — fires on Item save
def on_item_save(doc, method):
    rs.invalidate_doctype("Item")  # invalidates all cached items on update
```

**Solution — Rate Limiting:**

```python
def on_request():
    """Frappe before_request hook — protects against abuse."""
    user = frappe.session.user
    ip = frappe.local.request_ip

    # 200 requests/minute per user
    if not rs.check_rate_limit(f"user:{user}", capacity=200.0, refill_rate=3.33):
        remaining = rs.rate_limit_retry_after(f"user:{user}", 200.0, 3.33)
        frappe.local.response["http_status_code"] = 429
        frappe.local.response["message"] = f"Rate limited. Retry after {remaining:.0f}s."
        raise frappe.DoNotSaveError

    # 1000 requests/minute per IP (bot protection)
    if not rs.check_rate_limit(f"ip:{ip}", capacity=1000.0, refill_rate=16.7):
        frappe.local.response["http_status_code"] = 429
        raise frappe.DoNotSaveError
```

**Solution — Parallel Catalog Fetch (sale page load):**

```python
def get_flash_sale_catalog(item_codes: list) -> dict:
    """Fetch 50 items in parallel via Rust."""
    queries = [
        {"doctype": "Item",          "filters": {"item_code": ["in", item_codes]}, "fields": ["*"]},
        {"doctype": "Item Price",    "filters": {"item_code": ["in", item_codes]}, "fields": ["*"]},
        {"doctype": "Bin",           "filters": {"item_code": ["in", item_codes]}, "fields": ["actual_qty"]},
    ]
    return json.loads(rs.parallel_fetch_docs(json.dumps(queries)))
```

**Result:** Item page load drops from 650ms to 210ms (parallel fetch). Warm catalog reads: 0.01ms vs 8ms. Flash sales no longer cause worker exhaustion. Rate limiting adds  dict:
    """
    GDPR Article 20 — Right to data portability.
    Called from the HR portal when an employee requests their data.
    """
    # 1. Collect all personal data
    rs.gdpr_collect(employee_id, json.dumps(frappe.db.get_list(
        "Employee", filters={"name": employee_id}, fields=["*"]
    )))
    rs.gdpr_collect(employee_id, json.dumps(frappe.db.get_list(
        "Salary Slip", filters={"employee": employee_id}, fields=["*"]
    )))
    rs.gdpr_collect(employee_id, json.dumps(frappe.db.get_list(
        "Leave Allocation", filters={"employee": employee_id}, fields=["*"]
    )))
    rs.gdpr_collect(employee_id, json.dumps(frappe.db.get_list(
        "Attendance", filters={"employee": employee_id}, fields=["*"]
    )))

    # 2. AES-256-GCM encrypt the package
    export = json.loads(rs.gdpr_export(employee_id, ENCRYPTION_KEY))

    # 3. Ed25519 sign for audit trail
    signed = json.loads(rs.gdpr_sign_export(json.dumps(export), SIGNING_KEY))

    # 4. Store audit record
    audit = frappe.new_doc("GDPR Audit Log")
    audit.employee = employee_id
    audit.action = "data_export"
    audit.records_exported = export["records"]
    audit.signature_hex = signed["signature_hex"]
    audit.public_key_hex = signed["public_key_hex"]
    audit.payload_hash = signed["payload_hash"]
    audit.insert(ignore_permissions=True)
    frappe.db.commit()

    return {
        "ciphertext_b64": export["ciphertext_b64"],
        "nonce_b64": export["nonce_b64"],
        "signature": signed["signature_hex"],
        "records": export["records"]
    }

def handle_right_to_erasure(employee_id: str):
    """
    GDPR Article 17 — Right to erasure ("right to be forgotten").
    Deletes all personal data and logs the erasure.
    """
    # Anonymize Frappe records
    frappe.db.set_value("Employee", employee_id, {
        "first_name": "DELETED",
        "last_name": "DELETED",
        "personal_email": "deleted@example.com",
        "cell_number": "",
        "date_of_birth": None,
    })

    # Purge all collected GDPR data from the in-memory store
    result = json.loads(rs.gdpr_delete_tenant(employee_id))

    # Audit the erasure
    audit = frappe.new_doc("GDPR Audit Log")
    audit.employee = employee_id
    audit.action = "erasure"
    audit.records_erased = result["records_erased"]
    audit.insert(ignore_permissions=True)
    frappe.db.commit()
```

**Result:** GDPR data exports complete in under 2 seconds for any employee. Each export is encrypted and signed — the cryptographic signature proves it hasn't been tampered with if challenged in court. Erasure is instant and audited.

## 9. Production Operations & Incident Response

**Phases:** 5, 10, 15
**Problem:** A Frappe Cloud operator needs to detect incidents faster, understand root causes automatically, and run chaos tests before major releases. Their current monitoring is limited to Gunicorn worker counts and Redis queue depth.

**Solution — Automated Incident Detection:**

```python
import frappe_rs as rs
import json
import frappe

def run_operations_scan():
    """Scheduled every minute via Frappe Scheduler."""

    # 1. Parse the last 10,000 lines of Frappe error log
    errors = json.loads(rs.parse_frappe_errors("/home/frappe/logs/frappe-default.log"))
    error_messages = [e["message"] for e in errors]

    # 2. Cluster errors into templates (Drain algorithm)
    templates = json.loads(rs.drain_parse(error_messages, depth=3, similarity_threshold=0.5))
    top = json.loads(rs.top_templates(n=10))

    # 3. Detect change-points in error rate time series
    # Build a per-minute error count series from the last hour
    now = frappe.utils.now_datetime()
    error_rate_series = [
        frappe.db.count("Error Log", {"creation": ["between", [
            frappe.utils.add_to_date(now, minutes=-(i+1)),
            frappe.utils.add_to_date(now, minutes=-i)
        ]]})
        for i in range(60)
    ]
    error_rate_series.reverse()

    cp_idx = rs.cusum_detect(
        error_rate_series,
        target=sum(error_rate_series[:30]) / 30,  # baseline from first 30 min
        slack=2.0,
        threshold=10.0
    )

    if cp_idx >= 0:
        # 4. Build causality graph to find root cause
        event_log = json.dumps([{"template": t["pattern"], "ts": i * 60}
                                  for i, t in enumerate(top)])
        correlations = json.loads(rs.temporal_correlation(event_log, window_secs=300))
        causes = json.loads(rs.query_likely_causes(correlations, top[0]["pattern"] if top else ""))

        # 5. Create an Incident doctype with diagnosis
        incident = frappe.new_doc("Incident")
        incident.detected_at = frappe.utils.now_datetime()
        incident.trigger_template = top[0]["pattern"] if top else "Unknown"
        incident.probable_cause = causes[0]["cause"] if causes else "Unknown"
        incident.error_count = len(errors)
        incident.severity = "High" if len(errors) > 100 else "Medium"
        incident.insert(ignore_permissions=True)
        frappe.db.commit()
```

**Solution — Pre-Release Chaos Testing:**

```python
def pre_release_chaos_gate():
    """Run in CI before every production deploy."""
    import json

    # 1. Circuit breaker: ensure all external services recover correctly
    for service in ["payments-gateway", "email-relay", "ehr-api"]:
        # Simulate 10 failures
        for _ in range(10):
            rs.chaos_circuit_breaker(service, False, threshold=5, half_open_secs=0)
        # Verify circuit opened
        state = json.loads(rs.chaos_circuit_breaker(service, False, threshold=5, half_open_secs=0))
        assert state["state"] == "open", f"{service} circuit did not open!"
        # Reset with success
        rs.chaos_circuit_breaker(service, True, threshold=5, half_open_secs=0)

    # 2. Memory probe — ensure we have headroom for the deploy
    probe = json.loads(rs.chaos_resource_probe(alloc_mb=512))
    if probe["approx_rss_mb"] > 20_000:
        raise Exception("Insufficient memory headroom for deployment")

    # 3. Property checks at deployment scale (10k trials)
    suite = json.loads(rs.propcheck_suite(n_trials=10_000))
    assert suite["all_passed"], f"Property violations: {suite}"

    # 4. Production readiness audit
    config = frappe.conf.as_dict()
    audit = json.loads(rs.audit_production(json.dumps({
        "signing_secret": config.get("keymaster_secret", ""),
        "hsm_backend": rs.hsm_backend(),
        "token_ttl_secs": config.get("token_ttl_secs", 0),
        "redis_url": config.get("redis_cache", ""),
        "s3_backend": config.get("s3_backend", "memory"),
        "doc_cache_ttl_secs": config.get("doc_cache_ttl_secs", 0),
        "gdpr_tested": True,
        "isolation_enforced": True,
    })))

    if not audit["production_ready"]:
        failures = [i for i in audit["items"] if not i["passed"]]
        raise Exception(f"Production audit failed: {[f['id'] for f in failures]}")

    print("Chaos gate passed. Safe to deploy.")
```

**Result:** Incidents are detected and root-caused automatically. Circuit breakers prevent cascading failures. The pre-release chaos gate catches regressions before they reach production.
