**Solution — Benchmark Regression Guard:**

```python
import frappe_rs as rs
import json
import time

def benchmark_regression_gate():
    """Fails CI if any function regresses by >20%."""
    baselines = {
        "duckdb_query":       50.0,   # ms for 10k rows
        "pelt_changepoints":  5.0,    # ms for 1000 points
        "parallel_fetch_docs": 250.0, # ms for 3 DocTypes
    }

    for fn_name, baseline_ms in baselines.items():
        start = time.perf_counter()
        # ... call the function with standard input
        elapsed_ms = (time.perf_counter() - start) * 1000

        if elapsed_ms > baseline_ms * 1.2:
            raise Exception(
                f"Performance regression in {fn_name}: "
                f"{elapsed_ms:.1f}ms > {baseline_ms * 1.2:.1f}ms"
            )
    print("Benchmark gate passed.")
```

**Result:** New Rust modules are scaffolded in seconds. Breaking API changes are caught in CI before merge. Performance regressions are flagged automatically. Type stubs give every developer full IDE autocomplete on all ~400 Rust functions.

## 11. Data Warehouse Integration

**Phases:** 3, 10, 13
**Problem:** A retail chain running ERPNext wants to move their business intelligence from Excel to a proper analytical pipeline — but without a separate data warehouse team. They need daily exports to Parquet, SQL-level analytics on Frappe data, and automated anomaly alerts on sales trends.

**Solution:**

```python
import frappe_rs as rs
import json
import frappe
from datetime import date, timedelta

def daily_warehouse_export():
    """Frappe scheduled job — runs at 2 AM daily."""

    today = date.today().isoformat()
    yesterday = (date.today() - timedelta(days=1)).isoformat()

    # 1. Fetch yesterday's transactions in parallel
    raw = json.loads(rs.parallel_fetch_docs(json.dumps([
        {"doctype": "Sales Invoice",  "filters": {"posting_date": yesterday}, "fields": ["*"]},
        {"doctype": "Purchase Order", "filters": {"transaction_date": yesterday}, "fields": ["*"]},
        {"doctype": "Payment Entry",  "filters": {"posting_date": yesterday}, "fields": ["*"]},
        {"doctype": "Stock Entry",    "filters": {"posting_date": yesterday}, "fields": ["*"]},
    ])))

    # 2. Export each to Parquet (columnar, compressed, 85% smaller than CSV)
    for doctype_slug, doctype_name in [
        ("sales_invoice", "Sales Invoice"),
        ("purchase_order", "Purchase Order"),
        ("payment_entry", "Payment Entry"),
        ("stock_entry", "Stock Entry"),
    ]:
        data = json.dumps(raw[doctype_name])
        path = f"/warehouse/{today}/{doctype_slug}.parquet"
        result = json.loads(rs.frappe_to_parquet(data, path))
        frappe.logger().info(f"Exported {result['rows']} {doctype_name} rows → {path}")

    # 3. Run daily OLAP analysis
    si_json = json.dumps(raw["Sales Invoice"])

    # Top products by revenue
    top_products = json.loads(rs.duckdb_query(si_json, """
        SELECT item_code, SUM(net_total) AS revenue
        FROM t
        GROUP BY item_code
        ORDER BY revenue DESC
        LIMIT 20
    """))

    # Revenue by region
    by_region = json.loads(rs.duckdb_aggregate(si_json, "territory", "net_total", "sum"))

    # 4. Anomaly detection on daily revenue
    daily_revenues = frappe.db.get_list(
        "Sales Invoice",
        filters={"docstatus": 1},
        fields=["posting_date", "net_total"],
        group_by="posting_date",
        order_by="posting_date desc",
        limit=90
    )
    revenue_series = [float(r["net_total"]) for r in reversed(daily_revenues)]

    if len(revenue_series) >= 30:
        # CUSUM: detect if today's revenue is a change-point
        cp = rs.cusum_detect(revenue_series, target=sum(revenue_series[:30])/30, slack=0.1, threshold=5.0)

        if cp >= 0 and cp == len(revenue_series) - 1:
            # Today is the change-point — alert
            alert = frappe.new_doc("Notification Log")
            alert.subject = f"Revenue anomaly detected on {today}"
            alert.for_user = "Administrator"
            alert.insert(ignore_permissions=True)

    # 5. Scan historical Parquet with SQL (cross-day query without loading into Python)
    all_time_top = json.loads(rs.duckdb_scan_parquet(
        "/warehouse/2025-01-01/sales_invoice.parquet",
        "SELECT item_code, SUM(net_total) AS total FROM parquet GROUP BY item_code ORDER BY total DESC LIMIT 5"
    ))

    frappe.db.commit()
```

**Result:** Full data warehouse pipeline with zero external dependencies — no Spark, no Airflow, no separate warehouse cluster. Daily exports in Parquet are queryable with SQL. Anomalies are flagged the morning after they occur.

## 12. API Partner Ecosystem

**Phases:** 11, 12, 14
**Problem:** An ERPNext implementation exposes its data to 50 partner companies via a public API. Problems: partners abuse the API with no throttling; there is no GraphQL for flexible queries; requests from partners are not authenticated in a standard way.

**Solution — GraphQL for Partners:**

```bash
# Start the relay — auto-generates GraphQL schema from your Frappe DocTypes
FRAPPE_URL=http://localhost:8000 \
FRAPPE_API_KEY=your-key \
RELAY_PORT=7071 \
./relay_bin

# Partner queries only what they need — no over-fetching
curl -X POST https://api.yourcompany.com/graphql \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer " \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{
    "query": "{
      salesorder(status: \"Submitted\") {
        name
        customer
        grand_total
        delivery_date
      }
    }"
  }'
```

**Solution — Per-Partner Quotas:**

```python
import frappe_rs as rs
import json

# Provisioning: set quotas when onboarding a partner
def onboard_partner(partner_id: str, tier: str):
    tiers = {
        "starter":     {"api_calls": 1000,  "window": 86400},
        "professional": {"api_calls": 10000, "window": 86400},
        "enterprise":  {"api_calls": 100000, "window": 86400},
    }
    cfg = tiers[tier]
    rs.quota_set(partner_id, "api_calls",    cfg["api_calls"], cfg["window"])
    rs.quota_set(partner_id, "webhooks",     1000,  3600)
    rs.quota_set(partner_id, "bulk_exports", 10,    3600)
    rs.wfq_set_weight(partner_id, 3.0 if tier == "enterprise" else 1.0)

# Middleware: enforce on every partner API call
def partner_api_middleware(partner_id: str):
    result = json.loads(rs.quota_consume(partner_id, "api_calls", 1))
    if not result["allowed"]:
        return {
            "error": "QUOTA_EXCEEDED",
            "retry_after": result.get("retry_after"),
            "message": "API call quota exceeded. Upgrade your plan or wait for reset."
        }, 429

    # Record metering for billing
    rs.meter_record(partner_id, "api_calls", 1.0)

# Billing: generate invoice data
def generate_partner_invoice(partner_id: str) -> dict:
    snap = json.loads(rs.meter_snapshot(partner_id))
    rs.meter_reset(partner_id)  # reset for next billing cycle
    return {
        "partner": partner_id,
        "api_calls": snap["metrics"].get("api_calls", 0),
        "period": frappe.utils.getdate(),
    }
```

**Solution — Signed Webhook Delivery:**

```python
import frappe_rs as rs

def send_webhook(partner_id: str, url: str, event: dict):
    """Send a signed webhook so the partner can verify authenticity."""
    body = json.dumps(event)
    secret = frappe.db.get_value("API Partner", partner_id, "webhook_secret")

    sig_header = rs.sign_request("POST", url, body, secret)

    import requests
    resp = requests.post(url, data=body, headers={
        "Content-Type": "application/json",
        "X-Frappe-Signature": sig_header,
        "X-Partner-ID": partner_id,
    }, timeout=10)

    rs.meter_record(partner_id, "webhooks", 1.0)
    return resp.status_code
```

**Result:** Partners get a modern GraphQL API instead of raw REST. Every partner is rate-limited and billed precisely. Webhooks are signed — partners can verify authenticity without a shared secret management system. 50 partners operate independently with zero cross-contamination.

## 1. Setup & Import

### Install the Rust extension

```bash
cd apps/frappe_rpg
maturin develop --manifest-path rust/Cargo.toml
```

### Import patterns

```python
# Direct import — fastest, no wrapper overhead
import frappe_rs as rs

# Via the Python API layer — adds Frappe context (site, permissions, hooks)
from frappe_rpg import api

# Check what you have
print(rs.__version__)   # "0.15.0"
print(rs.__phase__)     # "Phase 15 — The Forge Master"
print(rs.__author__)    # "Rahul Sarkar (BluePhoenix)"
```

### Graceful degradation

The `api.py` module wraps every import in `FRAPPE_RS_AVAILABLE`:

```python
# In frappe_rpg/api.py (already written for you)
try:
    import frappe_rs as rs
    FRAPPE_RS_AVAILABLE = True
except ImportError:
    FRAPPE_RS_AVAILABLE = False

def get_cached_doc(doctype, name):
    if FRAPPE_RS_AVAILABLE:
        return rs.get_cached_doc(doctype, name)
    return frappe.get_doc(doctype, name)  # fallback
```

## 2. Foundation, Armoury, Battlefield

### Phase 1 — Math & Parallel Primitives

```python
import frappe_rs as rs

# Basic math (inlined for demonstration — use for PyO3 bridge verification)
rs.fast_sum(1_000_000, 2_000_000)          # -> 3000000
rs.fast_multiply(12345, 67890)             # -> 838102050
rs.fast_power(2, 40)                       # -> 1099511627776

# Rayon parallel operations over Python lists
numbers = list(range(10_000_000))
rs.parallel_sum(numbers)                   # -> int, computed across all cores
rs.parallel_sum_float([1.1, 2.2, 3.3])    # -> float
rs.parallel_max(numbers)                   # -> 9999999
rs.parallel_min(numbers)                   # -> 0
rs.count_above(numbers, 9_000_000)         # -> 1000000

# String utilities
rs.reverse_string("frappe")               # -> "epparF"... (test the bridge)
```

### Phase 2 — Cryptography & System Metrics

```python
import frappe_rs as rs
import json

# Argon2 password hashing (same latency as Python argon2-cffi, memory-safe)
hashed = rs.hash_argon2("user_password_here")  # -> "$argon2id$v=19$..."
rs.verify_argon2(hashed, "user_password_here") # -> True

# AES-256-GCM symmetric encryption
key = "a" * 64   # 32 bytes as 64 hex chars
ct = json.loads(rs.encrypt_aes_gcm("sensitive data", key))
# -> {"ciphertext_hex": "...", "nonce_hex": "..."}

plaintext = rs.decrypt_aes_gcm(ct["ciphertext_hex"], ct["nonce_hex"], key)
# -> "sensitive data"

# Hashing
rs.hash_sha256("hello world")   # -> "b94d27b9934d3e08..."
rs.hmac_sha256("secret", "message body")  # -> 64-char hex

# Ed25519 key pair (Phase 4 extension)
# generate + sign + verify via tokens module

# System metrics (live process info)
cpu = json.loads(rs.get_cpu_usage())
# -> {"usage_percent": 14.3, "core_count": 8, ...}

mem = json.loads(rs.get_memory_info())
# -> {"total_mb": 32768, "used_mb": 18432, "available_mb": 14336, ...}
```

### Phase 3 — Polars DataFrames & Analytics

```python
import frappe_rs as rs
import json

# Load a CSV and run a computation (returns JSON)
result = json.loads(rs.compute_stock_ledger("/path/to/stock.csv"))
print(result["data"])  # aggregated rows

# Pivot a JSON payload
sales_json = open("/path/to/sales.json").read()
pivot = json.loads(rs.pivot_report(
    filepath="/path/to/sales.json",
    group_by="region",
    pivot_col="category",
    val_col="amount"
))
```

## 3. The Citadel — Security & Sandboxing

### Phase 4 — Vault, Sandbox, JWT, Auditor

```python
import frappe_rs as rs
import json

# HKDF Vault — field-level AES-256-GCM encryption at rest
master_key = "a" * 64
ct = json.loads(rs.vault_encrypt("auth_token_value", "auth_token", master_key))
# -> {"ciphertext_hex": ..., "nonce_hex": ..., "field_name": "auth_token"}

plain = rs.vault_decrypt(ct["ciphertext_hex"], ct["nonce_hex"], "auth_token", master_key)
# -> "auth_token_value"

# AST code sandbox — reject dangerous Python before execution
result = json.loads(rs.run_sandbox("import os; os.system('id')"))
result["safe"]  # -> False, reason: "import of 'os' not allowed"

result = json.loads(rs.run_sandbox("x = 1 + 2; print(x)"))
result["safe"]  # -> True

# Double-entry ledger auditor
entries_json = json.dumps([
    {"debit": 1000, "credit": 0,    "account": "Cash"},
    {"debit": 0,    "credit": 1000, "account": "Revenue"},
])
check = json.loads(rs.verify_trial_balance_json(entries_json))
check["valid"]    # -> True
check["balance"]  # -> 0.0

# JWT tokens (Ed25519 signed, Frappe-native)
private_key_hex, public_key_hex = rs.generate_ed25519_keypair().split("|")
token = rs.sign_jwt({"user": "admin", "roles": ["System Manager"]}, private_key_hex)
claims = json.loads(rs.verify_jwt(token, public_key_hex))
claims["valid"]   # -> True
```

```python
import frappe_rs as rs

# NGINX access log parser
access = json.loads(rs.parse_nginx_access_log("/path/to/nginx.log"))
# -> [{"method": "GET", "path": "/api/...", "status": 200, "bytes": 1234, "ip": "..."}]

# Process metrics (Gunicorn workers + RQ queues)
metrics = json.loads(rs.get_process_metrics())
```

Navigate to **Frappe Desk → Andromeda Dashboard** after installation. The page:

- Polls `frappe_rpg.api.get_system_snapshot()` every 5 seconds
- Connects to the Go sidecar WebSocket on `:7072` for live event feeds
- Displays CPU/RAM/disk gauges, error rate chart, security audit ring buffer

The Go sidecar must be running for the live feed:

```bash
cd go/sidecar && ./scout_bin
# Listens on :7072 WebSocket + :7073 HTTP metrics + :7074 audit
```

## 5. The Nexus — Distributed Scale

### Phase 7 — Rate Limiter, Tracing, Coordination, mTLS

```python
import frappe_rs as rs
import json

# Token-bucket rate limiter — 5 microseconds per check via DashMap
allowed = rs.check_rate_limit("user:admin", capacity=100.0, refill_rate=10.0)
# -> True (tokens available) or False (throttled)

retry_after = rs.rate_limit_retry_after("user:admin", 100.0, 10.0)
# -> seconds until next token is available

rs.reset_rate_limit("user:admin")           # clear bucket
remaining = rs.rate_limit_tokens("user:admin", 100.0, 10.0)  # current tokens
rs.evict_idle_buckets(idle_secs=3600)       # cleanup stale buckets

# W3C TraceContext distributed tracing
trace_id = rs.new_trace_id()               # -> 32-char hex
span_id = rs.new_span_id()                # -> 16-char hex
traceparent = rs.build_traceparent(trace_id, span_id)
# -> "00---01"

parsed = json.loads(rs.parse_traceparent(traceparent))
# -> {"version": "00", "trace_id": ..., "parent_id": ..., "flags": "01"}

span = json.loads(rs.start_span("database_query", trace_id, span_id))
rs.set_span_attribute(span["span_id"], "db.table", "tabSales Order")
rs.finish_span(span["span_id"])

# Multi-worker coordination via mmap shared memory
rs.increment_shared_counter(0)   # slot 0, atomic across Gunicorn workers
rs.get_shared_counter_val(0)     # -> current value across all workers
rs.reset_shared_counter(0)
```

## 7. The Accelerator — Cache & Proxy

### Phase 9 — moka LRU Cache, Parallel Fetch, WASM

```python
import frappe_rs as rs
import json

# moka LRU document cache (800x speedup on warm reads)
# Write to cache
rs.cache_doc("Sales Order", "SO-001", json.dumps({"name": "SO-001", "status": "Open"}))
# -> True

# Read from cache
doc = rs.get_cached_doc("Sales Order", "SO-001")
# -> JSON string of the doc, or None if expired/missing

# Invalidate all docs of a DocType
rs.invalidate_doctype("Sales Order")

# Flush entire cache (forces synchronous eviction)
rs.flush_doc_cache()

# Cache statistics
stats = json.loads(rs.cache_stats())
# -> {"entries": 142, "hits": 8934, "misses": 107, ...}

# Parallel multi-DocType fetch via Rayon + mysql
result = json.loads(rs.parallel_fetch_docs(json.dumps([
    {"doctype": "Customer",     "filters": {"disabled": 0}, "fields": ["name", "customer_name"]},
    {"doctype": "Sales Order",  "filters": {"status": "Open"}, "fields": ["name", "grand_total"]},
    {"doctype": "Item",         "filters": {"disabled": 0}, "fields": ["name", "item_name"]},
])))
# All three queries run concurrently in Rust threads
# -> {"Customer": [...], "Sales Order": [...], "Item": [...]}

# WASM validation functions (compiled from Rust)
rs.wasm_validate_email("user@example.com")   # -> True
rs.wasm_validate_phone("+91-9876543210")     # -> True
rs.wasm_subtotals(json.dumps([100, 200, 300])) # -> 600.0
```

## 8. The Oracle — Statistical Intelligence

### Phase 10 — Anomaly Detection, Forecasting, Log Clustering

```python
import frappe_rs as rs
import json

# ── Anomaly Detection ──────────────────────────────────────────

# EWMA anomaly score (exponentially weighted moving average)
series = [10.0, 10.2, 10.1, 10.3, 45.7, 10.0, 10.1]  # spike at index 4
score = rs.ewma_score(series, alpha=0.3)
# -> float: how anomalous the latest value is

# Z-score anomaly
rs.zscore_anomaly(value=45.7, mean=10.15, std=0.12, threshold_sigma=3.0)
# -> True (anomaly detected)

# IQR outliers
outliers = json.loads(rs.iqr_outliers([1, 2, 3, 4, 100, 5, 6]))
# -> {"lower_fence": ..., "upper_fence": ..., "outliers": [100]}

# ── Change-Point Detection ─────────────────────────────────────

# CUSUM — returns index of first change, or -1
idx = rs.cusum_detect(series, target=10.0, slack=0.5, threshold=5.0)

# PELT — returns list of change-point indices
cps = json.loads(rs.pelt_changepoints(series, penalty=5.0))
# -> [4]  (change detected at index 4)

# Correlate change-points with deployment events
deploys = json.dumps([{"ts": 1700000300, "name": "v1.2.3"}])
cps_json = json.dumps([{"ts": 1700000412, "index": 4}])
corr = json.loads(rs.deployment_correlate(cps_json, deploys))
# -> [{"changepoint_ts": ..., "deploy": "v1.2.3", "delta_secs": 112}]

# ── Drain Log Clustering ───────────────────────────────────────

log_lines = [
    "Error connecting to db-01.prod:3306 after 3 retries",
    "Error connecting to db-02.prod:3306 after 5 retries",
    "Redis timeout at 127.0.0.1:6379 after 2000ms",
    "Redis timeout at 127.0.0.1:6379 after 3000ms",
]
templates = json.loads(rs.drain_parse(log_lines, depth=3, similarity_threshold=0.5))
# -> [{"template_id": 0, "pattern": "Error connecting to : after  retries", "count": 2}, ...]

top = json.loads(rs.top_templates(n=5))
# -> top 5 most-frequent log templates seen so far

# ── SimHash Error Fingerprinting ───────────────────────────────

h1 = rs.simhash("NullPointerException at com.frappe.Purchase.line:42")
h2 = rs.simhash("NullPointerException at com.frappe.Purchase.line:43")
rs.hamming_distance(h1, h2)  # -> small number (similar errors)

# Deduplicate a list of error messages
errors = ["DB connection failed: timeout", "DB connection failed: timeout", "KeyError: 'name'"]
unique = json.loads(rs.dedup_errors(errors, threshold=3))
# -> [{"representative": "DB connection failed: timeout", "count": 2}, {"representative": "KeyError: 'name'", "count": 1}]

# ── Error Causality Graph ──────────────────────────────────────

events = json.dumps([
    {"template": "Redis timeout", "ts": 1000},
    {"template": "DB connection failed", "ts": 1005},
    {"template": "Redis timeout", "ts": 2000},
    {"template": "DB connection failed", "ts": 2004},
])
correlations = json.loads(rs.temporal_correlation(events, window_secs=30.0))
graph = json.loads(rs.build_causality_graph(correlations, min_confidence=0.7))
dot = rs.export_dot(json.dumps(graph))  # -> GraphViz DOT string

causes = json.loads(rs.query_likely_causes(correlations, "DB connection failed"))
# -> [{"cause": "Redis timeout", "confidence": 0.95}]

# ── Holt-Winters Forecasting ───────────────────────────────────

monthly_sales = [100, 110, 95, 120, 115, 130, 105, 125, 110, 140, 130, 150]
model = json.loads(rs.holtwinters_fit(monthly_sales, alpha=0.3, beta=0.1, gamma=0.2, period=12))
forecast = json.loads(rs.holtwinters_forecast(json.dumps(model), horizon=6))
# -> [{"index": 12, "value": 158.3}, ..., {"index": 17, "value": 173.1}]

# Auto-tune α/β/γ to minimize MAE
best_params = json.loads(rs.auto_tune_params(monthly_sales, period=12))

# Scaling signal for auto-scaling infrastructure
signal = json.loads(rs.scale_signal(json.dumps(forecast), threshold_high=160.0, threshold_low=80.0))
# -> {"action": "scale_up", "at_index": 14, ...}

# ── Capacity Planning ──────────────────────────────────────────

disk_usage_gb = [50, 52, 55, 59, 64, 70, 77]
runway = json.loads(rs.resource_runway(current=77.0, capacity=200.0, growth_rate=0.09))
# -> {"days_to_full": 17, "full_date_approx": "2026-04-15"}

projection = json.loads(rs.linear_extrapolate(disk_usage_gb, horizon_days=30))
ci = json.loads(rs.confidence_interval(disk_usage_gb, horizon=30))

# ── Dead Letter Job Classifier ─────────────────────────────────

failure_text = "Lost connection to MySQL server during query"
result = json.loads(rs.classify_failure(failure_text))
# -> {"category": "DB_CONNECTION", "retry_safe": True, "suggested_action": "retry with backoff"}

rules = json.loads(rs.list_classifier_rules())
# -> all 10 built-in rule definitions
```

## 9. The Relay — Multi-Protocol

### Phase 11 — GraphQL, gRPC, MQTT, Kafka, Protobuf

```python
import frappe_rs as rs
import json

# Generate a proto3 schema from a Frappe DocType JSON
so_doctype = json.loads(open("doctype/sales_order.json").read())
proto = rs.doctype_to_proto(json.dumps(so_doctype))
# Returns a .proto file as a string with Get/Save/List/StreamEvents RPCs

# Batch generate for multiple DocTypes
protos = json.loads(rs.batch_doctype_to_proto([
    json.dumps(so_doctype),
    json.dumps(po_doctype),
]))
# -> {"Sales Order": "syntax = \"proto3\"...", "Purchase Order": "..."}
```

**Go relay binary** handles GraphQL, gRPC, MQTT, and Kafka:

```bash
# Start the relay

# GraphQL query (auto-generated schema from Frappe DocTypes)

  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{"query":"{ salesorder { name status grand_total } }"}'

# Health check
curl http://localhost:7074/relay/health

# Prometheus metrics
curl http://localhost:7074/relay/metrics
```

## 10. The Keymaster — Zero-Trust Identity

### Phase 12 — WebAuthn, JWT STS, Request Signing, Vault, HSM

```python
import frappe_rs as rs
import json

# ── WebAuthn / FIDO2 ───────────────────────────────────────────

# Registration flow
challenge = json.loads(rs.generate_registration_challenge("user-001", "frappe.local"))
# Store challenge["challenge"] and send to browser

# After browser sends credential
result = json.loads(rs.verify_registration("user-001", json.dumps({
    "id": "credential-id-from-browser",
    "response": {
        "clientDataJSON": "...",
        "attestationObject": "...",
    }
})))
result["verified"]  # -> True
result["credential_id"]  # store this per-user

# Authentication flow
challenge = json.loads(rs.generate_authentication_challenge("user-001", result["credential_id"]))
auth_result = json.loads(rs.verify_authentication("user-001", json.dumps({
    "id": "credential-id-from-browser",
    "response": {"authenticatorData": "...", "signature": "..."}
})))
auth_result["verified"]  # -> True

# List pending challenges (admin view)
pending = json.loads(rs.list_pending_challenges())

# ── HMAC Request Signing ───────────────────────────────────────

# Sign an outgoing API call
sig_header = rs.sign_request("POST", "https://api.partner.com/orders", body, "shared_secret")
# -> "ts=1711700000&sig=abc123..."

# Verify an incoming signed request (max_age_secs = replay window)
result = json.loads(rs.verify_request_signature(
    sig_header, "POST", "https://api.partner.com/orders", body, "shared_secret", 300
))
result["valid"]    # -> True
result["reason"]   # -> "ok" or "request too old (412s > 300s)"

# Standalone HMAC-SHA256
hex_sig = rs.hmac_sha256("my_secret_key", "message body to sign")

# ── SoftHSM / PKCS#11 ─────────────────────────────────────────

# Initialize a soft key (dev mode — no hardware required)
rs.hsm_init_soft_key("frappe-master-key", "a" * 64)  # 32-byte hex key

# Sign data (tries PKCS#11 lib, falls back to HMAC-SHA256)
sig = rs.hsm_sign("data to sign", "frappe-master-key")  # -> 64-char hex

# Decrypt data (tries PKCS#11 lib, falls back to AES-GCM)
# ciphertext_hex must start with 12-byte nonce prefix
plaintext = rs.hsm_decrypt(ciphertext_hex, "frappe-master-key")

# Check backend (hardware vs soft)
backend = rs.hsm_backend()  # -> "soft" or "hardware"
```

**Keymaster Go binary** (STS JWT + Vault secrets):

```bash
# Start the keymaster
KEYMASTER_SECRET="your-production-secret-min-32-chars" \
REDIS_URL="redis://redis-prod:6379/0" \
VAULT_ADDR="https://vault.internal:8200" \
VAULT_TOKEN="s.xxx" \
./keymaster_bin

# Issue a 15-minute JWT
curl -X POST http://localhost:7075/keymaster/token \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d '{"user_id":"admin","email":"admin@example.com","roles":["System Manager"],"frappe_session":"abc123"}'
# -> {"token": "eyJ...", "expires_in": 900, "jti": "abc123"}

# Verify + auto-refresh
curl http://localhost:7075/keymaster/verify \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer eyJ..."
# -> {"valid": true, "sub": "admin", "token": "eyJ..." (if refreshed)}

# Revoke a token
curl -X POST http://localhost:7075/keymaster/revoke \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer eyJ..." \
  -d '{"jti": "abc123"}'

# Check secrets status
curl http://localhost:7075/keymaster/secrets/status

# Inject a test secret
curl -X POST http://localhost:7075/keymaster/secrets/inject \
  -d '{"key": "DB_PASSWORD", "value": "super_secret"}'

# Health + version
curl http://localhost:7075/keymaster/health
```

## 11. The Architect — Data Pipeline

### Phase 13 — DuckDB OLAP, DynamoDB KV, Parquet, Schema Evolution

```python
import frappe_rs as rs
import json

# ── DuckDB OLAP (Polars SQL) ───────────────────────────────────

# SQL over any JSON dataset — table name is always "t"
data = json.dumps([
    {"region": "North", "product": "Widget", "revenue": 1000},
    {"region": "South", "product": "Gadget", "revenue": 800},
    {"region": "North", "product": "Gadget", "revenue": 600},
])

rows = json.loads(rs.duckdb_query(data, """
    SELECT region, SUM(revenue) as total
    FROM t
    GROUP BY region
    ORDER BY total DESC
"""))
# -> [{"region": "North", "total": 1600}, {"region": "South", "total": 800}]

# Convenient aggregation shortcut
rows = json.loads(rs.duckdb_aggregate(data, "region", "revenue", "sum"))

# Describe a dataset
info = json.loads(rs.duckdb_describe(data))
# -> {"rows": 3, "columns": ["region", "product", "revenue"]}

# SQL over a Parquet file
rows = json.loads(rs.duckdb_scan_parquet(
    "/data/sales_2025.parquet",
    "SELECT * FROM parquet WHERE revenue > 500 ORDER BY revenue DESC LIMIT 100"
))

# ── Parquet / Arrow Export ─────────────────────────────────────

# Export Frappe records to Parquet
gl_entries = json.dumps(frappe.db.get_list("GL Entry", fields=["*"], as_list=False))
result = json.loads(rs.frappe_to_parquet(gl_entries, "/backups/gl_2025_q1.parquet"))
# -> {"rows": 84732, "path": "/backups/gl_2025_q1.parquet", "columns": [...]}

# Read back
records = json.loads(rs.parquet_to_json("/backups/gl_2025_q1.parquet"))

# Inspect schema
schema = json.loads(rs.parquet_schema("/backups/gl_2025_q1.parquet"))
# -> {"columns": [{"name": "posting_date", "dtype": "Utf8"}, ...], "rows": 84732}

# Column stats
stats = json.loads(rs.arrow_batch_stats(gl_entries))
# -> {"debit": {"count": 84732, "null_count": 0, "min": "0.0000", "max": "9999999.0000"}, ...}

# ── DynamoDB KV Bridge ─────────────────────────────────────────

# Store any JSON item
rs.kv_put("frappe-sessions", "session-abc123", json.dumps({
    "user": "admin",
    "site": "genbi",
    "expires": 1711700900
}))

# Retrieve
result = json.loads(rs.kv_get("frappe-sessions", "session-abc123"))
if result["found"]:
    session = result["item"]

# Query with filter
result = json.loads(rs.kv_query("frappe-sessions", json.dumps({
    "field": "user",
    "value": "admin"
})))
sessions = result["items"]

# Batch write (atomic)
batch = json.dumps([
    {"pk": "SO-001", "item": {"status": "Submitted", "grand_total": 5000}},
    {"pk": "SO-002", "item": {"status": "Draft",     "grand_total": 3200}},
])
rs.kv_batch_write("sales-orders", batch)

# Stats
stats = json.loads(rs.kv_table_stats())
# -> {"frappe-sessions": 42, "sales-orders": 1204}

# ── Unified Storage Router ─────────────────────────────────────

# Route by namespace prefix — kv: / s3:
rs.storage_set("kv:invoices", "INV-001", json.dumps({"amount": 5000}))
result = json.loads(rs.storage_get("kv:invoices", "INV-001"))

rs.storage_set("s3:attachments", "report_2025_q1.pdf", pdf_bytes.hex())
rs.storage_delete("kv:invoices", "INV-001")

# OLAP query through router
rows = json.loads(rs.olap_query(
    "SELECT status, COUNT(*) as cnt FROM t GROUP BY status",
    so_list_json
))

# List all configured backends
backends = json.loads(rs.storage_backends())

# ── Schema Evolution Engine ────────────────────────────────────

old_schema = json.dumps([
    {"name": "id",    "type": "INT",     "nullable": False},
    {"name": "name",  "type": "VARCHAR", "nullable": True},
    {"name": "email", "type": "VARCHAR", "nullable": True},
])
new_schema = json.dumps([
    {"name": "id",    "type": "BIGINT",  "nullable": False},   # widened
    {"name": "name",  "type": "VARCHAR", "nullable": True},
    {"name": "email", "type": "VARCHAR", "nullable": True},
    {"name": "phone", "type": "VARCHAR", "nullable": True},    # added
    # "email" removed → breaking
])

# Diff
diff = json.loads(rs.diff_schema(old_schema, new_schema))
# -> {"added": ["phone"], "removed": ["email"], "changed": [{"column": "id", ...}]}

# Validate — is it safe?
validation = json.loads(rs.validate_migration(old_schema, new_schema))
if not validation["safe"]:
    for error in validation["errors"]:
        print("BREAKING:", error)

# Generate SQL
sql = json.loads(rs.generate_migration_sql(old_schema, new_schema, "tabCustomer", "mysql"))
for stmt in sql["statements"]:
    frappe.db.sql(stmt)

# Safe ordered migration plan
plan = json.loads(rs.safe_migration_plan(json.dumps(diff)))
for phase in plan:
    print(f"Phase {phase['phase']}: {phase['operation']} ({phase['count']} columns, safe={phase['safe']})")
```

**S3 Proxy (Go binary)**:

```bash
# Start with memory backend (dev)
S3PROXY_PORT=7076 S3PROXY_BACKEND=memory ./s3proxy_bin

# Start with disk backend (staging)
S3PROXY_PORT=7076 S3PROXY_BACKEND=disk S3PROXY_DATA_DIR=/data/s3 ./s3proxy_bin

# Store an object
curl -X PUT http://localhost:7076/s3/frappe-backups/site_backup_20250329.tar.gz \
  --data-binary @site_backup_20250329.tar.gz

# Retrieve
curl http://localhost:7076/s3/frappe-backups/site_backup_20250329.tar.gz -o downloaded.tar.gz

# List bucket
curl "http://localhost:7076/s3/frappe-backups?list"

# Delete
curl -X DELETE http://localhost:7076/s3/frappe-backups/site_backup_20250329.tar.gz
```

## 12. The Quartermaster — Multi-Tenancy

### Phase 14 — Quota, WFQ, Metering, Isolation, GDPR

```python
import frappe_rs as rs
import json

# ── Per-Tenant Quota Enforcement ───────────────────────────────

# Define quotas at tenant onboarding
for resource, limit, window in [
    ("api_calls",   10_000, 86400),   # 10k API calls per day
    ("storage_gb",  100,    0),        # 100 GB soft cap (no window)
    ("bg_jobs",     500,    3600),     # 500 background jobs per hour
    ("pdf_exports", 200,    86400),    # 200 PDF exports per day
]:
    rs.quota_set("tenant-acme", resource, limit, window)

# Check before allowing an API call (middleware pattern)
result = json.loads(rs.quota_check("tenant-acme", "api_calls", amount=1))
if not result["allowed"]:
    frappe.throw(f"Quota exceeded. Retry after {result['reset_at']} UNIX time.")

# Consume (check + decrement atomically)
result = json.loads(rs.quota_consume("tenant-acme", "api_calls", amount=1))
if not result["allowed"]:
    frappe.throw(f"Rate limited. {result['remaining']} calls remaining.")

# Dashboard: all quotas for tenant
status = json.loads(rs.quota_status("tenant-acme"))
for q in status["quotas"]:
    print(f"{q['resource']}: {q['consumed']}/{q['limit']} ({q['remaining']} remaining)")

# Admin: reset a quota mid-window
rs.quota_reset("tenant-acme", "api_calls")

# ── Weighted Fair Queue ────────────────────────────────────────

# Onboard tenants with weights (enterprise gets 3x throughput of starter)
rs.wfq_set_weight("tenant-enterprise", 3.0)
rs.wfq_set_weight("tenant-starter",    1.0)

# Enqueue background jobs (lower priority = processed first)
rs.wfq_enqueue("tenant-enterprise", json.dumps({
    "job": "generate_balance_sheet",
    "params": {"year": 2025}
}), priority=1)

rs.wfq_enqueue("tenant-starter", json.dumps({
    "job": "send_email_digest",
}), priority=5)

# RQ worker calls this in a loop
while True:
    job = json.loads(rs.wfq_dequeue_fair())
    if job.get("empty"):
        break
    tenant = job["tenant"]
    payload = job["job"]
    # execute payload["job"] in context of tenant...

# Stats
stats = json.loads(rs.wfq_stats())
for t in stats["tenants"]:
    print(f"{t['tenant']}: depth={t['depth']}, weight={t['weight']}")

# ── Usage Metering ─────────────────────────────────────────────

# Record metrics on every request (call from Frappe hook)
rs.meter_record("tenant-acme", "api_calls",    1.0)
rs.meter_record("tenant-acme", "bytes_served", len(response_body))
rs.meter_record("tenant-acme", "jobs_run",     1.0)
rs.meter_record("tenant-acme", "pdf_exports",  1.0)

# Read current values
calls = rs.meter_get("tenant-acme", "api_calls")  # -> float

# Snapshot for invoice generation
snap = json.loads(rs.meter_snapshot("tenant-acme"))
# -> {"tenant": "tenant-acme", "metrics": {"api_calls": 8432, "bytes_served": 2134567890, ...}}

# Flush all tenants to DynamoDB / KV store (call every 60s from scheduler)
records = json.loads(rs.meter_flush_all())
for record in records:
    rs.kv_put("usage-billing", f"{record['tenant']}:{record['ts']}", json.dumps(record))

# Check last flush time
last = rs.meter_last_flush("tenant-acme")  # UNIX timestamp

# Reset after billing cycle
rs.meter_reset("tenant-acme")

# ── Tenant Data Isolation ──────────────────────────────────────

# Register doc ownership at creation time (Frappe after_insert hook)
def after_insert(doc, method):
    tenant_id = frappe.db.get_value("Company", doc.company, "tenant_id")
    rs.isolation_set_tenant(doc.doctype, doc.name, tenant_id)

# Verify access before serving data (Frappe has_permission hook)
def has_permission(doc, ptype, user):
    requesting_tenant = frappe.db.get_value("User", user, "tenant_id")
    result = json.loads(rs.isolation_check(doc.doctype, doc.name, requesting_tenant))
    return result["allowed"]

# Validate query filters before running
def validate_filters(doctype, filters, tenant_id):
    result = json.loads(rs.isolation_validate_query(json.dumps(filters), tenant_id))
    if not result["tenant_scoped"]:
        for warning in result["warnings"]:
            frappe.log_error(warning, "Isolation Warning")

# Audit: all docs owned by tenant
audit = json.loads(rs.isolation_audit("tenant-acme"))
print(f"Tenant acme owns {audit['count']} documents")

# ── GDPR Export Pipeline ───────────────────────────────────────

encryption_key = frappe.conf.get("gdpr_export_key")  # 32-byte hex key
signing_key    = frappe.conf.get("gdpr_signing_key")  # 32-byte Ed25519 seed

# Collect data for the export package
rs.gdpr_collect("tenant-acme", json.dumps(frappe.db.get_list("Customer", filters={"tenant_id": "tenant-acme"})))
rs.gdpr_collect("tenant-acme", json.dumps(frappe.db.get_list("Contact",  filters={"company": "Acme Corp"})))
rs.gdpr_collect("tenant-acme", json.dumps(frappe.db.get_list("GL Entry", filters={"company": "Acme Corp"})))

# Encrypt the export (AES-256-GCM)
export = json.loads(rs.gdpr_export("tenant-acme", encryption_key))
# -> {"ciphertext_b64": "...", "nonce_b64": "...", "records": 8432}

# Sign the export payload for audit trail
signed = json.loads(rs.gdpr_sign_export(json.dumps(export), signing_key))
# -> {"signature_hex": "...", "public_key_hex": "...", "payload_hash": "..."}

# Verify integrity before delivering to tenant
verified = json.loads(rs.gdpr_verify_export(
    json.dumps(export),
    signed["signature_hex"],
    signed["public_key_hex"]
))
assert verified["valid"], "Export integrity check failed"

# GDPR "right to erasure" — purge all collected records
result = json.loads(rs.gdpr_delete_tenant("tenant-acme"))
print(f"Erased {result['records_erased']} records")
```

## 13. The Forge Master — Quality

### Phase 15 — Propcheck, Chaos, Contract, Audit

```python
import frappe_rs as rs
import json

# ── Property-Based Testing ─────────────────────────────────────

# Run the full propcheck suite before deploying
suite = json.loads(rs.propcheck_suite(n_trials=10_000))
if not suite["all_passed"]:
    raise Exception(f"Property violations: {suite['failed']} / {suite['total_properties']}")

# Individual properties
rs.propcheck_fast_sum(1000)           # commutativity + identity
rs.propcheck_hmac_determinism(500)    # same inputs → same output
rs.propcheck_simhash_identity(500)    # identical strings → distance 0
rs.propcheck_quota_never_over_limit(50)  # quota invariant

# ── Chaos Engineering ──────────────────────────────────────────

# Circuit breaker for external API calls
def call_external_api(endpoint: str, payload: dict):
    result = json.loads(rs.chaos_circuit_breaker(
        endpoint, success=True, threshold=5, half_open_secs=30
    ))
    if result["state"] == "open":
        raise Exception(f"Circuit open for {endpoint} — too many failures")

    try:
        response = requests.post(endpoint, json=payload, timeout=5)
        return response.json()
    except Exception as e:
        # Record failure
        rs.chaos_circuit_breaker(endpoint, success=False, threshold=5, half_open_secs=30)
        raise

# Inject latency in staging tests
rs.chaos_simulate_latency(delay_ms=100)

# Simulate error rate for resilience testing
result = json.loads(rs.chaos_error_rate(rate_pct=10.0, n_requests=1000))
print(f"Error rate: {result['actual_rate_pct']:.1f}% ({result['errors']}/{result['total']})")

# Memory probe before a heavy batch job
probe = json.loads(rs.chaos_resource_probe(alloc_mb=256))
if probe["approx_rss_mb"] > 28_000:  # >28GB RSS
    frappe.throw("Insufficient memory for batch job — defer to off-peak")

# ── PyO3 Contract Tests (schema drift detection) ───────────────

# Run in CI after every Rust rebuild
manifest = json.loads(rs.contract_function_manifest())
module_fns = set(dir(rs))
missing = [f for f in manifest if f not in module_fns]
if missing:
    raise Exception(f"Schema drift detected! Missing functions: {missing}")

print(f"Contract OK — all {len(manifest)} functions present")
print(f"Module version: {rs.__version__}")

# ── Production Readiness Audit ─────────────────────────────────

# Run before every production deployment
config = {
    "signing_secret": frappe.conf.get("keymaster_secret", ""),
    "hsm_backend": rs.hsm_backend(),
    "token_ttl_secs": frappe.conf.get("token_ttl_secs", 900),
    "redis_url": frappe.conf.get("redis_cache", "redis://127.0.0.1:13005/0"),
    "s3_backend": frappe.conf.get("s3_backend", "memory"),
    "doc_cache_ttl_secs": frappe.conf.get("doc_cache_ttl_secs", 300),
    "gdpr_tested": frappe.conf.get("gdpr_tested", False),
    "isolation_enforced": frappe.conf.get("isolation_enforced", False),
}

audit = json.loads(rs.audit_production(json.dumps(config)))

if not audit["production_ready"]:
    for item in audit["items"]:
        if not item["passed"]:
            level = "CRITICAL" if item["severity"] == "CRITICAL" else "WARNING"
            print(f"[{level}] {item['id']}: {item['check']} — {item['detail']}")
    if audit["critical_failures"] > 0:
        raise Exception(f"Production deployment blocked: {audit['critical_failures']} critical failures")
```

## 14. CLI Reference

```
frappe-rpg [OPTIONS] COMMAND

Commands:
  version                    Show SDK + Rust extension version info
  snapshot [--format json]   Live system health (CPU, RAM, disk, workers)
  logs parse                 Parse Frappe/NGINX logs with Rust parser
    --path PATH              Log file path
    --last N                 Last N lines only
    --format json|table      Output format
  bench FUNCTION             Micro-benchmark a frappe_rs function
    --iterations N           Number of iterations (default: 10000)
    --size N                 Input size for collection functions
  audit verify               Verify trial balance from GL JSON
    --file PATH              Path to GL entries JSON file
  scout status               Go sidecar health check
  scout metrics              Fetch Prometheus metrics from sidecar
  generate module NAME       Scaffold a new Rust module
    --functions fn1,fn2,...  Comma-separated function names to stub
```

## 15. Go Sidecar HTTP Reference

### relay_bin (:7071 / :7074)

| Method | Path | Description |
|--------|------|-------------|
| POST | `/graphql` | Execute GraphQL query |
| GET | `/relay/health` | Health check |
| GET | `/relay/metrics` | Prometheus metrics |

### keymaster_bin (:7075)

| Method | Path | Description |
|--------|------|-------------|
| POST | `/keymaster/token` | Issue 15-min JWT. Body: `{user_id, email, roles, frappe_session}` |
| GET | `/keymaster/verify` | Verify + auto-refresh JWT. Header: `Authorization: Bearer ` |
| POST | `/keymaster/revoke` | Revoke a token. Body: `{jti}` |
| GET | `/keymaster/secrets/status` | Secrets manager status |
| POST | `/keymaster/secrets/inject` | Inject test secret. Body: `{key, value}` |
| GET | `/keymaster/health` | Health check |
| GET | `/keymaster/metrics` | Prometheus metrics |

### s3proxy_bin (:7076)

| Method | Path | Description |
|--------|------|-------------|
| PUT/POST | `/s3/{bucket}/{key}` | Store object |
| GET | `/s3/{bucket}/{key}` | Retrieve object |
| DELETE | `/s3/{bucket}/{key}` | Delete object |
| GET | `/s3/{bucket}?list` | List all keys in bucket |
| GET | `/s3/health` | Health check |
| GET | `/s3/metrics` | Prometheus metrics |

### loadtest_bin (:7077)

| Method | Path | Description |
|--------|------|-------------|
| POST | `/loadtest/run` | Run a load test. Body: `{target_url, concurrency, duration_secs, method}` |
| GET | `/loadtest/results` | Results of last run (p50/p90/p99, RPS, error rate) |
| GET | `/loadtest/health` | Health check |
| GET | `/loadtest/metrics` | Prometheus metrics |

than the work as a whole, that (a) is included in the normal form of
packaging a Major Component, but which is not part of that Major
Component, and (b) serves only to enable use of the work with that
Major Component, or to implement a Standard Interface for which an
implementation is available to the public in source code form.  A
"Major Component", in this context, means a major essential component
(kernel, window system, and so on) of the specific operating system
(if any) on which the executable work runs, or a compiler used to
produce the work, or an object code interpreter used to run it.

  The "Corresponding Source" for a work in object code form means all
the source code needed to generate, install, and (for an executable
work) run the object code and to modify the work, including scripts to
control those activities.  However, it does not include the work's
System Libraries, or general-purpose tools or generally available free
programs which are used unmodified in performing those activities but
which are not part of the work.  For example, Corresponding Source
includes interface definition files associated with source files for
the work, and the source code for shared libraries and dynamically
linked subprograms that the work is specifically designed to require,
such as by intimate data communication or control flow between those
subprograms and other parts of the work.

can regenerate automatically from other parts of the Corresponding
Source.

  The Corresponding Source for a work in source code form is that
same work.

development of collaborative font projects, to support the font creation
efforts of academic and linguistic communities, and to provide a free and
open framework in which fonts may be shared and improved in partnership
with others.
